# Changelog

All notable changes to fathom. Format loosely follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/); dates are ISO-8601.

**How to read this file.** Each release opens with what it was for, then
one block per day: a bold topic naming the program and its plan rows
(`SH-1..6`, `O16`, `ADR-025` are durable reference points -- `fathom plans
archive` checks that every archived row id is absorbed here, in `ADR.md`, or
in the per-release narrative), followed by what changed. The full narrative
record -- root causes, measurements, rationale as originally written -- lives
per release under `docs/changelog/` and is never normative; plans, ADRs, and
observations are.
From 2026-08-25 the `[Unreleased]` block follows the change-records rules
(`agentic-kit/docs/project-intent/changes.md`): a `Release impact:` line first,
one line per observable change at most two sentences, `### Breaking` first
when present, the delta not the journey; released blocks stay as written.
Compatibility surface: `docs/intent/scope.md`.

## [Unreleased]

Release impact: minor (pre-1.0: breaking bumps minor)

### Breaking
- **`fathom init`, `fleet reconcile --apply` and the console apply write the fathom stanza into `AGENTS.md` only, for every `--harness` (ADR-059).** A root `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `CLAUDE.local.md` that exists (and is not `AGENTS.md` itself through a link) gets one marked region holding `@AGENTS.md`, an existing CLAUDE.md stanza region is rewritten to it on the next rewire (receipted; `fleet undo` restores it), `init --harness=gemini-cli` sets `context.fileName` instead of writing `GEMINI.md`, and fathom never creates a CLAUDE file. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-agents-md-one-instruction-file.md`.
- **`fathom gen-claude-md` is renamed `gen-agents-md`, writing `workspace/artifacts/AGENTS.md` by default (ADR-059).** The old name still runs through 0.9.x with its old default output and a deprecation line on stderr.
- **Done means proven AND wired: `fathom verify --strict` (the pre-push gate) refuses a claimed row whose Go function a test reaches but no product entry point does (ADR-018 amendment 2026-09-28).** The new `wired` criterion walks the verify call graph from every `package main`'s `main` outside `testdata/` and the `init` and variable initializers of the packages they link; such a row is `library-only` (Unknown: the default gate reports it, `--strict` fails it) unless it declares `library: true` with a `reason:` that predates its claim.
- **`search` refuses a bare `root` outside every indexed service's root (`-32602`), reads at most 1 MiB of ripgrep output (stopping rg), 512 columns a line, `limit`+1 matches a file and 5 context lines, and names a stopped search in `partial` (RT-88, §4.5).** The Go fallback stops at 200 hits (was 20,000) under 64 MiB / 5 s. Pass `service`, or a `root` under an indexed one.
- **`references` searches the caller's own service unless `services` names others; `services: ["*"]` is the fleet (admitted in the `fleet` class, as is a list naming more than one service), and a call with neither a `services` argument nor a resolvable caller is a `-32602` listing the indexed services (RT-86, RT-159, §4.5).** Hooks, the CLI and `serve-mcp` resolve the caller from its working directory (`X-Fathom-Caller` gains `cwd=`); an HTTP agent passes `services`. The text scan streams under 64 MiB / 5 s per call (interface files a bare query reads included) and names a stopped scan in `partial`; definitions come from the store's name lookup, a service the budget never reaches costs only that lookup, and `exact` defaults on when a scanned service has typed edges. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-references-own-service.md`.
- **A read of a drifted service answers from the index as it stands and queues the re-ingest; every daemon ingest runs on one refresh queue (RT-97..RT-100, RT-111; ADR-047 §7.1).** Auto-refresh no longer ingests before answering: the envelope says `refresh: {"<svc>": "queued (index_stale) — served from the index at <indexed>; HEAD is <head> …"}` (`refreshing` once it runs) and `staleness_report` adds `refresh`. The queue runs one ingest per service (a request mid-run becomes one follow-up) and one at a time, the most recently queried first, 60 s after a start and one per 30 s during warm-up, pauses background work at shed and refuses at refuse and in store distress (and background work in safe mode or for a quarantined service); the `ingest` tool waits on it and answers `coalesced: true` when it shared a run, and `post-commit-hook` now queues through `POST /refresh-request` (202) and returns instead of running the ingest in its request (a daemon without the route is still asked through the `ingest` tool), and no longer onboards a service the daemon does not index — it prints the `ingest` tool call with a path that does; `post-commit-hook --wait` runs the ingest tool and waits up to `--timeout` (the console's refresh remedy uses it). A read inside a failed refresh's retry backoff says so in its `refresh` note. A queued ingest nobody waits on still notes store distress, busts the identity cache, logs its failure and leaves it as `staleness_report`'s `refresh_failure` until a success. `serve-mcp` runs the `ingest` tool on its auto-refresh queue too. A non-git service's read probes its stats (at most one walk per 5 s) and never recomputes the content manifest; moved stats queue a refresh.
- **The daemon records every start and exit and trips a crash-loop breaker (RT-37..RT-40, RT-156, RT-158, ADR-047 §2.3).** `~/.fathom/daemon/<id>/lifecycle.jsonl` names an unclean predecessor at the next start (with jetsam or resource-report evidence); 3 counted exits (over budget, unclean, a foreign-held port, a failed start) in 30 min start the daemon in safe mode — tree and fleet refused with a named 503 `-32050`, auto-refresh, feeders and the decision port off — and 5 in 60 min stop it (`disabled.json {reason: breaker}`, exit 0; `fathom daemon start` resets it). For 120 s after any start each heavy class holds one slot (a second call is refused under the default `admission = "observe"`), a `batch` is gated by its sub-calls' classes, and auto-refresh ingests nothing inline; `task deploy` writes a `deploy` intent row (`fathom daemon deploy-intent`) so its kickstart is not counted, and `serve-http --lifecycle-id` gives an unmanaged daemon a persistent ledger.
- **serve-http binds before it opens its store and waits for an unavailable store instead of exiting; a held port and SIGTERM exit by rule (RT-41..RT-44, RT-165, §8.4, §8.5).** While waiting `/healthz` is 503 `waiting-for-store: <errno> (<label>)` — a `tcc-wedge` names the daemon binary, its signing identity and the Full Disk Access grant it needs — and tools/call is 503 `store unavailable: …`; a port a managed fathom of the same label serves exits 0, any other holder exits 75, named; the managed daemon's SIGTERM drains within 12 s and exits 0 (runtime fatals to `crash.log`). SQLITE_FULL/IOERR from a read or a write, or a low store volume, refuses ingest and `/refresh-file`, an unmanaged daemon waiting for its store keeps its `--ttl`/`--idle-exit`/`--exit-with-parent`, and `list_services` names an unreadable count as `symbol_count_error` instead of `symbol_count: 0`.
- **serve-http bounds its own memory and exits 70 over budget (RT-29..RT-36, RT-153..RT-155, RT-161, RT-171..RT-173, ADR-047 §2).** A budget (auto: RAM/8, 1–4 GiB; `[tenancy]` in `~/.fathom/config.toml` only — a repository's `[tenancy]` is ignored and named) drives a ladder sampled every 2 s over the daemon's footprint plus its child processes: shed at 0.75× drops console and fleet memos and pauses background work, refuse at 1× drops every cache, cancels in-flight tree and fleet calls and stops whole-tree, fleet and ingest reads, and the exit mark (2×, capped at RAM/4) halts an in-flight ingest, quarantines its service, and exits 70 with an `over_budget` lifecycle row if the next sample is still over. An unmanaged serve-http takes `[tenancy.unmanaged].budget` (512 MiB, exit at 1 GiB): a bench or script whose scratch daemon legitimately needs more sets `FATHOM_TENANCY_BUDGET=<size>` (or `FATHOM_TENANCY_EXIT=off`).
- **Work the ladder stops fails by name, and only a completed explicit `ingest` tool call lifts a quarantine (RT-153, RT-155, RT-172, RT-30, RT-31, RT-32).** An ingest that reaches refuse fails (`over budget: … halted at refuse`) with its prior watermark kept instead of indexing less, obligations reports its halted corpus read as `coverage_skipped`, a call refuse cancels answers a JSON-RPC error with `data.outcome: "abandoned-by-governor"` (call log `outcome`, `/metrics` `fathom_governor_abandoned_total`), a hook's refresh of a quarantined service is refused, and a malformed `gc_hint` / `FATHOM_MEMORY_LIMIT` or `refuse_at` below `shed_at` stops serve-http at start. `/statusz` gains a `tenancy` block (level, marks, unknown readings), and refuse diagnostics are captured at most once per 10 min, keeping 10 captures / 200 MiB.
- **Discovery skips the default cache directories — `.cache`, `.tools`, `target`, `dist`, `build`, `__pycache__`, `.venv`, `.gradle`, `.next`, `.turbo`, `coverage` — and names each one it pruned — in the ingest summary, `--json` and the MCP `ingest` tool's `default_ignored_dirs` (RT-145).** A repository that commits source under one of those names re-includes it with `[connector] include` in its own `.fathom/config.toml` (refused in `~/.fathom/config.toml`, where it would change nothing); a file over 2 MiB is counted under `oversize` on every ingest, not only the first (migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-default-cache-dirs.md`).
- **An unmanaged `serve-http` is a bounded scratch daemon (RT-20..RT-22, ADR-047 §10.1).** Unless started with `--managed` or by launchd under the default label or an installed fathom agent's `Label` (the same rule the kill switch and self-restart use), it exits within 10 s of its parent's death, after 20 min idle (`--idle-exit`) or 8 h (`--ttl`), or when its executable or store is unlinked, logging the reason; it defaults to `--auto-refresh=false`, runs no self-restart watcher or scavenger, and refuses the managed daemon's store and call log — named by its record or its installed plist, under `HOME` and the account's own home — without `--allow-operator-state`, refusing too when the managed daemon's record cannot be read. On SIGTERM, SIGINT or SIGHUP it removes its record and exits 128+signo. A systemd unit or other supervisor running the long-lived daemon must add `--managed`.
- **`concerns.cue` (both scopes) refuses what it used to ignore: an unknown top-level field, a `where:` on a kind that reads no corpus, a check field the kind does not read (`file:` on a `required_pattern`, `pattern:` on a `commands_resolve`, `command:` on a `max_lines`), and an optional (`name?:`) concern or top-level field.** Each loaded and did nothing (`pack:` for `packs:` would have enabled nothing; `file:` on a `required_pattern` scanned the whole corpus instead); the load error names the field and the kinds that read it, and `fathom config audit` and doctor's `concerns` row report it. Remove the named field.
- **`fathom fleet report --json` omits a service's `calls_total` and `calls_agent` when the call log could not be read, and `session_count` counts distinct sessions from the ledger `FATHOM_SESSION_LEDGER` names.** They were `0` beside a note saying "never zero", and ledger rows read from a hardcoded `~/.fathom` path (migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-fleet-report-honest-columns.md`).
- **`serve-http` watches its own binary for self-restart only when it is the managed daemon (`--managed`, or launched by launchd under the installed fathom agent's label), and adopts a new binary by exiting 75 instead of 0 (RT-11, RT-12).** A daemon under your own supervisor no longer self-restarts unless started with `--managed`; exit 75 is what a `KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit: false}` plist or `Restart=on-failure` relaunches on.
- **`review_diff` gains an additive `receipt` section: the change risk receipt over data the call already resolves (CX-5, `docs/plans/change-and-compliance.md` §2).** Blast radius, cross-service link hints, certificates, conservation and active obligations/arch findings feed the band and its cited factors; rows-proven, zone-interest, uncertified-contracts and suggested-reviewers stay at their honest not-yet-wired zero value rather than a fabricated claim.
- **`fathom review --receipt --format sarif|check` renders the change risk receipt through the shared SARIF/check-run emitter instead of the full review (CX-6, GL-20/GL-21).** One result/annotation per fired factor, ruleId/title the factor's own name; the check-run's `conclusion` is always `"neutral"` — fathom never scores a pass/fail verdict from the band (CX-8, ADR-040), CI decides what a band does.
- **`review_diff` additionally emits its `receipt` section as SARIF and a GitHub check-run payload (`receipt_sarif`, `receipt_check`; SR-49, `docs/plans/surface-reckoning.md`).** Same emitter and factor-vocabulary CX-6's CLI path uses, through the new `review.ChangeRiskReceipt`, so an MCP caller gets the code-scanning/Checks-API shapes without a second round trip through `fathom review --receipt`.
- **Every git read that goes through `internal/gitro` (status and diff in the console and review, the connector's log and diff reads, the HEAD fallback) runs with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` beside `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (incident maps IM-F2).** A service checked out as a partial clone reads an object it does not hold as an error instead of fetching it from the promisor remote, and a read that would prompt for a credential fails instead of waiting.

- **serve-http admits every tool call in its cost class and serves through a hardened `http.Server` (responsible tenant wave B: RT-53..RT-65, RT-152; ADR-047 §3).** Every MCP tool declares a cost class (`point`/`index`/`tree`/`fleet`/`ingest`; `docs/mcp-tools.md`), and `[tenancy].admission` (default `observe`: would-refuse is logged and counted, nothing is refused; `enforce`; `off`) gates each call by per-class slots, per-origin waits and deadline feasibility — an enforced refusal is HTTP 503 with `Retry-After`, `X-Fathom-Busy-Scope` (`class` or `daemon`; hooks back off daemon-wide only on `daemon`) and JSON-RPC `-32050` (`fathom busy: <reason>`), and `batch` now takes at most 16 calls, each admitted in its own class. `/rpc` work now runs under the request's context bounded by the class deadline and `X-Fathom-Deadline`, so a caller already gone is never run (refused `caller_gone`, in either mode) and a call whose client left or whose deadline passed mid-call answers a named `abandoned` error instead of its result (an ingest still runs detached, and no longer hits the 150 s write deadline); every route requires a loopback `Host` and a same-origin `Origin` (a hosted `--modules-token-env` deployment excepted); the managed daemon answers a test binary 409; call-log rows move to `schema_version` 3 with `outcome` on every row (`ok`, `partial`, `refused`, `abandoned`, `abandoned-by-governor`, `error`) plus `class`, `queued_ms`, `bytes_read` and `caller` (migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-admission-and-call-log-v3.md`).

**The typed pass records package initialization as a caller (TypedEdgeEpoch 3).** A non-test `init()` and a non-test file's package-level variable initializers (a route table, `var x = memo.NewGroup[...](...)`, a func literal kept in a var for tests) are keyed `<pkg>.init`, so `find_callers`, `blast_radius` and verify reach what they register; the epoch bump re-resolves every Go package at the first ingest after upgrade.

**The daemon's derived caches share one budget, and whole-repository facts come from ingest (responsible tenant wave C: RT-66..RT-68, RT-72..RT-79, RT-82, RT-160, RT-163; ADR-047 §4–§6).**
- Every symbol snapshot, call graph and layer-coverage path set is an entry in one registry (`internal/memo`): built once per key however many ask, byte-accounted under `[tenancy.caches] fraction`, evicted when its service's watermark moves, trimmed to `shed_trim` at shed (and held there, one rebuild at a time), dropped at refuse (which also cancels the builds in flight) and after `idle_drop` without a call; `fathom serve-mcp` takes the same registry budget. One snapshot and one graph per (service, watermark) now serve the MCP tools, the console's project pages, row evidence and search palette — except `review_diff`'s conservation dimension, which still builds its own graphs from the store per review.
- An ingest writes `file_stats`, `service_summary` and `observation_calls` (new additive store tables; an indexed reverse call lookup) in its final transaction; a store written before them is derived by SQL at first use and once by the daemon after start, with no re-ingest (`file_stats` waits for the next ingest).
- `list_services` answers each service from its summary row — a row not yet derived (or a service whose ingest is in progress or was interrupted, in any process) is `symbol_count_error` naming why, a read-only store derives the row without writing it, unsupported languages come from the row, `root_missing` is one stat — and obligations' layer coverage takes its paths from `file_stats` (a nested guide written since the last ingest is still served by the raw fallback).
- The request path's file reads (the obligations corpus and ancestor-guide probe, the edited file's freshness hash and tokens) go through `internal/corpus`: stat first, an 8 KiB binary sniff (a binary file is skipped by name), and every byte read charged to the call's admission meter.
- The store opens with bounded SQLite memory (8 MiB page cache per connection, 256/512 MiB soft/hard heap, 64 MiB WAL journal limit) and an `(service_id, symbol)` index, and reads a service's observations without a temp B-tree.
- `[connector] respect_gitignore = true` (off by default until RT-M11) discovers a git work tree through `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard`, the always-skipped and default-ignored directories still applied; `fathom ingest --dry-run --stats --respect-gitignore` measures the delta. `pkg/client` gains `ServiceSummary`, `BackfillDerived`, `DerivedState` and `StoreID`.

**Query tools answer from indexes and bounded scans (responsible tenant wave C: RT-71, RT-84, RT-85, RT-87, RT-88, RT-89, RT-90; ADR-047 §4.5, §4.6, §6).**
- Routes and outbound HTTP client calls are extracted at ingest into new additive tables (`service_routes`, `service_http_calls`, `service_refs_state`) — every readable file at a service's first ingest after upgrade, then the changed files — and `blast_radius`, `cross_repo_blast_radius` and `review_diff` join their `service_ref_hops` from those rows instead of reading every service's source per call; a service not re-ingested since is named in `service_ref_unextracted`.
- The dispatch byte-budget backstop measures a result's size without marshaling a copy of it and trims an over-budget map's largest array without the marshal → unmarshal → per-field decode cascade (answers unchanged).
- `find_symbol` and `find_callers` (and every `FindSymbol`/`FindCallers` caller, `fathom verify` included) read only the matching rows — the `(service_id, symbol)` index and the `observation_calls` index — instead of decoding the service's whole observation set; a service whose call index is not complete keeps the old read for callers.
- Cross-service callers (`symbol_context`, `find_callers`, `cross_repo_callers`) are one indexed query over `observation_calls` and cross-service callees one name lookup per service, instead of loading every service whole per call; a service whose call index is not yet complete keeps the old scan.
- `mcp.QueryInterface` has no whole-tree `Sources`: tools that read file contents (`traceability`, `spec_coverage`, `dep_drift`, `traverse`, `secret_scan`, `shell_out`, `http_client_calls`, `cloc`, `stale_docs`, `proto_conformance`, `spec_refs`, `review_diff`, `references`) stream them through `ScanSources` under a 64 MiB / 5 s budget and name a stopped scan in a `partial` field (`review_diff`: `corpus_partial`; `references`: a `degraded` row). `pkg/client` gains `(*Client).ScanSources`.

**The request-path rule is a gate (responsible tenant wave E: RT-91..RT-95, RT-137; ADR-047 §4.7, §4.8).**
- Concurrent cold `obligations` calls read each edited file and each layer guide once between them. Each client single-flights those reads, and a caller that arrives after the read finds the result already stored.
- `read_source`, the integrity dimension of `review_diff`, and the enclosing-symbol lookup in `search` now read through the corpus reader. Their bytes are charged to the call's admission meter (`bytes_read` on the call-log row), and a binary file is refused after the 8 KiB sniff instead of being read whole. `review_diff` and the self-skip in `verify_plan` run git under the call's context, so git stops when the call is cancelled.
- `task validate` runs the tenancy lint (`internal/lint/tenancy`, the `tenancy-lint` task) with an allowlist that may only shrink, `.fathom/tenancy-allow.txt`. It also runs the per-tool cost harness (`internal/mcp/costtest`), which checks every registered tool, cold and warm, against its class ceiling at 1× and 10× fleet scale. Point and index tools may grow at most 1.5× when the fleet grows 10×, and 32 cold concurrent callers must start one build per key and read one walk's bytes. `pkg/client` gains `FleetAdapter`, the daemon's fleet backend, which moved out of `cmd/fathom` so the harness measures the same adapter.

**Admission observes and names (RT-53..RT-65).** `/metrics` gains `fathom_inflight`, `fathom_queue`, `fathom_slots`, `fathom_rejected_total`, `fathom_would_refuse_total`, `fathom_calls_total` and `fathom_test_callers_rejected_total`; the daemon's own console reads are admitted as `console-inprocess` and never trigger an auto-refresh; `/route` and the obligations re-rank are admitted as the egress class and skipped with a disclosure at the refuse rung and in safe mode; waiters on the list_services walk and the console memos leave when their request ends (a walk cut short is never cached as the answer); a registry read refused while a fleet call runs shows the last good registry marked `stale: fathom shedding`; `fathom doctor` gains `admission_mode`, which warns while the daemon serves with admission `off`.

**The console under the tenancy contract (responsible tenant: RT-112..RT-119, RT-121; ADR-047 §14).** A page past the model's 30 s TTL is served the last model at once while one rebuild runs outside the lock (a cold cache waits for that same rebuild, and a rebuild dropped under memory pressure or one that fails never answers an empty model), and the rebuild and Operate read the call log and session ledger as tails; the call-log tails keep the log's all-time fold plus a 35-day ring, never a row per line, and the session ledger is folded as it is read (each session's newest state and a compact row index, never the rows' bytes) for the counts, the sessions table, the activity tab and the replay; the shed rung frees the console's page caches but keeps the tails and the hooks index's offsets, so memory pressure never triggers a whole-log re-read; every console reader of `hooks.jsonl` (session pages, the catches timeline, the impact model's beat-windows, the flywheel's custodian check) reads a per-file index extended by appended bytes, never the whole log. Inside the daemon the console no longer calls itself over loopback HTTP or forks `git` on a page's path (statusz, the agent surface's `tools/list`, `/learn/ask`, plan verification and the intent audit's index oracle run in process, admitted as `console-inprocess`; HEAD is read from the repository's files); plan verification, `/refactors`' conserve and the project index run in one pool (2 workers, queue 32, one flight per key, a superseded queued key dropped, a panicking job finished as a named failure) with git under the page's deadline and a range wider than the new `[console] conserve_max_files` (default 500) refused by name; the event feeders run only while `/events` has a subscriber and read compile runs in record order from a cursor (with none, a page reading a live panel's freshness polls the ledgers first and the store off the request path, at most once per 10 s). A conserve report stopped by its 2-minute deadline is a named failure, never a report with its unfinished planes shown clean, and a request waiting on the project index or a cold model leaves when its client does. Operate gains a Tenancy panel rendering `fathom resources --json` (kept for 2 minutes and refreshed off the request path), which gains `footprint.hourly` and `ladder`, and beside it each tool's p95 per hour from the new `fathom metrics traffic --by tool-hour`.

**Interrupted ingests, deferred auto-refresh and the daemon's own instruments (responsible tenant wave B: RT-109, RT-150, RT-45, RT-46, RT-162; ADR-047).**
- An ingest records its watermark only at its final commit and writes `ingest_state = in_progress` first (a new additive store table), so a killed or halted ingest never looks fresh; auto-refresh re-ingests a service another process left `in_progress` once that process is gone (a running `fathom ingest` is never duplicated), even at the same commit and even for a killed first ingest (the row names the corpus root), unless it is quarantined.
- Auto-refresh runs no inline ingest at shed and above, while paused (safe mode — `daemon pause` now has this effect), during the 120 s warm-up after any start (`[tenancy.breaker].warmup`) or for a quarantined service, and the response envelope says so: `"refresh": {"<service>": "deferred (<reason>) — …"}`.
- The scavenge line reads total−released and `phys_footprint` before and after (the `heap_sys`/`heap_inuse` fields are gone) and is logged on every ladder event as well as the tick; the typed-pass line reads "process heap during pass … with up to N in-flight tickets" from runtime/metrics instead of "peak heap_inuse".
- Bytes written by the daemon and its sampled children (`go list`, git, rg) are kept per minute for 24 h and judged against 2 GiB/day outside explicit ingest: `/statusz` `disk_writes`, `/metrics` `fathom_bytes_written_total{subsystem="self"|"children"}` and `fathom_bytes_written_*` with the window they cover (`fathom_bytes_written_window_seconds` — the ring is in memory, so after a start it is not yet a day), and doctor's new `disk_writes` row (WARN over budget, disclosed, not enforced; names the window).

**What the daemon takes is visible when it is down (responsible tenant wave B: RT-47..RT-52, ADR-047 §13).** `fathom resources [--json] [--since 24h]` renders budget, footprint (self and children) history, lifecycle, quarantine, host, in-flight work, hooks, scratch daemons, disk and the last capture from files — the new fixed-size resource ring (`~/.fathom/daemon/<id>/resources.ring`, a sample a minute for 24 h plus every ladder transition) and the lifecycle ledger — and exits 0 within budget, 1 over budget, crash-looping, breaker-stopped or leaking scratch daemons, 2 when a file is unreadable.
- Doctor's `daemon_footprint` judges phys_footprint from that projection and WARNs with the ring's last sample when the daemon does not answer instead of SKIPping; its "restart the daemon" remedy is gone. New rows: `daemon_lifecycle`, `host_pressure`, `scratch_daemons`, `tenancy_scope`.
- `/statusz`'s `tenancy` block adds mode, the sample time, in-flight work by class and each call's tool and origin, refusals (the admission gate's refused and would-refuse totals with its mode; "not counted" in a process with no gate) and the last diagnostic capture; the session primer adds one ≤160 B line only when the daemon is not normal.
- `resources`, doctor and the primer claim only what their files earn: a lifecycle ledger with no start rows says starts and unclean exits are not recorded (crash-loop detection sees over-budget exits only) instead of "0"; a ring sample older than 5 min is shown with its age but never judged over budget or read as current host pressure; "leaked" scratch daemons are exactly the ones `daemon reap --apply` kills; `host_pressure` judges the resolved `[tenancy.host]` triggers; `tenancy_scope` WARNs on admission `off` and on a `memory_limit` / `gc_hint` conflict.
- The console's Operate page gains a `tenancy` tile (level, footprint of budget, mode, in-flight calls) from the same block, and the block in the page's JSON model; with no governor in the process the tile says so.

**Ingest off the request path, streamed (responsible tenant wave D: RT-97..RT-108, RT-110, RT-111; ADR-047 §7).**
- Auto-refresh, staleness checks and ingest read a work tree's HEAD from `.git/HEAD` and the ref files (loose, `packed-refs`, a linked worktree's `gitdir:`), cached per root until a file moves, instead of forking `git rev-parse HEAD` on every read; a layout the files cannot answer runs git under a 2 s deadline and reads as freshness unknown when it does not answer.
- An ingest stats every file first and reads only those whose size, mtime or inode moved since the daemon's last ingest of the service (and whose stored fingerprint still matches); `fathom ingest --rehash` reads everything. The files it reads stream through a 64 MiB in-flight window in input order — each hashed, extracted and written (observations 256 at a time) and then released — so an ingest's content memory no longer grows with the corpus; the summary reports `stat-skipped` and the peak bytes held.
- The typed Go pass runs only when a `.go`, `go.mod`, `go.sum` or `go.work` file changed (or was deleted) since its last successful pass, and the CUE pass only when a `.cue` file did, under the same one-at-a-time toolchain slot; a skipped pass keeps its stored edges and the ingest summary says `typed-edges: skipped — no input changed since the last successful pass`. A Go pass no longer purges the CUE pass's or the file resolver's stored edges.
- The file-resolve pass (TS/JS/Python import edges) uses the pipeline's own tree-sitter extractors instead of instantiating two new wazero modules per language per ingest that were never closed; `TSExtractor.Close` and `Pipeline.Close` release them (`Client.Close` calls it) and each module's linear memory is capped at 64 MiB (a parse past it is an engine fault, counted like any other).
- The file-resolve pass reads a TS/JS/Python file's imports from the store's new `file_imports` table while the file is unchanged (not read, not parsed), takes its symbols from the rows the ingest stored instead of extracting every file a second time, keeps binary and over-2 MiB files out of the parse, records an import-parse fault against the bytes (not retried until they change) and names every faulted file on each ingest (`FileResolveFaults`, a stderr warning in the CLI summary); it does not run when no such file changed. `fathom purge`, `remove_service`, `rename_service` and the ADR-017 self-heal cover the new table.
- A service whose watermark is a git SHA never falls back to the content manifest (a read of the whole corpus per query) when its HEAD cannot be read: freshness is unknown, disclosed. A non-git corpus's manifest is recomputed only when a file's path, size or mtime moved (a stat walk, no reads); over 20,000 files it is freshness unknown.
- The typed pass's toolchain children (`go list` and the compiles it forks, `go env`) run with `GOFLAGS` `-p` and `GOMAXPROCS` at most 2 (never raising an inherited value; other `GOFLAGS` flags kept; `FATHOM_TOOLCHAIN_PROCS=N|off` moves or lifts the ceiling) and at background priority: serve-http and a CLI `fathom ingest` put a `go` shim first on their PATH that runs the real toolchain under `nice -n 10` (and `taskpolicy -b` on macOS); the shim lives at `$TMPDIR/fathom-toolchain-<pid>`, and a killed process's is removed at the next start.
- `/refresh-file` leaves a file to a whole-service refresh the queue holds for its service (`skipped: "whole-service refresh queued|running …"`) instead of writing beside it.

**Auto-refresh re-ingests survive their requester and run one at a time (responsible tenant wave A: RT-149, RT-151; ADR-047).** The read that triggers an inline auto-refresh re-ingest waits for it only while its own request lives, and every other read of that service meanwhile answers at once from the index as it stands; the ingest runs detached from the request under one ticket shared by all inline auto-refresh ingests (the `ingest` tool and `/refresh-file` do not take it yet). A re-ingest that fails before it records the new commit is retried after 30 s, doubling to at most 30 min, instead of being debounced for that commit forever.

**The self-restart watcher stats its binary and hashes it only when it changed (RT-12).** It hashed the whole binary every 20 s; it now hashes only on a (size, mtime, inode) change, adopts only a binary whose stat held through its `version` check, and keeps a 5-minute cooldown between adoptions across restarts in `~/.fathom/daemon/<label>/lifecycle.jsonl` (when that ledger cannot be read it says so and waits until the process has itself run 5 minutes). A failed hash is logged and retried on the next poll, and a new binary whose `version` check failed is re-verified after the cooldown instead of waiting for the next deploy.

**A failed post-commit refresh says nothing was ingested (RT-170).** It announced a store-direct fallback it never ran; it now names the failure and how to refresh through the daemon once it is back.

**fathom as a responsible tenant — list_services, discovery and whole-tree reads bounded (vision 36, ADR-047; RT-1, RT-2, RT-27, RT-69, RT-70, RT-80, RT-144, RT-146).** `list_services` answers symbol counts and languages from a SQL aggregate cached per store watermark and single-flighted, never a whole-service symbol load, and classifies unsupported languages by file name only; the connector never reads a file over 2 MiB (counted under `oversize`, fingerprinted by size) and stops discovery and fetch when the requester's context ends.
- `GET /indexed?service=` answers "is this service indexed" from one `service_meta` row and the watermark-cached symbol-count aggregate (a service with zero symbols is still "not indexed", so `fathom verify` against it stays exit 2); `fathom verify`, the stop and edit hooks, `fathom plans`, `fathom init` and the console ask it instead of `list_services` (and fall back to `list_services` against an older daemon).
- `fathom ingest --dry-run [--stats] [--json]` reports files, bytes, files over the read cap, pruned directories and the largest files from stats alone, reading no file and opening no store.
- At most two whole-tree `client.Sources` reads are held at once per process; on the daemon a `/rpc` request keeps its slot until its response is written and stops waiting when the client disconnects. A request whose `X-Fathom-Origin` names a hook never waits for one. `obligations` itself no longer takes a whole-tree read (RT-143, RT-81), but a backend that refuses one makes it answer `coverage_skipped: "coverage skipped: whole-tree read in progress"` (and `layer_coverage.status: "skipped"`), which the edit and prompt hooks print as a warning line; `verify_plan` refuses by that name when a row needs spec coverage, and the Stop hook reports it as advisory, never a block. `pkg/client` gains `ServiceSymbolAggregates`, `IsIndexed`, `SourcePackages`, `PackageSymbols`, `WithHookOrigin`, `IsHookOrigin`, `WithSourcesScope` and `ErrSourcesBusy`.
- `POST /refresh-file` stats before it reads: a file over 2 MiB answers `refreshed: false` with `skipped: "oversize (…; not read)"` instead of being read (a file that grew past the cap has its old rows purged and the graph memo dropped), and the memoized graph is patched from the file's own rows, not a whole-service symbol load. A file outside the corpus an ingest walks (always-skipped or default-ignored cache directory, ignore files) answers `skipped: "outside the corpus: …"` instead of being indexed until the next ingest purges it.

**The operator can switch the daemon off, and launchd no longer relaunches it forever (responsible tenant RT-3..RT-6, RT-13..RT-15, RT-147, RT-148, RT-157, RT-167; ADR-047).** `fathom daemon stop [--for 2h]` writes `~/.fathom/daemon/disabled.json`, kills the managed pid behind a pid-reuse guard (SIGTERM, 10 s, SIGKILL) and reports success only once the process is gone and the port closed; the managed daemon (`serve-http --managed`) reads the switch before config, store or bind and exits 0 in under 50 ms (75 until a `--for` expires), scratch daemons ignore it, and `daemon start` undoes it (`status`, doctor and the session primer name it as the remedy while the switch is set, and `task deploy` installs without starting); `daemon pause`/`resume` set and clear a safe-mode flag that has no effect yet (RT-39/RT-150), and say so. Self-restart adoption exits 75 instead of 0, so a schema-2 plist relaunches it. `daemon install` writes plist schema 2 (`KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit: false}`, `ThrottleInterval 60`, `ProcessType Background`, low-priority I/O, `Nice 5`, `ExitTimeOut 20`, `--managed`); `daemon install --reconcile [--adopt]` rewrites only those keys of an installed plist with a backup and receipt under `~/.fathom/daemon/<label>/`, doctor's `daemon_posture` row and `daemon status` name the drift with the exact command, `status` names the running daemon process's signing identity (ad-hoc called out), `version --json` reports `tenancy_contract`, and `task deploy` fails with `fathom-dev signing identity missing` instead of installing an ad-hoc binary.

**Hooks no longer pile up on a slow or dead daemon (responsible tenant, RT-7..RT-10, ADR-047).** Every hook invocation has one deadline — its harness timeout minus max(1 s, 20%) — that every RPC derives from and sends as `X-Fathom-Deadline`, and past which a hook skips its optional local work (`session-hook`'s outbox sync and continuity brief, named in the primer; `doctor --brief`'s remaining checks, counted in a `hook_deadline` row); the service enumeration retries only a refused or reset connection inside it (it used to retry any failure with a fresh 4 s client). Hooks share a per-daemon breaker file (2 failures in 10 s, or a 503 with `Retry-After`, open it; backoff 5 s to 300 s; one `/healthz` prober under a `flock` lease; a good probe keeps the backoff, only a real success resets it; a hook's own client timeout counts as a failure; while it is open hooks are silent and the session primer no longer says "not responding"; `breaker_open`/`busy`/`deadline_spent` beats record what was held off), and a `disabled.json` or `paused.json` in `~/.fathom/daemon/` silences every hook addressing the managed daemon before any RPC — `daemon stop` and `daemon pause` record that daemon's address (`addr`, from `--url`), so a scratch daemon's hooks are never silenced, and a switch file the hook cannot stat (other than absent) still holds.

**`fathom daemon ps` and `fathom daemon reap` (RT-23, RT-24, RT-166, ADR-047 §10.2).** `ps` joins the daemon records — now `~/.fathom/daemon/<id>/record.json` (schema 2; the legacy `~/.fathom/daemons/` is still read this release) — with a live census of every fathom `serve-http` process and names each `ok`, `orphaned`, `idle`, `unlinked`, `no-record` or `stale-record`; `reap` is a dry run unless `--apply`, then kills only unmanaged daemons whose verdict is not `ok` (SIGTERM, 5 s, SIGKILL) — never the managed daemon, and never a `no-record` daemon whose parent is alive (its record is under another HOME) — and prunes stale records whose pid is still gone. A daemon recording itself prunes the records of scratch daemons whose pid is gone; the managed address comes from the installed plist before `[daemon].addr`.

**Tests never reach the operator's daemon (RT-16..RT-19, RT-164, ADR-047 §10.4).** Every request the `fathom` binary sends to a daemon host (loopback, or `FATHOM_HTTP`'s host) carries `X-Fathom-Caller: pid=…;ppid=…;origin=…;session=…;test=0|1`, with `test=1` derived from `FATHOM_TEST=1` as well as a test binary, and the daemon-URL defaults fall back to the unroutable `http://127.0.0.1:1` instead of `localhost:7575` under a test or `FATHOM_TEST=1` when `FATHOM_HTTP` is unset.

**The console's numbers mean one thing each (OC-H2..OC-H4, vision 35).**
- A call-log, verify-ledger or hooks-log row naming a service the daemon's registry does not list is left out of the catches timeline (`/impact`, `fathom metrics catches`) and of every per-service call figure, and named instead: `unregistered` (count and service ids) on the timeline and in each lane's denominator, `unregistered_calls` in `fathom fleet report --json`, and split out of `calls.total`'s denominator. A project filter naming such a service is a 404 that says why; with an unreadable registry nothing is excluded and `registry_note` says so.
- One metric per question: the Now and Fleet pages show `plans.proven` (the Plans page's headline, the gate's verdicts through `plans status`), `claims.current` (claims serving can still use, as on Layers and Packs) and `certify.live` (as on Certifications), each from one computation; `plans.verified`, `claims.fresh` and `certificates.live` are gone. `fathom brief --json`'s summary carries `claims_current` and `certify_live` in place of `claims_fresh` and `certificates_live`, and drops `plan_rows_verified`; the fleet table's claims column is `claims_current`.

**The config custodian audits the gate chain, not only agent config: named corpora and shipped packs (CC-11, CC-12, vision 22 §7).** A concern's `where:` names `agent-config`, `lefthook`, `taskfile` or `ci`, resolved behind the one audit engine, and `required_pattern`, `stanza_present`, `max_bytes` and `commands_resolve` join the kinds — the last holds every `task` target, local Taskfile include and repo script a corpus invokes to existence (docs → lefthook → Taskfile → scripts), resolved from the directory each command runs in (a task's or include's `dir:`, lefthook's `root:`, a GitHub `working-directory:`) and, inside an included Taskfile, in that include's namespace. `packs: ["claude-code", "codex", "lefthook", "taskfile"]` enables concern sets shipped as CUE data; a same-id entry replaces a pack's wholesale, `off: true` disables it by name, and this repo enables all four.

**`fathom doctor`'s `lefthook_gates` and `stanza_present` rows render the lefthook, claude-code and codex packs' findings instead of re-deriving them (CC-13, O27).** Their output is unchanged, except that a `lefthook.yml` that exists and cannot be read is now named unreadable instead of reported missing (I38), and `lefthook_gates` — like the capability table's lefthook column — now counts only a `fathom verify` on a non-comment line of the pre-push hook: a commented-out or pre-commit-only verify gates no push and fails the row (I17).

**`fathom config propose` offers a unified diff for each violated concern it can mend mechanically and names the remedy for the rest; it writes nothing (CC-9).** v1 relocates a dead pointer or dead script path to the one repo file with the same name (none or several are named, never guessed); `--patch` prints only the diff for `git apply`, `--json` the proposals.

**The Observatory becomes a console — wave F0 (vision 35, ADR-045; OC-1..11, OC-13, OC-15, OC-16).** Every page now renders on one app-kit shell with grouped navigation, breadcrumbs, a page heading naming its question and verbs, a light/dark/system theme control (`fathom-theme`), a skip link and a search field, at new routes: `/`, `/fleet`, `/projects/{svc}`, `/impact`, `/sessions`, `/sessions/{id}`, `/brief`, `/wiring`, `/statusz`, `/search`. The old `/observatory*` HTML routes answer 308 to their new homes while `?format=json` keeps `observatoryModel` v1 and the per-page JSON unchanged; the wiring POSTs stay at `/observatory/wiring/*` behind the local gate; `/statusz` JSON stays schema 3, and its page names every service's typed-edge state instead of rendering nothing: run by this daemon, recorded by an earlier daemon process (replayed from the store), failed with its reason, does not apply (no `go.mod`), or unknown (no pass recorded anywhere).
- Every console page answers `?format=json` with one envelope (`fathom.console.page/v1`): its verbs, the shared metrics it shows with a truth state per value (`present`, `zero`, `absent`, `stale`, `partial`, `error`), each verb's `--json` as a section (`/fleet` carries `fathom fleet report --json`, computed by the verb's own function), and the page's model. 404, 400, unknown-project and unreadable-store answers — and every wiring preview/apply/undo refusal, including a `GET` of those endpoints — render on the shell with the reason and the remedy; an unknown project's remedy is `fathom init --path <repo> --service <id> --url <daemon>`, and a stale index's is `fathom post-commit-hook --dir <root> --service <id>`, both of which ingest through the daemon (a shell `fathom ingest` wrote a store the daemon never reads).
- Shared metrics are computed once (`internal/console`) from one read per model, so pages cannot disagree even while the ledgers grow: the fleet's "tool calls (log)" tile and the home page count every row in the resolved call log, naming the unattributed rows and the rule that chose the log (the survey's 50,747-vs-88,875); sessions are distinct sessions everywhere, per repo included; the brief keeps a partial open-observation count as a "≥" lower bound and lists it under "Not measured". A call log or observations file that exists and cannot be read is an `error` value naming the failure — never "not measured", "no observations file" or a smaller total; `fleet report` gains `observations_error`.
- The call log is resolved once: `serve-http` records itself in `~/.fathom/daemons/<host>-<port>.json` once bound and renders from its own `--call-log`; `metrics traffic|series|rollup|interventions|rotate`, `instrument --adoption`, `flywheel`, `fleet report` and `research report|bundle` read that daemon's log (after an explicit `--call-log` or `FATHOM_CALL_LOG`) instead of a path relative to their working directory, `review` appends its gate row to it, and a daemon started with logging off makes them read none. Each names the log and its rule: a `call_log` object in JSON and a `call log: … (rule …)` line in text.
- `GET /events` streams `ingest` (content moved, never a no-op re-ingest), `compile` (one per run), `verify`, `session` (the repo label mapped to its service) and `daemon` events (names and IDs only) with `<epoch>-<n>` IDs, a 25 s keepalive, `Last-Event-ID` replay of the last 64 — the whole ring after a daemon restart — and a 16-subscriber bound; `GET /search?q=` returns projects, plans and plan rows, symbols, rules and records, verbs and pages as a page, an HTML fragment (`?format=fragment`) or JSON: every word must match, exact IDs lead, groups order by their best match, totals are exact, `&group=` pages through a whole group, and a query past 8 words or 200 characters is a 400. The ⌘K palette and the live "updated" affordance landed in stage 0 (OC-12, OC-14, below).
- Console page views are counted per route pattern (`fathom_console_page_views_total{route}` on `/metrics`, durably in `~/.fathom/console-views.jsonl`) for UX-M4; `HEAD` and `?format=json` requests are not views. `github.com/a-h/templ` becomes a direct requirement at the kit's pinned v0.3.1020 (the kit's `table` package).

**Now and the fleet — console wave B (vision 35 §3; OC-17..OC-20).** `/` becomes the Now page: a stat band, the attention queue — gate failures, unproven claims, stale or unknown indexes, certificate gaps, uncompiled governing guides, open observations, in that order, each with its evidence link and the command that fixes it — "since you last looked" (pushes, deploys, measurements and restarts read from reflogs, the call log, the daemon record and bench-reading commits, marked new against your previous view of Now), and a fleet pulse. `/fleet` becomes one sortable, filterable, paged table of every project with sort and filters in the URL, rolled up by health above it (unknown is never healthy), its default order still RG-4's unrealized value; every sparkline shares one scale and splits at a day the call log holds no row.
- `fathom brief --json [--store S]` prints the Now page's projection, which the page's JSON carries as a section; it reads the store the daemon at `--url` recorded, read-only. Plan proof is the verify ledger's newest run of each plan at the project's registered root (scratch clones and archived plans are left out), never a fresh verify.
- New shared metrics `fleet.wired_fresh`, `plans.verified`, `claims.fresh` and `certificates.live` (a count missing a source is a named lower bound). The four questions and the machine surfaces sit one disclosure below Now; the tool-load chart leaves `/fleet` (Impact's per-tool table carries its counts).

**`fathom verify` against the daemon checks every row of a long plan.** The daemon's response budget (SU-3) cut `verify_plan`'s answer to what fits — 20 of `observatory-console.md`'s 59 rows — and the daemon-mode gate, the Stop and claim-moment hooks and `plans status|next` read only those, so the rest were never gated; the cut rows are now verified in follow-up calls, and a row whose verdict still never arrives is reported unknown (`verdict-elided`), never left out. A daemon-mode gate may now fail a claim it never checked before.

**An unreadable source is named, never counted as nothing (I38).** Plan discovery (`plans status|next`) read an unreadable `docs/plans` as no plans — `filepath.Glob` drops the directory's read error — and now fails with it; the realization ladder's gating rung reads unknown there, not "not applicable". `certify gaps|status` name each target source they could not read (the intent corpus, `incidents.md`, a port's source file): `unread` in the JSON, an INCOMPLETE line in text, the counts then a lower bound and no "no manifests found" skip claimed — an unreadable repository used to read as one that declares nothing. Found by the console wave C browser proof while the external volume refused reads.

**The console's Proof pages — wave C (vision 35 §4.4, §4.7; OC-21..OC-26, RG-5, SD-2..SD-4).** `/plans` shows every plan across the fleet with a proof bar counted from the gate's verdicts, never the checkboxes, headed by the claimed-but-unproven gap for the fleet and per repository (`?svc=`), and the verify ledger's claimed-vs-proven trend once the ledger holds 14 days of rows (`stat.Absent` with the count until then); `/plans/{svc}/{plan}` adds a read-only board of rows by state (the kit's `kanban`), each card naming its contract kind, with a drawer showing a row's evidence and a burn-up from the plan's git history cross-checked with the ledger; `/plans/{svc}/{plan}/rows/{id}` is that evidence as a page — contract, verdict criterion by criterion, the call path from the test to the symbol or the line a lexical oracle matched (`code.RenderExcerpt`), and the row's history. `/certifications` counts every target by its best certificate's state (the kit heatmap with truth states, and its table) beside the gap list and each remedy; `/refactors?svc=&since=` shows `fathom conserve`'s planes as chips (a plane that did not run reads "not run", never "conserved") and the reading set with an excerpt of each partial function's unexecuted lines; the project overview leads with the realization ladder, the first unmet rung carrying its remedy.
- Plans are verified in the background through the same path `plans status` takes (an origin-stamped client, so never usage), keyed by the plan's content, the index watermark and HEAD (a commit that moves only HEAD re-applies the working-tree overlays to the daemon's verdicts already fetched, never asking the daemon again); a page waits a bounded time and names what is still verifying, and the plans panels are told over `/events` when the verdicts land.
- New verbs the pages render: `fathom plans history --service S --plan P [--json]` (the burn-up), `fathom verify … --row ID` (one row's verdict, gated alone), `fathom fleet realization --service S [--json]` (the ladder). `plans status --json` adds `op_proven` and `op_open` (the measurement rows among `proven` and `open`); new shared metrics `plans.claimed_unproven`, `plans.proven`, `certify.gaps` and `certify.live`.
- `plans status|next --service S` read lexical oracles and the self-skip history from the service's registered root, as discovery already did (they read the working directory, so an oracle row read another repository's file).
- The Proof pages show a repository whose plans or certify sources could not be read as an error with the reason, and ask again (a plan or a conserve report after 30 s, a certify report on the next read); a failure is never cached for good.

**The realization ladder reads its window and the verify ledger (RG-5, OC-25; `fathom fleet realization`, the project overview, the fleet grade).** The gating rung counts the gate runs the verify ledger records (SD-1) in the last 14 days — it read the session ledger's `verify_catches`, which a pre-push gate never writes, so repositories that gate on every push (conjure, agentic-kit) were told to add the gate — and a verify ledger that is missing or unreadable makes gating unknown, never "0 runs". Obligations injections and the idle rule's sessions now count the same 14 days (they were all-time beside evidence saying "in the last 14 days"); a session ledger that cannot be read grades the rungs, never "idle", and says so (`sessions_note`).

**The proof ledger's pages say what they count (wave C's review).**
- `/plans`' claimed-vs-proven trend counts the verify-ledger runs of the scope's current plans only — scratch service IDs, deleted plans and README.md are left out (the fleet trend read 321 plans beside a portfolio of 142) — and follows `?svc=`; it renders the new `fathom plans history [--service S] --json` (no `--plan`: the trend of a service's or the registry's current plans), which the panel's verb chip names.
- The claimed-but-unproven headline is a named lower bound ("≥ N" with the remedy) while claimed rows sit measured-stale behind a stale daemon (PC-5), never a clean measured 0; the proof bar's proven segment reads "proven, not counting (op) measurements" and the tables' "Proven, (op) included", so one label no longer carries two numbers.
- A claimed card the gate cannot decide names the real cause — "no contract" for a row with none, else the first undecided clause other than `runtime_pass`, which is unknown on every row and decides none.
- Row evidence carries a lexical or `gone:` row's count bound (`eq`/`min`/`max`, CV-29) in its contract and in the oracle's reading ("exactly 0 matches … the bound is met"), and an oracle file that does not exist reads as the gate's path-absent, not "could not be read".

**`/certifications` and `/refactors` name the store they read, and `/refactors` never writes the repository (wave C's review).**
- The `certify status|gaps` and `conserve` chips carry the daemon's `--store`: run as shown they read its default `<repo>/workspace/db/fathom.sqlite`, another store (fathom: 65 targets against the page's 77).
- The reading set's partial and unexecuted counts read "not measured" with the reason when no execution evidence classed a changed function, and a lower bound when only some were (they were measured zeros); `fathom conserve --json`'s residue adds `classed_by_execution` and its text says when neither class was measured. The band adds unattested and no longer calls static attestation "every changed line ran".
- `fathom conserve` and `certify`'s reads (`git status`, a diff against the working tree) run against a private copy of the index (`internal/gitro`), so a `/refactors` page view no longer rewrites `.git/index` in the registered checkout under `index.lock`, where an agent's concurrent `git add` failed.

**The plan board, its drawer and the Proof pages' controls (wave C's review).** A plan's board shares the content column from `md` up (lanes at least 12rem wide wrap to a second row instead of hiding behind the board's scroll: five fixed lanes needed 1,344 px of a 1,094 px column at 1440); a card's title link, the pagers on `/plans` and `/certifications` and the `/refactors` chooser are 44 px targets (FATHOM-C-KIT-05); the drawer's verb chip gets its Copy button (`console.js` reveals content announced with a `console:content` event); opening a row's evidence in the drawer counts as a view of the row page for UX-M4; and the plans panels are told over `/events` only when a plan's verdicts land or change, never on a recomputation that changed nothing (one commit to a busy checkout published 64 verify events and turned over the whole replay ring).

**The project workspace — console wave E (vision 35 §3; OC-35..OC-40).** `/projects/{svc}` becomes a workspace of nine tabs that are URLs: Overview, and Index, Intent, Activity and Wiring as their own pages at `/projects/{svc}/<tab>`, each with its verbs, JSON sections and shared metrics (`project.symbols`, `project.calls`, `project.sessions`); Governance, Plans, Proof and Refactor open `/layers`, `/plans`, `/certifications` and `/refactors` with `?svc=`.
- The Index tab shows languages with engine and tier, typed share, edge kinds and inventory keys (`console.IndexProfile`, the facts `report evidence` reports), a symbol's code neighborhood through the kit's `viz.LayoutEgo` with a table alternative (`console.Neighborhood`: depth 2, resolved and heuristic edges distinct, six per column with the rest aggregated and counted), and a read-only Scope panel: active suppressions, a glob's preview and its purge dry run per location, with the exact commands. SP-10 stays open: the page runs no purge, because the daemon has no purge path that flushes its own live caches.
- `fathom intent audit --read-only` renders the audit and writes nothing — no clock, baseline, debt or enforcement-probe cache row, no beat; the Intent tab (`console.IntentView`) runs it that way, its index lookups stamped as the console's own reads.
- `fathom doctor --repo-only` runs only the eleven rows that read the repository's own files; the Wiring tab (`console.WiringView`) shows them with drift (failing wiring rows plus the preview plan's apply and BLOCKED items) and receipts beside the unchanged Preview → Apply → Undo. The Activity tab (`console.ProjectActivity`) reads the project's calls from the resolved call log and its sessions from the ledger.

**Console polish from the F0 browser proof.** Repository paths wrap after their `/` separators and remedy commands at their spaces, never mid-name; `/statusz`'s services fit a 390 px screen (a table from `lg` up, one stacked entry per service below it; the service name is the row's link; a legend explains "replayed" once) and the nav names it "Daemon status", its title; the session page's heading names the session by its short id with the full id beneath, and states law 14 once; the top bar keeps a `fathom` brand below `md`.

**The console's own reads are no longer counted as usage, and the brief's figures are the shared ones (the F0 proof's two defects).** The observatory model's and the fleet page's registry reads carry the origin `console` in the call log (`mcp.OriginConsole`, still logged); every call-log reader — the call total, tool tables, fleet, impact, `metrics`, `instrument --adoption`, `flywheel`, `doctor`, research, discoveries — and server_stats' surface traffic leave them out, and the call total names how many it excluded. `/brief` and `fathom brief` report the call log's total (was the attributed subset, 51,814 against 90,258) with the attributed share named as a subset, and the agent surface's measured tool count through the new shared metric `surface.agent_tools` (was a literal "16-tool" beside "25 tools").

**Console panels say "Updated — refresh this panel" when their data changes, without moving (OC-14, the kit's `interaction.live-freshness`).** Every panel names the sources it follows and every other page body is one region following the sources its model reads; the page subscribes to `/events` from the position it was rendered at and shows the connection state with a stop/resume control. `/events` also names fleet-wide keys (`index`, `layers`, `plans`) and, when a reconnect's cursor cannot be replayed (a daemon restart, or more than 64 events missed), sends a `gap` event naming every source.

**⌘K (Ctrl+K elsewhere) opens a command palette over every console page (OC-12, the kit's `interaction.command-palette`).** The top bar's search field becomes the palette's trigger, a link to `/search` that still works without JavaScript; the palette searches as you type, moves with the arrow keys, opens a result on Enter and closes on Escape with focus returned. `/search?format=fragment` (or the `X-AppKit-Palette` header) now answers the kit's results fragment instead of fathom's list markup, and the `/search` page leads with its own search form.

**Search and the plan page answer "which rows of plan P remain" within three clicks (OC-55..OC-57, UX-M1).**
- A plan result on `/search`, in the ⌘K palette and in `/search?format=json` opens its plan page and a row result its row page — both opened the project page, even for an exact row ID. Rows are read with the plan parser the row page uses, so an in-progress `[~]` row or an `id:` that is not the first annotation key is found, a plan is found by its title too, and a row's chip is `fathom verify … --row ID --json`.
- A plan page leads with a Remaining panel listing every row `fathom plans next` lists, each linking to its row page — the board shows 12 cards a lane, so 23 of the observatory plan's 43 open rows were a closed table away — and its JSON carries them as `model.remaining` (a named lower bound while claims await their verdicts). The board's table alternative now lists the other rows, so each row sits in one table and the page grows by no row.

**The console moves to app-kit `9fa58e6`, carrying four more of fathom's kit returns (FATHOM-S0-KIT-01/02, FATHOM-F0-KIT-05/06), and the mirror now carries app-kit's FSL-1.1-ALv2 licence.** Each page's one `h1` is the kit page heading's, in the content with its trail and a wrapping meta list, and the top bar shows the brand below `md` instead of the page title; pager links are 44 px targets with a focus outline, the ⌘K dialog's and the plan drawer's backdrop is the theme's `scrim` role instead of a fixed black, and the wiring preview's "Show both versions" label is readable (4.5:1 or better, was below 3.2:1).

**The console moves to app-kit `d9dd789`, the kit revision carrying fathom's own vision-35 contributions (FATHOM-KIT-01..08) and wave F0's four returned defects (FATHOM-F0-KIT-01..04).** Tables scope their headers and scroll inside their own box, breadcrumb links are 44 px targets with a focus outline, and the fleet traffic and per-service sparklines fill their cards at every width; the tool-load chart draws a phone-sized version below `md` instead of scrolling its counts off the card.

**Console wave D: what fathom knows, and why an agent saw a rule — the Knowledge pages (vision 35, ADR-045; OC-27..OC-34).** `/layers` draws the four strata with every layer in its band (current claims, lifecycle, real compile runs and their cost — a refused run's estimate is never counted as spend), a projects × layers coverage matrix where a cell nothing compiled is hatched "not compiled", never 0, and the claim lifecycle; `/layers/{layer}` lists compile runs with cost, accepted and rejected counts, rejection reasons, uncompiled scopes and the dry-run command, `/claims/{id}` a claim's statement, cited lines, scope, tier, status and verdict history, `/packs` dialect packs as a language × capability matrix (measured precision only where a reading exists, "unmeasured" otherwise) beside layer packs and where each is active, and `/explain?svc=&file=` every candidate rule by scope tier, the cap, the served list and why each displaced rule lost.
- New verbs, each the JSON its page carries: `fathom layers strata|coverage [--json]`, `fathom layers show <name> --json`, `fathom claim show <id>`, `fathom report packs` and `fathom obligations --service S [--file F] [--layer rules|claims|all] [--explain] [--json]` (CLI only; the MCP `obligations` tool is unchanged). They read the store the daemon at `--url` recorded (else `[daemon].store`, else the workspace default) through a read-only mount, and refuse a missing store rather than create one.
- Serving never serves two claims whose normalized statements are identical (OC-33): the double "Inject clocks…" rule no longer takes two of the six slots for `internal/conserve/conserve.go`; the first in ranking order is kept and the duplicate is not counted as displaced.
- The Knowledge pages' first review: every claim count drills down to its rows — `/claims?layer=&svc=&status=` and `fathom claim list [--json]`, 50 a page — so "which of fathom's obligations claims are stale" has an answer; an unreadable `.fathom/layers` is named (a note, a lower-bound layer count, a layer it may define marked `unknown`, never `orphan`) instead of silently dropping the project's packs; a superseded or refuted claim's excerpt shows its compile-time text, since those citations are never re-anchored; resume remedies print a command that runs (`--store <the daemon's store> --continue --dry-run=false`; `--continue` refuses without `--store`); a layer nothing (really) compiled reads absent for its uncompiled scopes and its accepted and rejected counts, never "every compile covered its corpus" or a measured 0; a project pack that failed to load keeps its load error on `/packs` and no invented stratum or role; the dialect matrix adds each language's typed-edge share from the store and names the tier a precision/recall reading measured; the explainer pages its candidate table (50 a page), numbers raw-fallback rows apart so a rule has one number, and reads "filled the cap before this rule, scoped to the repository root"; heatmap row labels are fitted by width, not clipped; pack sources render as the kit's `callout.Badge`.

**The console's Evidence and System pages — wave F (OC-41..OC-45, SH-9).** `/impact` answers Q1–Q4 in order, draws each counterfactual arm as a range (p25–p75 of real tokens per turn, the median marked) beside its session count, says "not instrumented" for a tool whose calls record no outcome instead of a colored zero, labels every tool by caller class, and puts verify refusals, conserve losses, integrity catches and guard refusals on one filterable timeline; `/sessions` is a table of sessions (one row per session) with a windowed replay at `/sessions/{id}` — at most 2,000 events per response, paged and filterable by channel (the replay once shipped 9.3 MB); new `/operate` shows the daemon, calls per day per surface (a day with no rows hatched, never zero) with p50/p95/errors, latency by surface and caller class, the zero-call list, the flywheel, `config --explain`, time-to-remedy and a project's capabilities; new `/decide` shows the decision port's provider, sites, ruling log and the latest calibration reading's table. New verbs print the same projections: `fathom metrics impact|catches|sessions`, `fathom decide log` and `fathom fleet remedy` (time-to-remedy per repository from the report snapshots, the 2026-08-08 baseline recorded); `metrics traffic` gains `--by day|caller` and `--url`, `instrument --adoption` gains `--url`.

**Wave F's pages and verbs keep every truth state (the wave F fix round).** A guard refusal older than the hooks log's last rotation still counts: the guard lane and the replay read `hooks.jsonl.1` too (the real ledgers held 8 refusals the timeline showed as a measured 0). A `?svc=`/`--service` or window filter decides per lane whether its instrument recorded anything there, so a project with no review runs reads "not instrumented for <project>" and the verify lane counts over that project's runs; a project no registry entry and no instrument names is the shell's 404.
- No session ledger, an unreadable one, an unreadable hooks log, call log or decisions log is an absent or error value on `/sessions`, `/sessions/{id}`, `/impact`, `/operate` and `/decide` and in `metrics sessions`, `metrics catches`, `metrics traffic` and `decide log` — never "0 sessions", "no beats recorded", "no calls in window" or a site's 0 applied. `metrics traffic --json` then carries `state` and `reason` instead of `total_calls`; `metrics sessions --json` counts and `decide log --json` per-site `applied`/`errors` become truth-state values.
- New `fathom metrics load` prints the Operate page's load (calls, p50, p95 and errors per day per surface; tools by caller class), and `/operate` names it on those panels, gives the zero-call list its own `instrument --adoption` panel and renders `fathom harness matrix`; `harness matrix` reads the console's clock.
- Time to remedy keeps "≥" on a stale median that is also a lower bound (`fleet remedy --json` gains `median_lower_bound`), omits its gap counts when no snapshot exists, lists a gap-free repository's `episodes` as `[]`, and names its grade column "Grade at the latest snapshot".
- Verb chips quote a value a shell would split, so a pasted chip runs what the page ran; the session replay names its verb in its heading and on its ledger-row panel, and its HTML window stays 200 events whatever `?per=` asks; below `md` the per-tool outcomes, latency tables, gap table, harness matrix and ruling log are stacked lists and `/sessions` has a sort control; the catches panel states its 30 s reuse instead of "live"; the heatmap's scroll floor is a real `min-w-[560px]` rule.

**The console's Learn group — wave G (OC-46..OC-48).** `/learn` draws the pipeline — source, extract, index, compile, serve, gate, evidence — for a chosen project (`?svc=`, default the most-queried) with the kit's layered graph and its table alternative, one panel per stage naming its instrument, its live counts (absent, never 0, when an instrument did not answer), the `fathom capabilities` switches that deliver it and the page that shows it in full; its JSON carries `fathom fleet report --json` and the project's own `fathom capabilities --repo R --service S --json`. `/learn/ask` maps nine question shapes to the agent-surface tool that answers each, with how often each was asked on the project and a try-it (`?try=<shape>&q=`) that makes one read-only call on `/rpc/agent` with the console's origin (never counted as usage, never a ledger write), beside `fathom harness matrix --json`; `/learn/guides` and `/learn/guides/{slug}` render the use-case guides, the glossary and the laws built into the binary (`docs/fathomdocs`) with the kit's prose and table of contents.
- `fathom harness matrix` reads its demotion clock through `harnessMatrixNow`, so the verb and `/learn/ask` agree on a tier; the observatory CSS imports the kit's `docs/prose.css` and scans `appkit/ui/docs`.
- The Learn pages keep the truth state of what they read: an unreadable call log is an error on `/learn`'s serve stage and on every "asked here" of `/learn/ask` (it read as a measured 0), and plans the daemon cannot read are an error on the is-it-done example, never "no claimed row was found".
- A try-it whose tool cut its answer to fit its budget is `partial`, with the tool's own total, the rows it listed and its instruction for the rest (`listed`, `total`, `total_lower_bound`, `how_to_get_more` in the answer's JSON); it read as a complete answer whose total was the listed count.
- `/learn`'s gate counts the project's current plans (`docs/plans`) once each, whichever checkout ran them, at each plan's latest run, and names the ledger rows it leaves out (scratch copies, deleted plans); it counted every ledger row and every distinct plan path.
- A stage whose own count shows it at work reads "Working here" when a switch it can use is off (with the remedy only for a broken switch), and extract and index read "Stale here" with the daemon-routed refresh over a stale index — never "Off here" beside live counts; a stage links only to a page (or tab) the console serves, and says when its page is not built yet.
- A stage's verb chip is a CLI command that prints its headline (source's and compile's chips were a verb that does not, and an agent-surface call); calls are offered as calls, and every copy button keeps its label whole.
- The Learn facts are cached on their inputs — the store's watermark and compile runs, the call log read incrementally, the verify ledger and plans directory, the probed files — never on the model's 30-second clock; the guide's outline rail sticks with 44 px entries, and the pipeline's table alternative names each stage.

- Add opt-in, loopback-only `theme-guide` on app-kit's exact `dc77e66` mirror (re-vendored from the side-branch pin `bcfeb8e`) with synthetic Observatory states and source-preserving preview/reset/export over an app-owned baseline. This adds indirect uniseg v0.4.7 and updates the mirror's SDK/x/time graph; agentic-kit and normal daemon startup are unchanged (docs/app-kit.md).
- The Observatory's light `positive` role adopts app-kit's contrast-safe `oklch(0.51 0.14 155)` (was 0.55), so the generated theme is byte-identical to the kit's. `appkit.adoption.json` stops pinning high-churn source files and re-pins `recipe.styleguide.local` to the kit's record of this return.
- Registry counts disclose incomplete coverage; unreadable call logs and session ledgers no longer appear as zero activity. These are independent display repairs, not metric-reader or service-JSON changes.
- CSS generation requires a successful content read/hash instead of a separate access predicate, preserves read-error causes and still refuses missing, escaped, symlinked or concurrently changed sources before publication.

**The doctrine plane's computed views (vision 23 §2, custodian DP-3/DP-4, ADR-026 amendment 2026-09-25).** `fathom intent doctrine [--kind visions|bets] [--json]` shows where each vision of the series the router names stands — its status or trigger, the plans it declares as its own (`Plan:`) with their claimed rows (`landed` only when those rows show claimed work and no open row, never from a status word or a plan it merely cites), broken links and cited decision ids no record carries, days since its newest dated statement, and a for-review promise with no plan past 30 days — and every `docs/intent/scope.md` goal as a pre-registered bet (`{bet, instrument, threshold, review}` on its `Done when:`, the instrument resolved to a `fathom` verb, a Taskfile target or a repo script) or refused with what it lacks; doctor gains a `doctrine` row that warns and never fails.
- Only the vision series' promotion metadata is read (status line, condition labels, plan and decision pointers, links, dates), never into the intent corpus; a bet-only block never counts toward K0's annotation signal, and one outside a scope goal is named `misplaced-bet`; a vision home, plan, link target, decisions directory, Taskfile or script that exists and cannot be read makes the counts named lower bounds — never an absence, a broken link or a refusal (ADR-027); an unknown `--kind` is an error; G2 (arm bench) and G3 (thread bet) now carry their bets, review 2026-10-25.

**`fathom verify` lint names a claimed row with no contract — one whose only annotation is a skip waiver (`skip_test`/`skip` with no `symbol:`/`oracle:`/`gone:`/spec ID), or one with no annotation beyond its id — it verifies `no-contract`, which only `--strict` blocks.** Of the 28 claimed rows in that shape, 24 now carry lexical or ledger oracles on their evidence and the four voided 2026-09-13 pilots (SB-37/38/39/41, O41) are unchecked pending their receipts-wave-5 reruns; 18 measured or landed rows (CV-M1/2/4..14, CV-43/44, TC-M1/17/18) close with oracles on their readings.

**The observatory story page counts sessions, not ledger rows.** `/observatory/story` (and its JSON) now keeps one row per session id — the last one, the impact page's rule — so `sessions`, verify catches and edit sessions are per session, `ledger_rows` names the rows they came from, and the recent list shows up to eight distinct ids newest first instead of one id repeated.

**A service root that is itself a symlink is now walked (O39).** `fathom ingest --path <link>` indexed zero files and exited 0, and `references` found no occurrence of any symbol in a service whose recorded root names its directory through a symlink, while `verify_plan` still resolved the callers from the store; Discover now walks the link's target with identical repo-relative paths.

**`fathom report evidence` no longer reports zeros from a store it just created.** `--store` now defaults to `[daemon].store`, then the workspace store, and the verb refuses (exit 1, naming the store) when that store does not exist, indexes no service, or does not index the named `--service`; the report names the store it read (`store` in JSON).

**Reading set: a comment, blank line or reflowed line inside a function body no longer makes the function `modified`, and a coverage profile that maps only partly names its unplaced file keys (`coverage_unmapped`, `coverage_unmapped_sample` on `fathom conserve` and the review `conservation` dimension).** `modified` now compares each function's comment-free token stream, as a doc comment above it already was not a change (CV-M12 listed a comment-only `runLocalize`); `coverage_unusable` stays reserved for a profile that maps nothing.

**The reading set now prefers execution over static reach (CV-67..72, ADR-044 amended 2026-09-25, CV-M13).** CV-M13 calibrated the reading set's static-reach `attested` rule against real Go coverage: 3.7% of `attested` functions never executed, and 31 of 91 `unreached` functions DID execute, outside the first-party call graph. `task tests:attest` now runs `go test -json -coverpkg=./... -coverprofile=<results_dir>/coverage.out ./...` in ONE run (the same JSON still feeds the envelope; `TESTKIT_COVER=0` opts out) and writes a commit/dirty/race/passed-bound `coverage.meta.json`. `internal/conserve.LoadCoverage` maps a profile's blocks onto repo-relative files via each `go.mod`'s own module path (a repo may hold several), merging duplicate blocks by maximum count. When a usable, commit-bound profile covers a changed function (I44: unbound, dirty or failing-run profiles are never used, named as `coverage_unusable`; per-function fallback to static reach when that function's or its reaching tests' file moved since the profile's commit), `Residue` classes it `attested`/`partial`/`unexecuted` from the CHANGED blocks' own execution counts, but `partial` vs `unexecuted` is decided over the WHOLE function (a function with an unchanged block that ran is `partial`, never `unexecuted`, even when its own changed block did not) — a `partial` item names the `unexecuted_lines`, numerically ordered. A pure-deletion edit (no line added, so `git diff -U0` reports no `+` range) still counts as a changed "point" at its own head position, so removing a guard or statement from a function that never ran is never vacuously cleared the way a genuine comment-only edit is.
