Every item's new `evidence` field names which rule classed it (`"executed"` or `"static-reach"`); `unanalyzed_functions` reconciles `changed` against every class. `internal/testevidence.JoinCasesToSymbols` disambiguates a bare `Test*` name shared across packages by the case id's own package portion, instead of leaving both unjoined (CV-M13's control: 34 of 566 cases). `fathom conserve --coverage-meta FILE` (default `[tests] results_dir/coverage.meta.json`) wires it, and so does `fathom review --since`/MCP `review_diff`'s conservation dimension — the SAME range answers identically from every surface now, not static reach only for the latter two; `fathom.conserve/v1`'s `residue` object and the review payload's `residue` object both carry the new fields additively.

**`fathom conserve` reports the reading set: changed functions no attested test reaches (CV-60..65, ADR-044 amended 2026-09-25).** Decision 7 handed Review Kit "the residue no test covers" but nothing computed it. `internal/conserve.Residue` now finds every function added or modified in `base..head` (a changed, non-test, non-generated Go file; `ChangedFunctions`) and classes each `attested`/`unattested`/`unreached` from the index's resolved call edges back to `Test*` functions and those tests' commit-bound runs (the same O114 rule certificates use, `certify.ReachUnchangedSince`, now exported). It is never a plane (no verdict, no effect on `--strict`) and never a percentage or threshold (I43): counts and an ordered, capped item list only (`unreached` then `unattested`, by resolved caller count then symbol; default cap 200, `--residue-max`). Unproven — classing nothing — when the index is not at HEAD, the typed pass failed, or a changed file's base could not be read (I44); `Changed`/`Unanalyzed` are still reported, since the diff itself never depends on the index. `fathom conserve` prints it after the planes; `fathom.conserve/v1` carries it as an additive `residue` object; the `conservation` review dimension carries its counts plus the first five `unreached` items.

**`fathom.conserve/v1` findings and `fathom.certify.gaps/v1` targets now carry a source location (RK-36 anchors).** Both fathom-backed platform-engineering Review Kit adapters (`fathom-conserve`, `fathom-certify`) built `core.Entry` values with no `Span`, because neither wire shape gave them a location — every failing verdict cell printed "(no source anchor — defect)" even for a genuinely real finding. `conserve.Finding` gains additive, `omitempty` `file`/`line`: the finding's subject's own repo-relative location, at HEAD when it still exists there, else at BASE, populated by every plane that already has it at hand (integrity's guarded test, resurrection's reappeared function, surface's inventory item and consumer call site, absence's new call site, certificates' `// certifies:` marker/test file) — governance's own claims carry no single-file location and stay empty, honestly. `certify.GapTarget`/`certify certify gaps --json`'s `certifyGapsTarget` gain an additive `source` object (an invariant's row, a port interface's declaration, an incident heading, or a certifies marker's own line) threaded through `internal/certify/{gaps,port,lostdeclarations}.go`. Neither schema string changes.

**CV-M4 fixed and re-measured: the Go `api:` surface delta now agrees with apidiff (0.960 precision / 1.000 recall over 50 pairs).** `internal/analysis/inventory/apicompat.go` gives every Go `api:` type declaration a struct/interface/alias shape (from the same per-file `go/ast` parse, no `go/types` pass) and applies `golang.org/x/exp/apidiff`'s own compatibility rules over it instead of a whole-text diff: an added exported struct field, or an added interface method on an already-sealed interface, is compatible; a `type X T` → `type X = T` alias flip is always a loss; `any`/`interface{}` are canonicalized at render time for every Go item. Fixes the three disagreement classes `docs/bench/readings/cv-m4-apidiff-calibration-2026-09-24.md` measured (0.615p/0.941r, ADR-044's own revisit trigger) plus two more the full 50-pair re-run found (a `var`'s literal-vs-named-constant initializer; the same any/interface{} collapse inside a func-literal-valued `var`) — `docs/bench/readings/cv-m4-apidiff-full50-2026-09-24.md`. One residual false positive (a dependency's own cross-package type alias) is named, not fixed — out of this change's stated scope.

**Fixed: the Go `api:` surface delta now tracks an interface's own embedded-interface fields.** `interfaceMethodShapes` skipped every embedded interface field outright (never even an opaque marker), and `itemChanged` takes the type-identity branch exclusively once both sides carry a `TypeDetail` — so adding, removing, or retargeting an embed (e.g. `io.Closer`, a real apidiff-classified incompatible change: any external implementer must now also satisfy it) rendered an empty `Diff`, a recall regression against the OLD whole-text comparison this fix's own CV-M4 work replaced. Each embedded interface field now gets an `"embed <name>"` entry in `TypeDetail.Interface`, so an add/remove/retarget registers as a shape change via the same asymmetric rule a named method addition already gets — it does not resolve the embed's own method set (still a documented gap; no cross-package resolution). None of CV-M4's calibration corpus (50 release pairs) happens to add or remove an embedded interface, so this gap went unmeasured by that reading.

**CV-M5 fixed and re-measured: `conserve.Resurrected` no longer flags long-lived, independently-written twins as a Blindness copy.** The head matching pool now admits a candidate only when it is itself new in the range or its body hash changed in the range, not merely co-located in a changed file. `docs/bench/readings/cv-m5-blindness-replay-2026-09-24.md`'s update: ADR-025's DuckDB genuine range drops from 24 false `body_resurrected` findings to 0; both seeded copies (DuckDB, vector) still flag. A second, unrelated gap surfaced by the same replay is filed as O117, not fixed here: `fathom conserve`'s default plan discovery hands its plan reader absolute paths where repo-relative ones are expected, so `retires:`/`surface_change:` declarations may be silently invisible without an explicit `--plan`.

**`review_diff` (MCP) now wires the `integrity` dimension.** Before this, a `since` ref made `review_diff` build a real `internal/conserve.Input` and run every registered plane for `conservation`, but the SAME "integrity" plane's own findings were never flattened onto `IntegrityFindings`, so `DimIntegrity` rendered `checked:true` with zero findings once a base resolved — a silently clean-shaped pass, not "needs a base ref". `internal/mcp/review_integrity.go` mirrors `cmd/fathom`'s own wiring (`reviewintegrity.go`); the tool's own `Description` now names both `integrity` and `conservation` instead of neither.

**`fathom compile --dry-run` no longer prints a confident zero cost when it has no price for the model.** The dry-run path never called `WithLLM` at all, so a dry run's cost estimate was ALWAYS computed from a zero rate regardless of `--profile`, printing `cost_usd: est. 0.00-0.00` even for a model this binary actually knows a real price for. `cmd/fathom/modelprice.go`'s `rateForModel` resolves the profile a dry run would use and looks up a real per-model rate; when known, the estimate is a genuine range; when not, `cost_usd: cost unknown: no price for model <m>` — never a fabricated or zero-shaped number. `core.CompileRun` gains `PriceKnown` (dry-run-only, not persisted).

**Fixed: `compile.go`'s and `servehttp.go`'s real-run `WithLLM` calls now actually reference `modelprice.go`'s named rate constants.** `modelprice.go`'s own doc comment claimed `knownModelInRatePerKTok`/`knownModelOutRatePerKTok` let `rateForModel` and those two call sites "read the identical number under one name instead of a second, silently driftable literal," but neither call site was ever changed — both still hardcoded their own `0.001, 0.005`. Today all three sites agreed by coincidence; a future edit to only the constants, trusting the comment, would have silently diverged the dry-run estimate from the real bill with nothing to catch it. Both call sites now pass the named constants directly; `TestRealRunWithLLMCallSitesShareNamedRateConstants` (new) lexically pins both against drift.

**A layer's very first compile no longer refuses outright just because its whole corpus exceeds `max_tokens_per_run`.** CV-41's new default layer set (`adr-rules`/`spec-bindings` activate whenever their corpus matches) could never actually start on a real repository bigger than one run's budget: the pre-flight refusal fired on the FIRST compile exactly like a re-compile, so an operator needed to already know to pass `--continue`/an override for a layer that had never compiled anything. `semantic.CompileInput.NoPriorRun` (set by `pkg/client.CompileLayer` from the store's own `compile_runs` history) skips the pre-flight refusal only when no compile_run exists yet for that (service, layer) — the run makes real progress and stops as `budget_stopped` instead, resumable with `--continue` exactly as today; a genuine re-compile still refuses at pre-flight.

**CV-45 and CV-46 proven; CV-M1/CV-M2/CV-M6/CV-M7/CV-M8/CV-M9/CV-M10/CV-M11 measured.** `docs/plans/refactor-conservation.md`: ADR-043's kill rule and the CLAUDE.md refactor-routing stanza both already matched their lexical contracts and are flipped `[x]`. New reading `docs/bench/readings/cv-m8-fleet-recompile-2026-09-25.md`: the fleet recompile (26 layer compiles, 12 services, 2,798 claims, $7.05 over three passes) hit both defects fixed above — a $0.00 dry-run price and a pre-flight refusal on every sizeable repository — before they were fixed. Every other `(op)` row's `skip:` text now cites its own measured reading instead of a bare "measurement: …" placeholder.

**Wave 3 integrated: docs/intent/questions.md Q23 and Q27 gain the measured evidence that was already sitting unlinked in `docs/bench/readings/`.** Q23 (test edit vs. weakening): 0/50 real commits touching guarded tests produced a true loss-kind finding, 9/50 the informational `edited` kind (`cv-m2-guarded-test-commit-replay-2026-09-24.md`), and both real i28 cells still flag (`cv-m1-guards-pilot-8-replay-2026-09-24.md`). Q27 (cross-language evidence): the file-resolved tier is now measured, not just shipped, for two languages (`l0-ts-py-2026-09-24.md`) — short of the typed-edge, three-language bar the question sets, so it stays open. Q22 gains a pointer to CV-44's evidence without answering it.

**Review Kit armed at warn on this repository (CV-44).** `.reviewkit/` (packs, config, dispositions) and a new `CODEOWNERS` arm platform-engineering's `refactoring-conservation` and `testing-certifications` packs' three requirements (`refactoring.no-undeclared-loss`, `refactoring.proof-integrity`, `testing.certified-targets`) at warn, none at deny. A real `leadsman sound` against this repository's own history confirmed the two `fathom-conserve`-backed requirements genuinely fire; `testing.certified-targets` currently reads a false pass here because review-kit's `fathom-certify` adapter guesses the service from the sounding directory's basename instead of threading `--service` the way `fathom-conserve` already does (the same O15 failure class) — see `docs/bench/readings/cv-44-reviewkit-armed-2026-09-25.md`.

**Eighteen dead `internal/analysis/*` packages retired as a refactor program (CV-M6).** `antipattern`, `apichangelog`, `churn`, `cognitive`, `crossrepocycles`, `dataflow`, `deprecation`, `doccoverage`, `endpoints`, `erraudit`, `fieldusage`, `ineffassign`, `license`, `markers`, `naming`, `schemaexport`, `sqlmap`, `structtags` had zero non-test importers anywhere in the module (their MCP tools were already pruned, SP-B1/SP-B2) — retired via `docs/plans/retire-unused-analyzers.md`, proven with `fathom verify`'s new `GoneEvidence` adapter and a clean `fathom conserve --strict`. `migrationtargets` and `packagedeps` matched the same zero-Go-importer scan but are kept: the former is claimed by an existing plan row, the latter is a literal fixture dependency of a scenariobench regression test — see `docs/bench/readings/cv-m6-dogfood-2026-09-24.md`.

**CV-M6 measured: the dogfood refactor program passes.** `docs/bench/readings/cv-m6-dogfood-2026-09-24.md`: all 18 retired-package rows proven, `fathom conserve --strict` clean on every plane, no regression across the full test suite or any other plan's gate. No plan row is flipped — CV-M6 is an `(op)` measurement row.

**`fathom verify --store` can now prove a `gone:` row.** `cliEvidence` gained `verify.GoneEvidence`: `IndexAtHead` refuses on a stale ingest or a dirty working tree (never a fabricated pass), `GoFiles`/`CorpusFiles`/`ModuleFile` read the live corpus, `FailedTypedPackages` reads the store's persisted typed-edge status, and `TypedCallers` resolves a `package:`-scoped symbol against the call graph. Before this every `gone:` row read Unknown forever, independent of how true the claim was — discovered dogfooding CV-M6 (`docs/plans/retire-unused-analyzers.md`, ADR-044).

**Docs corrected: the Review Kit `fathom-conserve` adapter's invocation always names `--service`.** `docs/design/refactor-conservation.md` §7a and `docs/use-cases/refactor-conservation.md` §6 showed `fathom conserve --json --since <base>` with no `--service`; `conserve` has no default-from-repo-directory fallback (unlike `certify gaps`/`certify manifest`/`certify status`/`certify orphans`), so every real invocation shaped like the old example would exit 2. Platform-engineering's adapter now threads its sounding's own configured Service through as `--service` (CV-44).

**A resurrection `moved` finding's text no longer contradicts itself.** Its Detail already says "no retirement was declared"; the wire-level `declared` field stays `"prior"` (Finding's only loss-exempt value, unchanged for `no-undeclared-loss`-style consumers reading the JSON), but `fathom conserve`'s text table and `review_diff`'s `conservation` dimension now render it as "no declaration needed" instead of echoing "(declared: prior)" on the same line.

**`fathom refactor plan` refuses a dirty working tree.** `max:` was seeded from the live working tree (`c.Sources()`) while `Baseline:` is the last COMMIT — an uncommitted edit at plan-creation time let the two disagree for the same pinned baseline sha, so `fathom refactor status`'s own baseline count (read from git, not disk) could contradict `max:` from the very first check. `refactor plan` now refuses outright on a dirty tree instead (CV-33).

**`fathom refactor plan` refuses rather than seeding a ratchet's `max:` at 0 from a partial corpus read.** A `c.Sources()` failure (a locked directory, a read error) used to silently fall back to zero source files, so every `gone: pattern` ratchet's `max:` read 0 even for a package the plan's own typed call graph proves has live references today — `fathom refactor status`'s very first run would then fail immediately against a baseline the plan never actually measured. `buildRefactorPlan` now returns an error naming what could not be read instead of writing a plan at all (CV-33).

**`fathom conserve --since <ref>`** — the refactor-conservation report (vision 34, ADR-044). Six self-registering planes (`integrity`, `surface`, `certificates`, `governance`, `absence`, `resurrection`) report `conserved`/`losses`/`unproven` per plane — never a score, and a missing input is always `unproven` (I44) — as `fathom.conserve/v1` JSON or a text table; `--strict` exits 1 on any `losses`. Docs: `docs/design/refactor-conservation.md`, `docs/usage.md`.

**The proof-integrity plane catches a certifying test tampered with, not just deleted.** A guarded test (named by a `// certifies:` marker, a manifest entry, an invariant's enforcement cell, or reach of a changed symbol — judged strictly AT BASE, so the same change cannot also launder its own citation) that was deleted, renamed, weakened, skipped (a `t.Skip*`/`testing.Short()` guard or a newly added `//go:build` line), or had its golden data/marker moved over base..head is a finding; an honest edit or a clean rename is informational and never fails `--strict` on its own (CV-7..12, design §1).

**Certificates bind to the commit their run exercised (O114).** `live` now requires a passing run bound to a commit (test-kit's `env.commit`/`dirty`/`race`) after which nothing the certificate reaches has changed; an unbound run is `unproven`, a changed reach since is `stale`; `task tests:attest` re-attests this repository's own suite (CV-1..6).

**Self-declaration spans the whole change range (I45).** `fathom verify --since <ref>` reports a skip/disposition/`retires:`/`surface_change:`/raised `max:` added anywhere across base..head as self-declared, and a row deleted since base too; a self-declared skip still fails the gate — generalized from O56's single-commit check (CV-10, CV-14, CV-35).

**The capability inventory and its base→head delta.** `api:`/`json:`/`route:`/`env:`/`rpc:`/`cli:`/configured-pattern (`.fathom/config.toml`'s `[[surface.pattern]]`) keys are extracted from file content at base and head, including a `json:`-tagged embedded field; a loss is excused only by a `retires:`/`surface_change:` declaration already present at base; a removed/changed `route:` key also names any other known service whose client code still calls it (CV-15..17, CV-20).

**Absence proofs and the anti-Blindness detector.** `gone:` plan rows prove an import, module, lexical pattern, or kept-but-unused symbol is actually gone (`Unknown`, never falsely proven, on a stale or partial index); a new caller of a `Deprecated:` symbol, and a deleted function body reappearing under another name, are findings — the latter only when the range (or an earlier one) actually claimed the original retired, so an ordinary undeclared rename reads informational, never a loss (CV-27/28/30/31/32).

**`fathom refactor plan <target>` / `refactor status`** writes a gate-ready plan for retiring one symbol, import, or package — a pinned baseline, ratcheted `gone:` rows scoped to today's own callers, `certified_by:` rows, a closing conserve instruction — and reports each ratchet's live count against `max:`, its count at the baseline, and drift landed on main since (CV-33/34).

**`fathom report evidence`** gives, per service, typed edge share, edge tier per language, certificate density, inventory counts, and whether a sampled receipt re-derives — ranking no service or language by tooling maturity (CV-39).

**The pre-push hook and `review`/`review_diff` carry conservation too.** `lefthook.yml`'s `refactor-conserve` step runs `fathom conserve --since` as a warning on every push (CV-47); `fathom review --since`/`review_diff`'s `conservation` dimension is now actually built from the same planes instead of left unset.

**`fathom ingest -scip` resolves real scip-typescript/scip-python output (O115).** `internal/scip.ImportIndex` maps a definition by position against the service's own already-extracted symbol table instead of `DisplayName`, which every real producer omits — `find_callers`/`references`/`blast_radius` now resolve SCIP-imported TypeScript/JavaScript/Python edges at all.

**Kill criteria measured: CV-M1/CV-M2/CV-M5 pass, CV-M4 fails with a named cause.** CV-M1 (guards-pilot-8 replay) and CV-M2 (50-commit replay, 0% false flags against a 10% bar) both pass. CV-M4 fires this ADR's own revisit trigger: the Go surface delta is a textual diff, not type-identity-aware, measured 0.615 precision / 0.941 recall against a 0.95/0.95 `apidiff` bar. CV-M5 first found the resurrection detector over-firing on a genuine, unseeded large removal (24 false findings replaying ADR-025's DuckDB removal); now passes 2/2 after the fix below. No plan row is flipped — all four are `(op)` measurement rows. Readings: `docs/bench/readings/cv-m1-guards-pilot-8-replay-2026-09-24.md`, `cv-m2-guarded-test-commit-replay-2026-09-24.md`, `cv-m4-apidiff-calibration-2026-09-24.md`, `cv-m5-blindness-replay-2026-09-24.md`.

**The resurrection detector no longer flags a long-lived, independently-written twin as a resurrection (CV-M5).** `conserve.Resurrected` matched a deleted base function against ANY function in a changed file at head sharing its body hash, whether or not that MATCHING head function was itself touched by the change — the genuine ADR-025 DuckDB removal produced 24 false `body_resurrected` findings this way, because `DuckStore` and `SQLiteStore` had implemented the same `Store` interface with convergent CRUD bodies for years before one twin was deleted. A head function is now a resurrection candidate only when it is itself new in the range or its body hash changed in the range; both migrations (ADR-025, ADR-041) now replay clean on their genuine ranges while a seeded copy in either still flags, as designed.

**Fixed: `fathom conserve`'s default plan discovery — its only real invocation — no longer loses every `retires:`/`surface_change:` declaration (O117).** `discoverPlanPaths` handed `internal/conserve.NewInput` absolute `docs/plans/*.md` paths (`verifyRepoRoot()` is always absolute) where its git-based reader (`gitShowFile`, `worktreeFile`) expects repo-relative ones — `git show <ref>:<abs path>` always reports the path absent, and `filepath.Join(root, <abs path>)` doubles the path into one that never exists — so a declaration was silently invisible at both base and head whenever `--plan` was not passed explicitly, the only way `lefthook.yml`'s pre-push step (CV-47) invokes it. `NewInput` now relativizes an absolute plan path against `root` itself (`planPathRelativeTo`), fixing every caller (the CLI's own discovery, `review_diff`'s) in one place. A `retires:` row declared at base is now honored with no `--plan` flag: a resurrected body with a claimed retirement reports `body_resurrected` (a loss), not `moved`.

**`store.PutTypedEdges`-driven SCIP re-imports no longer leave a package's stale edges standing once it correctly drops to zero (O116).** `internal/scip.ImportIndex` only ever listed a package in its result when at least one edge landed in it, so a package whose corrected mapping now yields zero edges was never even passed to `store.PutTypedEdges` — its previous, wrong rows survived indefinitely. `Import.Packages` now lists every package with at least one mapped definition, edges or not, so a re-import always clears a package that dropped to zero.

**The decision port's `route` site fails its own calibration check (CV-M11, ADR-042 amended) — no threshold fixes it.** Measured 45.7% right against the 0.9-right-nine-in-ten bar, non-monotonic in confidence (the highest-confidence slice scored worse); the prompt hook's `/route` line is now hedged at every confidence instead of phrased as firm, and `[decide] sites` gates which of the four sites may send a question off-host at all, defaulting to unchanged behavior (ADR-044 decision 8, I14).

**Rounding out stage 5/6: default layers, re-anchoring, and two eval gates cleared.** `fathom compile` with no `--layer` now compiles `obligations` plus `adr-rules`/`spec-bindings` whenever their own corpus matches a file (CV-41); a citation whose lines moved re-anchors by its own anchor text instead of going stale (CV-40); the `findings` layer clears its eval gate at 1.00 precision/recall and `obligations`' production-model recall rechecks at 0.86 (CV-M9/CV-M10); the language tracker records the file-resolved tier as shipped (CV-37/SU-11).

**The edit-hook payload ceiling is the measured one: 320 tokens (Q21, ADR-016 amended).** The 150-token ceiling was missed on 47% of post-edit and 83% of pre-edit payloads; 320 is the measured post-edit p90. It gates only what the hook appends after the rules (spec lines, memories, findings), so payloads in the 150–320 band now carry content that was silently skipped; the rules block keeps its 6-rule, 28-word caps. Choosing the governing rule stays open (`docs/plans/verbs.md` VB-40).

**`fleet reconcile`'s drift report and `--rebaseline` now cover hook entries, not just MCP/skills (PC-28).** A PROJECT-scope hook row in `.claude/settings.json` that changed outside fathom's apply path (e.g. a hand or committed edit) was silently invisible to `fleet reconcile`'s dry run and to `--apply --rebaseline` — the documented remedy for a `wiring_current`/`wiring_drift` hook drift could never clear it, leaving `.fathom/owned.json` permanently stale — even though the same drift was already detected and reported by `fathom config audit` and `fathom doctor`. The dry run now lists hook drift the same way it lists MCP drift (`DRIFT: hook ...`), and `--apply --rebaseline` re-records the drifted hook entries' ledger digests (`internal/wiring.RecordKeyBaselines`), never rewriting the entries themselves.

**Truncated MCP answers point at refinements that work.** Five tools' elision lines named parameters they do not take (`symbol_context` "depth", `outline` "depth or kinds", `repo_map` "package", `staleness_report` "limit"); each now names a real refinement — `find_callers` with `depth`, `read_source` with a line range, `budget`, a package-qualified symbol, or `read_source`/`outline` on the doubted file.

**Test certifications, specified and built through stage 3 (vision 33, ADR-043).** The design, decision record and gate-ready plan for a deterministic `certifications` claim layer over test-kit declarations and attested runs — `fathom verify certified_by`, a `certifications` review dimension, `fathom certify status|gaps|manifest|orphans|suggest`, a `certification` receipt kind — with the vocabulary owned by test-kit (ADR-0042 amendment) and adequacy judged by Review Kit. Stage 0 (facts only, TC-1..5) ships in this entry: test-kit's `manifest.cue`/`result.cue` schema mirrored under `schema/testkit/` (drift-gated, `task testkit-schema-check`); additive store tables `test_suites`/`test_runs`/`test_cases`/`certify_declarations`; `fathom tests ingest --manifest <dir> --results <file|dir>` loads declared `tests/*.cue` manifests and attested `#TestResult` envelopes (plain, NDJSON, or DSSE/in-toto-wrapped), joins each case to a `Test*` symbol by exact name, and scans `// certifies: <kind> <id> [(<strength>)]` markers above `Test*` functions; `fathom tests status` reports class/tier/last-run-verdict and the join rate; `[tests] results_dir`/`artifacts_dir` in `.fathom/config.toml` make a plain `fathom ingest` pick up envelopes too, without disturbing suites a separate manifest ingest declared. TC-M1 measured the join rate on test-kit's `examples/runnable` (100%) and fathom's own `./internal/...` (94.76%) — both clear the 80% stage-0 bar; `docs/bench/readings/test-certifications-stage0.md`. Stage 1 (TC-6..14) ships too: the `certifications` layer compiles one claim per (target, test) from declarations × joins with the tier/state ladders; `fathom verify` accepts `certified_by: <Test>`; `review_diff`/`fathom review` gain the `certifications` dimension; `fathom certify status|gaps|manifest` list every target with no live certificate (JSON versioned `fathom.certify.gaps/v1` for the Review Kit adapter) and regenerate the certifications artifact; receipts gain kind `certification` (`fathom receipt verify` re-derives reach with go/types, `pass: unverified (no run)` unless `--run-tests`); `fathom doctor` reports a `certifications` row. fathom's own repo now declares certificates for its deny-severity invariants and open incidents' named tests (TC-15, `tests/fathom.cue` and `// certifies:` markers). Stage 2/3 (TC-19..24): an incident's own `Outcome: certificate <Test>` line can close a gap without a marker; `certify gaps --kind port` finds every exported interface with an indexed implementation and no `conformance`-strength certificate, evidenced typed > assertion > method_set; a flake in the last three attested runs now drops a certificate's tier one step instead of reporting `verified`; `fathom certify orphans` lists `Test*` symbols that certify nothing, reach no symbol changed in the last 180 days, and never failed or flaked in any ingested run — a list, never an action; `fathom tests select` now ranks a test that already certifies a target whose scope intersects the diff ahead of the existing reach ranking (advisory only); `fathom certify suggest` asks the decision port (`[decide] provider = llm|typesafe`) one batched request per chunk of unregistered `Test*` symbols — a strength choice and an "asserts a real behaviour" noul — and writes proceed-band answers to `workspace/artifacts/certify-suggestions.jsonl`, rendered as a hint in `certify gaps`'s table output; a suggestion is never a claim and is never written to the store, and `provider = none` prints one line and exits 0 — measured live against the typesafe (Jev) adapter: 60 candidates, 43 proceed-band suggestions, 0 errors, 1.29s wall-clock (`docs/bench/readings/test-certifications-suggest.md`). What's now observable: `fathom certify status|gaps|manifest --check|orphans|suggest`, `fathom doctor`'s `certifications` row, `review_diff`'s `certifications` dimension, and `docs/certifications.md` (regenerated manifest) all report fathom's own live/gap counts against its own invariants and incidents — the layer certifies itself.

**The daemon's logging, fleet cost and doctor correctness are fixed in one sweep (O112, DG-1..DG-21).** Every daemon diagnostic line now goes through a leveled, timestamped `internal/dlog` logger (`FATHOM_LOG_LEVEL`) with per-key dedupe, instead of an unattributable, undeduped `fmt.Fprintf` stream that let one stale-service condition alone reach 97%+ of the error log; the launchd-piped `fathom-http.log`/`.err` are bounded (64MB, one prior generation) and every HTTP response now carries `X-Fathom-Request-Id`. `list_services`'s per-service unsupported-language/root-missing check is memoized per (service, store watermark) instead of re-walking the filesystem on every call (measured live at p95 4.6s/max 182s across ~31k calls) — a vanished corpus root now reports `"root_missing": true` plus a remedy naming the cleanup command, never a bare re-walk error. `GET /metrics` gains live resident-memory gauges (`process_resident_memory_bytes`/`process_phys_footprint_bytes` on darwin via `proc_pid_rusage`, `process_resident_memory_bytes` on linux via `/proc/self/status`, an honestly-named `process_max_resident_memory_bytes` peak) instead of mislabeling `getrusage`'s peak Maxrss as current usage; startup now retries an `EADDRINUSE` bind with backoff for up to 10s before failing, absorbing a self-restart/redeploy race. Typed-edge diagnosability moved from stderr-only to structured, restart-durable state: a loading failure names the failing package(s), and discovery gaps / out-of-scope edge drops are carried on `TypedEdgeStatus` (`list_services`'/`staleness_report`'s `typed_edges` block, `doctor`'s row). Post-edit identity resolution no longer lets a later unverified basename guess overwrite an earlier daemon-verified identity, and now serves a bounded-stale cached identity (capped 24h) on a genuine round-trip failure instead of falling back to an unverified guess (closes O15's failure class, alongside `fleetWiringNotes` now checking user-scope hook wiring before reporting an absence). `doctor`'s `binary_fresh` and `unproven_claims` rows name which side of HEAD is behind and carry the evidence timestamp they already computed. A selector-method call (value or pointer receiver) and an interface-dispatch call are regression-locked as correctly typed-confirmed (O39/O40), narrowing but not closing O39's remaining lead. Docs: `docs/guides/fleet-index.md`, `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/mcp-tools.md`, `.fathom/observations.md` O112, `docs/plans/diagnostics-sweep.md`.

**The daemon's memory is observable and returns freed heap to the OS on a schedule (O108).** *Correction (INC-23, INC-24):* an earlier draft of this entry said the memory was "bounded at the peaks". It was not. That claim rested on `heap_sys`, which never falls, and on sequential drivers, and on 2026-09-26 the daemon took the machine at about 29 GB (INC-23). The real bound is the responsible-tenant program (ADR-047): the budget and ladder, cost-classed admission, and the cost and stampede tests. The footprint instrument is `phys_footprint` (`fathom resources`), not `heap_sys`, and `memory_limit` is a GC hint, not protection. `GET /metrics` gains `go_memstats_heap_inuse_bytes`/`_heap_sys_bytes`/`_sys_bytes`, `go_goroutines`, `process_uptime_seconds`, and per-subsystem gauges (`fathom_graph_memo_entries`/`_hits_total`/`_misses_total`, `fathom_open_stores`, `fathom_autorefresh_total`, `fathom_typed_pass_total`); `net/http/pprof` is mounted at `/debug/pprof/*` (loopback-only, same trust boundary as every other route); `fathom doctor` gains `daemon_footprint`, WARNing above a 2 GiB heap-in-use budget. Six synthetic drivers (docs/plans/daemon-footprint.md) reproduced the daemon's operating conditions — sequential and concurrent call-graph-memo churn well past its cap, thousands of pure tool calls, repeated real typed-edge re-ingests — at a scale beyond the 2026-09-22 incident's own rate; none reproduced unbounded growth, ruling out the graph memo, the typed pass, store handles, the call logger and goroutines as the cause. The daemon now calls `debug.FreeOSMemory` on a fixed cadence (default 15 min, `FATHOM_SCAVENGE_INTERVAL` to tune, `FATHOM_SCAVENGE=off` to disable) instead of relying on Go's own lazy scavenger — a mitigation aimed at the one signature every driver reproduced (`heap_sys` never decreasing after a peak), not a located leak. A 2026-09-23 production reading reframed root cause as idle heap left behind by PEAKS (typed-edge passes over large services, reclaimed lazily by darwin) rather than a live leak, and confirmed the scavenger closes the OS-visible gap (`top -stats mem,cmprs`, not `ps rss`, is the right instrument — `ps` counts `MADV_FREE`'d pages as resident). Two more bounds now target the peaks themselves: a process-wide semaphore serializes the typed-edge pass (`fathom_typed_pass_inflight` gauge, `fathom_typed_pass_waited_total` counter on `/metrics`), and `[daemon] memory_limit` (Go size syntax, default `"3GiB"`, `FATHOM_MEMORY_LIMIT` override, `"off"` to disable) applies a SOFT ceiling with `runtime.SetMemoryLimit` at startup (`go_memory_limit_bytes` on `/metrics`; named in doctor's `daemon_footprint` reason). Docs: `.fathom/observations.md` O108, `docs/plans/daemon-footprint.md`, `docs/configuration.md`.

**The agent-surface `tools/list` schema tax is cut 62.7% (O101, TD-2).** Bench evidence (lookups-ts/-py) measured a median 15,219 cache-read tokens/turn on the wired arm vs 5,165 for grep — a ~10k-token fixed tax paid every turn, traced to the 48,341-byte `tools/list` payload served on `POST /rpc/agent`. `outputSchema` (RP-11, 18,827 B, 39% of the payload) is now off `tools/list` by default — still generated from the Go result-type registry, still returned as `structuredContent` on every `tools/call`, and still requestable on `tools/list` via `?contracts=true` on the HTTP surface (`internal/mcp`'s `WithContracts`) for a client that validates envelopes. Every agent-surface tool `Description` is capped at 260 bytes and every `inputSchema` property description at 120 bytes, flat (no more 700/1000-byte tiers); alias/history prose moved to `docs/mcp-tools.md` where it was not already documented there (the aliases themselves are unchanged). Measured: 48,341 → 18,008 bytes. Docs: `docs/mcp-tools.md`.

**Bug reports route to `localize` (O102).** The SessionStart brief and the stanza's opening moves now send a bug report, stack trace, error string or failing test to `localize` first — one bounded, logged walk of the index before any grep; the first LCA round showed the verb was never reached for from an issue-shaped prompt.

**The decision port, off by default (vision 32, ADR-042).** `[decide] provider = none | llm | typesafe` in `.fathom/config.toml`; with `none` nothing is called and nothing changes. With `llm`, the daemon asks one batched typed question per site — is this guide section a binding rule (the compile pre-filter, skipped sections counted, disclosed and still served raw), does this rule govern this edit (obligations re-ranked inside a governing-scope tier only; envelope `ranked_by`, per-rule `relevance`), which verb does this prompt want (`POST /route`; the prompt hook injects one firm route line at the proceed band, one hedged line at confirm, nothing below that or on any failure — the port never asserts a code fact, only the prompt's shape). Every answered decision is logged to `decisions.jsonl` beside the configured call log; a doctor row names the provider. `typesafe` (2026-09-22, ADR-042 amendment) routes the same three question sites through the kit's TypeSafe (Jev) adapter directly — `endpoint`/`model`/`api_key_env`/`api_key_cmd` configure it, the key resolved at call time (cmd wins over env, unset defers to `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`), never a literal in config; both `llm` and `typesafe` fail open to `none` with one warn line on a missing key, an unreachable endpoint, or any other open failure (I14), and `fathom doctor`'s `decision_provider` row names what failed; the same `typesafe` adapter also reaches the real Jev 1.13 model through OpenRouter with no direct TypeSafe key (`endpoint = "https://openrouter.ai/api"`, `model = "typesafe/jev-1.13"`), first measured live in O110 — routing and the obligations re-rank work over that route, the compile pre-filter does not yet (unbounded batch size, not an OpenRouter limitation). **`rank_within_tier` now defaults to `false`** (2026-09-23, ADR-042 amendment): a decision-port A/B (`docs/bench/readings/decision-port-ab-2026-09-23.md`) measured the obligations re-rank net negative on governed edits once a real provider is configured (guards-jev-r1, paired mean -0.19, 3 tasks worse/0 better), so anyone with `provider` already set to `llm`/`typesafe` picks up this behavior change on upgrade unless they set `rank_within_tier = true` explicitly; routing and the compile pre-filter are unaffected. Docs: `docs/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/configuration.md`.

**A refreshed file reaches the next graph query (SU-4, O99).** POST /refresh-file now patches the daemon's memoized syntax+typed call graph for the file's package in place (the memo is keyed by a watermark a single-file re-ingest never moves, so it kept serving the pre-refresh graph) and reports `graph_patched`; the resolved-only graph rebuilds on its next use.

**The push gate verifies the tree it pushes (INC-21).** The pre-push `verify` step ingests HEAD into a scratch store under `workspace/tmp` and proves every claimed plan row against that index, instead of against the daemon's index of the operator's checkout; a failed scratch ingest is a named fail-open line. Running it hermetically exposed that `fathom verify --store` walked a syntax-only call graph while the daemon's `verify_plan` walked syntax plus typed edges, so rows whose reachability crosses a method value, an interface dispatch, or a bare call on a receiver sharing a method name with another type verified on one surface and failed on the other (O40, O98). One graph now: the CLI verify and plans-context paths add the typed edges, and `reachable`, `test_impact`, `dead_code` and `find_callees` walk the same watermark-cached graph `verify_plan` and the nav tools use. A bare `symbol:` that collides with a Markdown heading of the same name is resolved to its code definition rather than handed to the graph as an ambiguous name. `task push` now sends the pack over smart HTTP derived from the push remote (INC-22, I41): git opens the transport before the pre-push hook, and an ssh session does not survive this repository's five-to-nine-minute gate. Both push-gate scripts moved upstream into agentic-kit's fathom-pack (operating.md §5, kit 89fca073f70b) and are consumed from the vendored kit; the pin moved with them.

**Claims gain bi-temporal validity (ADR-041 decision 7, SU-19).** `semantic_claims`
gains additive `valid_from`/`valid_to`/`asserted_by` columns (`claim_events` gains
`asserted_by` too), stamped at mint and closed at every supersede/refute path;
`semantic.ObligationsAsOf` and the `obligations` MCP tool's new `as_of` argument (RFC3339
timestamp or commit sha) answer "what governed this file AT that instant" — including a
claim since superseded or refuted, if `as_of` predates that event — labeled `as_of` in
the response. `fathom findings near --as-of` mirrors it on the CLI. Default (`as_of`
omitted) serving is byte-identical to before this row. Docs: `docs/mcp-tools.md`,
`docs/layers.md`, `docs/usage.md`.

**Claims gain an explicit supersedes chain (ADR-041 decision 7, SU-20).**
`semantic.WriteSupersedes` appends a `supersedes` claim link (never `PutClaims`'
wholesale link-set replace — the new `store.Store.AppendClaimLink` appends one row in
place) from a successor claim to the predecessor it replaces; `semantic.SupersessionChain`
walks it back through however many times a subject has been re-superseded.
Wired at native review reconciliation (`fathom review` and the `review_diff` MCP tool),
via `review.SupersedeSuccessor`'s reverse lookup of this run's OWN re-emitted finding for
the predecessor's subject — the genuine successor case, as opposed to a finding that
simply stopped firing (fixed, no replacement), which still supersedes but links to
nothing. External closure/revocation and `fathom claim refute` close `valid_to` (closing
a gap left in SU-19) but never call `WriteSupersedes`: both retire a claim in place with
no distinct successor row to link to.

**Claims gain symbol-bound anchors (ADR-041 decision 7, SU-21).** When a claim's
citation resolves to exactly one symbol definition in the index, it also gets an
`anchors` claim link (`internal/semantic.AnchorSymbol`, wired into the compiler's own
citation step) — the symbol's `core.SymbolKey` plus its own current content
fingerprint. `Sweep` reads it as a SECOND healing path, only when the existing
literal-text re-anchor (T5-1/O13) is unavailable or fails: the anchored symbol's
CURRENT body hash must still match the one stored at anchor time — proof of a pure
line-range move, never a content edit passed off as one — before the citation is
re-pointed at its new range instead of flipping stale. `rename_impact` gains
`claims_anchored`/`claims_anchored_ids`, so a rename is never silent for governance.

**Claims record who asserted them (ADR-041 decision 7, SU-22).** `core.AssertedBy(kind,
id)` builds the canonical `"kind:id"` form every writer now records at mint: a compile
run's own `run.RunID` (`"compile:<run_id>"`), a review run's `--idempotency-key` or its
corpus watermark (`"review:<key-or-watermark>"`), an import's `producer@version`
(`"import:<producer>@<version>"`), and `fathom claim add`'s harness hook session id
(`"claim-add:<session>"`) via a new `AddClaimRequest.SessionID`. Served as `asserted_by`
on each obligations rule, omitted when empty.

**Vectors removed, not parked (ADR-041 decision 2, SU-8).** `internal/vector`, the
query engine's `WithVector` option, the `SymbolIndexer` interface, `hybridRank`'s
vector branch, and `core.Query.SemanticWt` (its only reader) are deleted, along with
fathom's own import of agentic-kit's embedding client (`embedding-client`,
`vector-store`) — the keyword+tier ranking (`scoreAndRank`) is unchanged, pinned by
the existing tests plus a new ranking-order golden. Not breaking: `WithVector` had no
production caller (`internal/modulehost/federated.go` always called `query.New` bare)
and `SemanticWt` did nothing outside the deleted path. `TestQueryEngineHasNoVectorOption`
refuses any future `*Vector*`/`*Embedding*` declaration in the query engine by AST scan;
revisit only through vision 31 §5's kill criterion. Docs scrubbed so "semantic" means
compiled claims, never vectors: `docs/architecture.md` (Hybrid semantic search section
rewritten), `docs/design/scope-and-purge.md`, `docs/design/semantic-layers.md`,
`docs/intent/futures.md` (F13 superseded). Evidence: `docs/vision/31-substrate.md`.

**`localize` walks the graph (ADR-041 D4, SU-14).** Every seed `localize` resolves now
expands through the typed/heuristic call graph (`expandSeedsByGraph`) to depth 2, ranked
by seed evidence, graph distance, resolved-over-heuristic, then how many distinct seeds
converge on a candidate; when seed extraction finds nothing at all, seeds are reformulated
from identifier splitting, quoted literals and file basenames (never a model call).
Candidates cap at 25 (`candidates_total` plus the standard elision block) and each
candidate's `why` caps at 3 citations, closing the 882,577-byte single payload lca-run-1
hit. Deterministic on identical input. The LCA corpus's `max_turns` (30) is confirmed
current; the Kotlin near-empty-index question from lca-run-1 is recorded as an open note
on the corpus rather than silently dropped.

**Typed edges beyond Go by import (ADR-041 decision 5, SU-9/SU-12/SU-13).** `fathom
ingest --scip PATH` (repeatable, or auto-detected `index.scip` / `.fathom/scip/*.scip`)
imports an external indexer's SCIP output as `typed_edges` — `internal/scip.ImportIndex`
maps definition/reference occurrences using the same package/qualifier convention the
language's own extractor already uses, replaced per fathom package; the ingest summary
names the producer (e.g. `via scip-typescript 0.3.16`). `indexbench scip-oracle`
(`task bench-l0-scip`) is the free-tier L0 oracle: precision/recall/unresolved-rate for
fathom's edges against a SCIP truth, per language and edge kind. `docs/languages.md` is
now kept true by `TestLanguagesTrackerMatchesRegistry` (`internal/connector`), and
`fathom capabilities --json` gains `typed_edge_producers` (native/scip:\<tool\>/file/
syntax per language). No new module dependency: SCIP's wire types come from the
already-vendored `third_party/scip` bindings `fathom export scip` uses.

**Language lookups corpora and push gate (ADR-041 decisions 9, 10; SU-23, SU-24, SU-26).**
`testdata/scenariobench/lookups-ts.json` (nest) and `lookups-py.json` (fastapi) mirror
lookups-vs.json's task classes on non-Go calibration subjects, committed and not run;
`TestLanguageLookupsCorporaParse` checks arms, criterion, and that no prompt names its
own gold file. Every committed corpus (and the LCA external corpus) now passes a pure
`PlanCells` enumeration (`TestAllCorporaPlan`); `docs/bench/README.md` gains a "Next
round" section ordering guards-fathom, findings-loop, lookups-vs, lookups-ts,
lookups-py, and LCA with cell counts and ledger-derived cost estimates. `task push`
refuses to push (`scripts/push-check.sh`, exit 3) unless the lefthook pre-push hook is
present, closing INC-20 (invariant I40).

**Findings-loop learning telemetry (vision 31 §3 D8, ADR-041 decision 8, plan `docs/plans/substrate.md` §D).** `findings candidates` now scores each rule candidate by sites × mean CODEOWNERS-ownership weight of its member files, decayed once per disputed (refuted) member, and caps emission at a configurable cadence with the rest counted `deferred`; every candidate's drafted rule now carries a `promoted_from: <pattern id>, members: N, producers: [...]` provenance tag (`internal/analysis/findings.CandidatePolicy`/`RuleCandidatesWithPolicy`). `findings status` gains `rules_never_fired_30d` and `top_fired`, computed from the edit hook's own beat log (`internal/analysis/findings.RuleTelemetry`) — which currently-served rules have gone quiet and which fire most. New `fathom claim add --store --service --scope GLOB [--scope GLOB ...] --rule TEXT [--remedy TEXT] [--cite path#Lstart-Lend] [--json]` mints one human-authored, content-addressed claim (producer `human.review`, tier capped at sme-stated regardless of any `--tier` value, its own `human-authored/v1` reconciliation domain) served by the pre-edit hook like any finding. `fathom claim refute` gains `--dry-run`: previews `would withhold N served row(s) across N file(s): <first 5 files>` (and the equivalent `{claim_id, would_withhold, files, truncated}` JSON) by walking the claim's own scope globs against the indexed corpus — proven to write nothing.

**Typed edges beyond Go: CUE native, TypeScript/JavaScript/Python file-resolved (ADR-041 decision 5, `docs/plans/substrate.md` §B).** `EnrichCUEEdges` (SU-10, `internal/fusion`) loads every CUE package under a repo with `cue/load`, resolving same-file, cross-file and cross-package `#A: #B & {...}`/import references to typed `core.EdgeDirect` edges (an unsettled reference is `core.EdgeUnresolved`, never dropped) — wired into ingest beside the Go pass, package-granular, under its own `cue:`-prefixed store-key namespace so it can never collide with a Go package's typed-edge rows; `ingest` gains a `cue: N pkg(s) processed (…), N edge(s)` line (`--json`: `cue_edges`). `ResolveFileEdges` (SU-11, `internal/fusion`) adds a per-file import/module-scope resolver for TypeScript/JavaScript and Python when no SCIP index is present for the service, emitting the new `core.EdgeFileResolved` kind — tier pre-scan, always heuristic (`c: "h"`) in `find_callers`/`resolved_nav`/every nav consumer, never presented as typed; `ingest` reports `file_resolved: N` alongside it. `docs/languages.md`'s CUE row moves to native (done); its TypeScript/JavaScript/Python rows mark the file fallback done.

**Substrate hygiene and freshness (ADR-041 decisions 3/4, wave 1 of the substrate round).**
`EnsureIndexes` adds the secondary indexes the obligations claim query and the
find_callers/find_callees typed-edge queries actually use, applied idempotently at every
store Open. `Store.ClaimsForServe` prefilters claims by layer, served status and the edited
file's directory prefixes in the backend (SQL `WHERE` for SQLite, a Go loop for the memory
store) before `obligationsCore` scores them in Go, byte-identical to the prior full-scan
path on every existing golden. Every `tools/call` result now passes through a
dispatch-level byte budget (`budgetedHandler`) so a handler that forgets to budget itself
still cannot exceed it. `CachedCallGraph.InvalidatePackage` patches one package's nodes and
edges into a warm call graph in place instead of forcing a full rebuild (production wiring
at the typed-edge and per-file re-ingest call sites is tracked as follow-up).
`fusion.Pipeline.ReingestFile` re-extracts one file's observations/syntax edges and
fingerprint on request, leaving typed edges package-granular; the daemon's new loopback
`POST /refresh-file` route serves it. The pre-edit hook now refreshes a stale file before
answering obligations (`ensureFileFresh`) instead of only reporting staleness, and the
prompt hook ignores harness-injected system notifications (`isSystemNotification`) so their
prose can no longer be mis-read as repo paths. Evidence: `docs/plans/substrate.md` SU-1..3,
SU-5..7.

**Portable bounded build cache (ADR-039).** `FATHOM_CACHE_ROOT` now relocates the
supported Taskfile/script build caches without embedding a workstation path; configured
missing roots refuse, and `FATHOM_CACHE_MAX_MIB` bounds the regenerable Go build cache.

**App-kit adoption (shell/nav/stat/theme, CSS pipeline).** Observatory pages now render
through the vendored app-kit dashboard shell, pinned to mirror `bcfeb8e`: shared
sidebar/mobile navigation with current-page state, a focusable main landmark and skip
link, kit stat tiles for metric display, kit-native action buttons with 44px touch
targets, and the kit's theme tokens for light/dark, all inside the existing bounded
content width. `task ui:css` (`scripts/ui-css.sh`) builds the CSS from that exact
mirror with `GOWORK=off`, needs no sibling app-kit checkout, and refuses unaudited,
missing, or changed inputs; `bash scripts/ui-css.sh --check` regenerates and compares
the committed output. `appkit.adoption.json` pins the reviewed `shell.layout` metadata,
generated CSS and selected source bytes the adoption checker verifies; the routed
contribution contract in `docs/app-kit.md` keeps fathom-owned display code separate
from reusable app-kit fixes, which flow back to the kit as their own reviewed,
versioned adoption. Evidence: `docs/app-kit.md`.

**Observatory display truth.** Freshness states reserve positive language for complete
evidence, render partial coverage as an explicit lower bound with its reason, and wrap
long diagnostics instead of clipping them. Impact no longer shows an unreadable
response cost as zero or carries over a stale per-tool ratio: call-log read failures
report their exact (escaped) reason, `classifyCallLogRead` preserves the underlying
scanner-error cause and discards incomplete prefix counts, and independent schema-tax
and measured-zero/positive cost figures stay distinct. Fleet, impact, story and home
now distinguish an unavailable observation count from a partial lower bound and from a
complete measured total (including zero); story labels the fleet snapshot separately
from the week, and home still preserves unavailable call-log/catch evidence rather than
filling it in. Missing configuration and unavailable comparable data now show a named
explanation and a link to the evidence behind it, and service share keeps its full
explanation, denominator and snapshot source. `fathom capabilities` and the observatory
now count `.fathom/guard.cue` entries through the guard schema — quoted labels, compact
CUE, comments and note-less guards all report correctly — while a malformed or
unreadable guard file stays an explicit broken input rather than reading as absent.
Evidence: `docs/app-kit.md`.

- **`task stranger-check` times a first proven answer, not just build/init/doctor (RH-9, `docs/design/reach.md` §5).** After init it ingests a second, tiny fixture service (fauxtown's checkout-service) into its own scratch store and verifies a two-row fixture plan (one row proven against what was just ingested, one left open on purpose), stopping the clock on that verify's exit 0. Per-step timings (build, daemon ready, init, ingest, first answer) print on every run; `FATHOM_STRANGER_READING=1` additionally writes them to a dated reading, `docs/bench/readings/reach/stranger-<date>.md`, against the real invoking checkout — opt-in so an ordinary pre-push `task validate` leaves no new untracked file behind. Measured on this repo: first proven answer in well under two minutes (`docs/bench/readings/reach/stranger-2026-09-28.md`).
- **`fathom plugin render --client cursor` also writes `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json`, Cursor's own manifest (RH-8, `docs/design/reach.md` §4).** Its `mcpServers` field is a path string (`"./mcp.json"`) pointing at the already-rendered portable `mcp.json`, not an inline server map — the one documented structural difference from the Agent Plugins pair (cursor.com/docs/reference/plugins). Omitting `--client` writes no such file. `renderCursorPlugin` also refuses (before writing) an `mcp.json` that depends on `${PLUGIN_ROOT}`/`${PLUGIN_DATA}`, which Cursor does not expand.
- **`fathom plugin render` stamps the live default surface and its opt-in groups into the rendered tree instead of leaving them undocumented (RH-5, `docs/design/reach.md` §4).** `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`'s `metadata` gains `defaultSurface` (the `/rpc/agent` eight, from `internal/mcp.DefaultSurfaceTools`) and `surfaceGroups` (`{"extended": [...]}`, from `ExtendedSurfaceTools`) — the Agent Plugins 1.0 `plugin.json` is a closed schema with no metadata field, so both `mcp.json` and `.mcp.json` instead carry the same default-surface list in an `X-Fathom-Default-Surface` header on the `fathom` server entry. A pinned content digest over the skills, hook table and this surface/group data (`pluginTemplateDigestPin`, `TestPluginTemplateDigestPinned`) must be regenerated — and `pluginVersion` bumped in the same change — whenever a rendered template moves, so a change cannot ship silently unversioned.
- **`POST /rpc/agent` — the primary path the user-scope MCP registration points at — now serves an eight-tool floor instead of the 25-tool companion allowlist (surface reckoning SR-33, F-86, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §1.1).** The floor is `find_symbol`, `search`, `read_source`, `symbol_context`, `obligations`, `verify_plan`, `blast_radius`, `review_diff` (7,715 B served, down from 17,998 B for the same eight tools' share of the old payload). The wider set — `find_callers`, `references`, `outline`, `repo_map`, `pack_context`, `staleness_report`, `test_impact`, `localize`, `list_services`, `ingest`, `findings_near` and `status` — moves to a new opt-in surface, `POST /rpc/extended`; `POST /rpc/companion`, the pre-reckoning name, is now a compatible alias onto `/rpc/extended` rather than onto the shrunk default, so a client already pinned to `/rpc/companion` keeps its old tool set minus the reckoning's own deletions/merges instead of losing tools outright. Every call still executes regardless of surface membership (ADR-014's execute-and-flag amendment is unchanged) — only what `tools/list` advertises by default moved. Register `/rpc/extended` (or keep `/rpc/companion`) for a session that needs the wider set. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `api_diff` MCP tool is removed — the calibrated API delta stays in `conserve`'s `surface` plane (surface reckoning SR-26, F-20, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `internal/conserve/surface.go` already computes the exported-API base→head delta via `internal/analysis/inventory` (reusing the same `internal/analysis/apisurface` package `api_diff` called directly); call `review_diff` with `since` for its `conservation` dimension, or `fathom conserve --since <ref>` store-direct. Unlike the removed tool, this compares one service's own base and head rather than two independently-ingested snapshots. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `diff_impact` MCP tool is removed — it was a thin unbounded-walk alias for `blast_radius`, and `review_diff` already runs the same unbounded walk in-process for a diff/PR verdict (surface reckoning SR-25, F-16, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `blast_radius` gives the identical answer with `max_depth: 0, max_symbols: 0`; the internal `impact.DiffImpact`/`DiffImpactWithTypedEdges` functions stay — `review_diff`'s impact dimension still calls them. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `find_callees` MCP tool is removed — `symbol_context` already returns direct callees, so the two-call `find_callees`-then-`symbol_context` pattern collapses to one (surface reckoning SR-21, F-05, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `symbolnav.Callees`, its sole production caller, is removed with it. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `entrypoints` MCP tool is removed — `repo_map`'s response now carries the same detector as a header (surface reckoning SR-22, F-10, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `entrypoints`/`entrypoints_more` (capped at 10) sit alongside `repo_map`'s existing `map`/`budget` fields, omitted when the service has none; `symbolnav.Entrypoints` is unchanged, only its sole tool wrapper is gone. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `capabilities`, `server_stats` and `list_sources` MCP tools are removed — one `status` tool answers all three, byte-compatible per section (surface reckoning SR-23, F-11, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `status {section: "tools"|"daemon"|"sources", service?}` returns exactly what each retired tool returned for its section; `list_services` (37,155 calls) stays a separate tool. `status` joins `POST /rpc/extended`/`companion`'s allowlist (21 tools now) and the hosted-modules floor, but its `"sources"` section — the one carrying list_sources' arbitrary-service fleet-pivot risk — stays refused there, mirroring list_sources' own historical exclusion. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `rename_impact` and `safe_delete_check` MCP tools are removed — `blast_radius` answers both through a `kind` argument, byte-compatible per kind (surface reckoning SR-24, F-13, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `blast_radius {kind: "change"|"rename"|"delete", service, changed_files?, symbol?}`: `kind:"change"` (the default) is unaffected; `kind:"rename"` returns every call site a rename must touch; `kind:"delete"` returns the remaining referents or a proven-zero receipt. `promotedVerbs()` drops `rename_impact` (blast_radius, already promoted, now answers it); `safe_delete_check`'s `symbol_id` alias is not carried over — pass `symbol`. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The four canned MCP prompts (`investigate_incident`, `review_change`, `onboard_service`, `plan_requirement`) are removed — zero lifetime calls (surface reckoning SR-20, F-78, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** `prompts/list`/`prompts/get` stay wired and now answer an empty list / not-found; call the tools each prompt named directly (`review_diff` for `review_change`; `entrypoints`/`find_callers`/`community`/`staleness_report` for `investigate_incident`; `entrypoints`/`list_sources`/`god_object` for `onboard_service`; `entrypoints`/`complexity`/`dead_code` for `plan_requirement`). Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The MCP `brief` tool is removed — zero lifetime calls, overlapping `pack_context` (surface reckoning SR-15, F-53 refined, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** Call `find_symbol`/`query_symbols`/`pack_context` directly; the console's `/brief` page (unaffected — a separate implementation) is the surviving worker-facing brief. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `fathom narrate` and `fathom brief` CLI verbs are removed — zero lifetime calls (surface reckoning SR-14, F-53, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** The narrate story-mode capture feature (story rows, the `narrate` capability, its observatory banner) is retired outright; `fathom init` no longer auto-arms it. `renderBriefMD`/`briefTuning` survive — the console's `/brief` page is the surviving value-brief surface, including its `--out`/`--print`/`--json` uses (open `/brief` instead). Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `dep_drift` and `cross_repo_blast_radius` MCP tools are removed — zero lifetime calls (surface reckoning SR-10, F-17/F-18, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** `internal/depgraph` and `internal/analysis/deps` stay for the system graph (ADR-050, vision 15 §3's N1); the `serviceRefHopsPayload`/`fleetServiceRefHops` helpers `cross_repo_blast_radius` shared stay too — `blast_radius` and `review_diff` still reach them. For a symbol's fleet-wide reach, call `cross_repo_callers` then `blast_radius`/`find_callers` per caller service. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `stale_docs` MCP tool is removed — `staleness_report` now carries the same scan as an additive `doc_drift` section, byte-compatible per row (surface reckoning SR-28, F-56, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `staleness_report`'s response gains `doc_drift: [{file, line, ref}]` (a Markdown inline code span or link target naming a symbol or file the index no longer knows); the tool moved to the `tree` cost class, since it now runs the same whole-tree Markdown scan `stale_docs` did. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `discoveries` MCP tool is removed, and `fathom routes` is no longer its own top-level verb (surface reckoning SR-29, F-47, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** Both had 0 lifetime calls; the SubagentStart hook's push-form render (`subagentDiscoveries`) already covers the pull-form MCP tool's need, and `fathom prime --routes [package|concern]` reaches the identical `cmdRoutes` render `fathom routes` used to. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `arch_conformance`, `spec_coverage` and `spec_refs` MCP tools are removed (surface reckoning SR-27, F-35, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `review_diff`'s engine already ran the first two in-process (`runArch`/`runSpecCoverage`, `internal/analysis/review/engine.go`) — call `review_diff` over `changed_files` for either dimension; its `arch_conformance` dimension resolves rules through the project rule pack only (no inline `rules`, since the standalone tool that took them is gone). `spec_refs`' one real consumer, the edit hook's spec-line RPC (306 calls), now reads the identical `{spec_id: [{file, line}]}` map from an additive `spec_refs` section on the `obligations` answer (`obligationsSpecRefs`, given `file`) it already fetches — a bounded, streaming search (`search`'s own `searchRun`/`resolveCorpusRoot`), never a second whole-tree scan. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The `http_client_calls`, `fleet_drift`, `reachable`, `traverse` and `history` MCP tools are removed — zero lifetime calls each (surface reckoning SR-12, F-22, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** `security.HTTPClientCalls` (still called by `fathom report`), `internal/analysis/configdrift`, `callgraph.Reachable` (still called by `verify_plan`/`certify`/`conserve`/`gen-status-cue`) and `backend.ListCommits` (still called by `fathom purge history`/`fathom plans history`) are unaffected — only the tool wrappers are gone; `internal/graph`'s `Traverse`/`Edge` types lose their one caller and stay for a future consumer. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The module MCP tools (`list_modules`, `module_obligations`, `module_query`, `module_staleness`), the `POST /rpc/modules` surface and hosted-modules mode (`serve-http --modules-token-env`) are removed; the `module_query` wire contract v0 leaves the compatibility surface (surface reckoning SR-17, F-68, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3/§8, ADR-053).** The module system stays: the Expert Module v0 manifest, `schema/module`, `.fathom/modules.cue`, `internal/modspec`, `internal/modulehost`, the `fathom module` verbs, and `serve-http --module <path>`, which still mounts stores read-only so `obligations` unions in bound modules' rules. `/rpc/modules` now 404s, `--modules-token-env` is an unknown flag, and every route (including `/statusz`, which the hosted bearer gate covered) requires a loopback `Host`. Doctor's `binding_modules_mounted`/`binding_module_readiness` rows skip on a current daemon, naming the reason. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **Anchored memory is removed — the `memory_search` MCP tool, the `fathom memory` verbs (`add`/`list`/`review`/`show`), the edit hook's `🧠 fathom memory` block and `internal/memory` — 0 memories in any repository (surface reckoning SR-13, F-45, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3, ADR-053).** Continuity threads carry the hand-off. A `<repo>/.fathom/memories.jsonl` left from before is no longer read; `fathom purge` now fails, naming it, when one is present (it may quote purged content and is no longer filtered) instead of reporting a verified purge. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **The twelve legacy analyzer MCP tools are removed — `complexity`, `god_object`, `community`, `dead_code`, `cloc`, `secret_scan`, `shell_out`, `proto_conformance`, `traceability`, `evidence_summary`, `contradiction_scan` and `batch` — zero lifetime calls each (surface reckoning SR-19, F-77, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** The underlying analyses (`internal/analysis/complexity`, `godobject`, `symbolnav.Community`, `callgraph.DeadCode`, `cloc`, `security.SecretScan`/`ShellOut`, `internal/analysis/proto`, `concern.EvidenceSummary`) stay — only their own tests call them now; the complexity metric U13 reads is a separate stored column (`internal/store/conformance.go`), untouched; traceability's join logic moves to U8's evidence pack when that is built, not a tool. `batch`'s admission code (`admitBatchCall`, `batchCall`, `batchResult`, `registerBatch`) and the governor's per-sub-call class fan-out (`classWeight`, `rpcBatchClasses` in `cmd/fathom/governor_rpc.go`, the `msg.Params.Name == "batch"` branch in `lifecycleGate`'s `rpcCallInfo`) are removed with it — RT-60 (`docs/plans/responsible-tenant.md`) retires. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-surface-reckoning.md`.
- **A plan row wrapped onto indented continuation lines keeps its contract (G2-5).** When the checkbox line carries no `{…}` block, the parser reads the row's indented continuation lines (title text, spec IDs and an annotation block that may itself span lines) instead of verifying it as a positional `md-NNN` row with no contract; an annotation on an unindented line under a row is named by lint, and `thread promote --route`, verify's follow-up calls and `plan_row_checked` conditions handle a wrapped row by all its lines. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-plan-grammar-wrapped-rows-and-needs.md` (plan grammar v2's checkbox-line rule and `needs:` are on the compatibility surface).
- **Deferred is not done: a `needs:` target that is a `[-]` row no longer satisfies it, in the same plan or across plans (G2-6).** `plans next` reports the dependent blocked (`deferred`) until the target revives, and a refused target names the dependent as needing re-planning (`replan` in `--json`, "needs re-planning" in the list), so `--eligible-only` stops offering it. Migration: `docs/upgrades/0.9.0-plan-grammar-wrapped-rows-and-needs.md` (plan grammar v2's checkbox-line rule and `needs:` are on the compatibility surface).

### Added
- **Every decision `fathom claim learn --apply` persists is announced with its signals: an intent-ledger row the session attention line counts (`learning N`) and `fathom intent audit` lists (GL-14).** None is silent; a dry run writes no row.
- **`fathom claim learn [--service S] [--store DB] [--apply]` promotes and retires claims under `.fathom/learning.cue` from the rule-outcome log (GL-13).** The dry run prints each decision with its signals; `--apply` persists the lifecycle and closes a retired claim's validity window, deleting nothing, and a never-triggered retirement is held while the log does not reach back over its window.
- **The edit hook's verdict ask names the rule's claim id, and the user's one-key answer is recorded (SR-48w, F-31).** A next prompt of exactly `c` or `r` (a note may follow) is read by the prompt hook while an ask is pending, and the following edit writes it as a corroborate or refute through `fathom claim`'s own verdict core, attributed to the session; prose is never read as an answer.
- **`fathom obligations` marks each `.fathom/guard.cue` layer rule governing the path checkable or not, and `fathom obligations promote <rule> [--write]` compiles a checkable one to an `enforce: true` guard (SR-45w, F-26).** The dry run prints the stanza; `--write` adds the one line and changes no other byte; a `no_cycles` or `cross_service_direction` rule is refused with its reason. `--json` gains `layer_rules`; the MCP `obligations` tool is unchanged.
- **Exploration maps arrive by hook (XM-8, XM-11 to XM-13, ADR-056): the `SubagentStart` primer carries one map id inside RC-28's 1,200-byte cap, a prompt or edit that touches code inside a map's anchored entries gets one pointer line once per session per map, the session brief names at most three maps, and `fathom thread note "…" --map-ref ID` references maps that the pre-compact pointer and the brief carry across a resume.** Deterministic, no model, no daemon call, and nothing at all for a repository with no maps; the hook codec now exposes the sub-agent's own id (`HookInput.AgentID`), the one place fathom sees it.
- **Exploration maps, CLI only (XM-1 to XM-7, XM-9, XM-10, XM-14, ADR-056): `fathom map new|append|name|include|compose|show|list` (`thread note --map` is the same append) files anchored, content-hashed entries, composes maps by reference as a DAG (cycles refused, depth capped at 4), mints the one composite id a handoff carries (`compose --id-only` prints it alone) and reads a map with `show ID --budget 1200`, which expands the includes once, re-resolves every entry against the code at read time (fresh, moved by its symbol key, or stale with its diff size, never served as if it were true), keeps to the byte budget and names what it cut.** A named map needs a purpose line; lifetimes follow references. Maps are rows in the existing threads log that the thread verbs never serve. No MCP tool is added: the default surface stays at its eight (a test pins the list), and a map tool, when built, lands on the extended surface. `internal/explmap` is the model.
- `fathom config audit` gains the check kind `agents_md_reaches_claude` (optional `allow_import: false`): AGENTS.md exists and no root CLAUDE file switches it off, or the first one imports it; the claude-code pack's `claude_code_stanza_present` now uses it under the same id (ADR-059).
- **The test-run hook, opt-in behind `[hooks] test_run` (default off; TR-1..TR-11, ADR-056 decision 6).** Two new Claude Code hook rows `fathom init` writes (`PostToolUse(Bash)` → `fathom session-hook --event test-run`, `PostToolUseFailure(Bash)` → `--event failed-test-run`) act only on a Bash command that runs a declared test command (Taskfile, Makefile, package.json, or the new `[tests] commands`); on a failing `go test`, `go test -json` or JUnit run they inject one line naming the failing tests and the changed symbols they reach (`test_impact` over `git diff HEAD`, `localize` at 4 hops) with the full output saved under `workspace/tmp/test-run/`, and nothing on a pass or an unknown result. One row per run in the hook log (channel `test-run-hook`, no output text, `attested: false`); never rewrites output, blocks or runs a command. The Claude codec now reads `PostToolUseFailure`'s `error` key (it read `tool_error`, which Claude Code does not send, so the fathom-tool failure line never quoted the failure) and gains the capability `test-run-line`.
- **`pkg/client` exports `RankedDoc`, `DriftedDocLink` and `ErrDocIndexUnavailable`, aliases of the doc-index result types and sentinel that `SearchDocs` and `DocLinkDrift` return.** A caller outside the module can now name them; nothing else changes.
- **An ingest indexes the service's Markdown for `search kind: docs`, hash-gated per file, inside a per-service byte budget that names what it cut (KR-8, KR-9).** The corpus is what discovery returned (`.fathomignore` and purge exclusions apply), the ingest summary and `--json` `docs` block count files and name elided units and unindexable files, `fathom purge` and service remove and rename now reach `doc_units`, `docs_fts` and `doc_links`, and a plain-text doc query with a hyphen, apostrophe or slash is searched as its words instead of failing as FTS5 syntax.
- **`fathom tests select` leads with the certifying-first selection: the minimal tests that re-prove every live certificate the diff touched, `--safe` adding every test that reaches a changed symbol (TQ-15w, SR-50).** It names what it skipped and what no live test certifies, and prints the CI time saved from attested run durations (`unknown` with none), appending each known figure to `workspace/artifacts/tests-select/time-saved.jsonl` (TQ-16w); `--json` gains a `selection` object.
- **`test_impact` takes `rank: "risk"`: the service's untested, dangerous symbols ranked by uncovered share x centrality x churn x severity x incidents, each factor cited (TQ-1..TQ-5).** Ingest now stores per-symbol centrality (transitive callers over the call graph's SCC condensation, memoized per watermark) beside per-file churn, so the rank is a sort with a limit, never a graph walk; `symbol` is required only without `rank`.
- **`fathom verify --converge` reports claimed-unproven tasks, unanchored tasks and changed code no task anchors, and `--write-fixups` writes fix-up tasks only inside the spec's fathom converge block (SI-5, SI-6, ADR-049).** The unclaimed-code pass reads `git diff -U0 <base>` (default: the commit that added the spec) against the index's symbol ranges for the changed files and needs `--store`; text outside the converge markers never changes.
- **`fathom verify --plan` reads Spec Kit and OpenSpec task files in place (SI-1..SI-4, SI-9, ADR-049).** The dialect is detected by path and content shape (`--dialect` overrides); a foreign task's contract is its inline fathom annotation or the first path it names, verdicts point at the foreign file's lines (JSON gains `line`), the file is never written, and exit codes stay 0/1/2.
- **Plan grammar gains `weight:` and `accept:` (SI-7, SI-8, ADR-049).** `weight:` is a positive integer (default 1) read into `Task.Weight` for weighted rollups, and lint refuses anything else; `accept:` is quoted acceptance text that never gates by itself and, like every grammar key, makes a mangled block loud (I22).
- **Plan grammar gains a `Proof: certified` header line (SI-14, ADR-049).** Once set, every `symbol:` row in the plan must also carry `certified_by:`, or is refused `certificate-absent` through the existing `EvaluateCertifiedBy` path — with no `skip_test` escape; unset is today's tiers, unchanged.
- **`fathom verify --plan` reads Kiro's `.kiro/specs/<name>/tasks.md` in place too (SI-10, ADR-049).** Numbered checkbox rows resolve their own `_Requirements: 1.1, 2.3_` references against the spec's `requirements.md` (EARS acceptance criteria) into `accept:`, the same non-gating pattern OpenSpec's spec-delta linking uses; `--dialect kiro` selects it explicitly.
- **`fathom verify --dialect gh-tasklist --plan <file>` reads an exported GitHub issue or PR task list in place (SI-13, ADR-049).** Plain checkbox rows, positional ids, anchors from an inline annotation or the paths the task names; unlike every other dialect this one is never auto-detected (an exported body carries no trustworthy path or shape), so `--dialect` is always required.
- **`fathom verify --plan` reads Task Master's `.taskmaster/tasks/tasks.json` in place too (SI-12, ADR-049).** Tasks and subtasks (dotted ids, e.g. `1.1`), `status: "done"` as claimed; anchors come from an inline fathom annotation on the task's title, else the first path named across its `details`/`testStrategy` text (never the title itself).
- **The verify ledger writes schema 3: every reading carries a `digest` of the plan's per-row verdicts, and a `rows` array (`id`, `state`, `criterion`, `weight`) only when that digest changed since the plan's previous reading (PE-1, `docs/design/progress-and-economics.md` §3).** States are `open`, `claimed-proven`, `claimed-unproven`, `unknown` and `dispositioned`; v1 and v2 rows still parse and nothing is backfilled.
- **`fathom plans status --weighted [--json]` rolls proven weight over total weight up by fleet, project, plan and section (PE-3).** A section is the plan's `## ` heading; a row weighs its `weight:` (default 1) and a dispositioned `[-]` row weighs nothing.
- **New console page `/progress` (Proof; PE-2, PE-4..PE-12, `docs/design/progress-and-economics.md` §4): goal gauges, weighted progress, a read-only derived kanban, the frontier, a burn-up with a projected finish, proof regressions with their commit, time-to-proof, claim honesty per agent identity and a proof heatmap, filterable by service, plan, wave, goal, agent and date range.** Every per-row view projects the verify ledger's schema-3 readings (a bounded tail read, cached); with fewer than 7 days of data the projected finish is withheld and says why, and a session ledger that records no harness or model says so rather than guessing.
