- **New console page `/work` (Proof; WI-8, WI-11, WI-13, `docs/design/work-intelligence.md` §3): per repository, the model and harness scorecard (descriptive unless G2 admits a comparison), the interest rate and cost per zone with the first-attempt split, the friction map with proposed remedies, and hotspots.** Rows are agent identities only (I58); remedies are proposals, never applied; unread sources (CI minutes, hook-log search events, rework) are named, never zero.
- **`.fathom/config.toml` gains `[zones]`: zone name to repo-relative path globs, the unit work-intelligence views aggregate to (WI-1, `docs/design/work-intelligence.md` §2).** Unset, a zone is a Go package's directory or the deepest directory holding indexed symbols; a change splits evenly across its zones.
- **CSS extraction lands (`.css`, LB-Q-css): selectors (every shape — tag/class/id/universal/attribute/pseudo-class/pseudo-element/namespace/nesting and the four combinators), `@keyframes`, custom properties, and `@import` edges via the generic relative-path strategy (LB-F11).** `level_met` is `none`: combined `sym_recall`/`sym_precision` (0.05/0.13) are dominated by a verified oracle limitation — universal-ctags 6.2.1's CSS parser does not track paren nesting across picocss/pico's multi-line `:has()`/`:is()`/`:not()` selector lists and emits malformed tags; on `necolas/normalize.css` alone fathom matches 58/70 (0.83/0.83), short of L1 good only on ctags' own text-normalization quirks. Not query-fixable; `LB-Q-css` stays unchecked and `LB-Q-css-rest` records the deferred disposition and trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`, `internal/extractor/dialects/css/PROVENANCE.md`). Adding CSS as the first dialect after LB-F1's five migrated languages also closed three registry gaps every later dialect would otherwise hit: `fusion.New` had no path for a new tags-engine dialect into the real pipeline (`no-extractor` for every file, both at ingest and in the language-breadth harness); a definition's stored `Line` always took its (possibly shared) definition node's own row, wrong for CSS's grouped selector list where N names share one `rule_set`; and a new dialect had no way to declare its own tier-B ctags invocation (`internal/extractor.Dialect.Ctags`, mirrored in `schema/dialect/dialect.cue`) — the langbench harness's ctags spec lived only in the five languages' embedded `incumbents.json`.
- **`scripts/tenancy-stress.sh`, the stress harness for the operator's RT-M3/M5/M10 runs (RT-136, RT-168, design §15.2).** A scratch unmanaged daemon on a `.backup` store copy with `--lifecycle-id`, a scratch HOME and a forced budget, the §15.2 load mix, SIGKILL/relaunch cycles, `/metrics` reachability sampled every second, and a reading with the verdict first; it refuses the managed daemon's port, store and call log and a fixture on the internal disk, and `--self-check` (run by `task scripts-check`) proves it.
- **Doctor gains `hook_budget` and `disk_retention`, and the custodian's config walk is bounded (RT-134, RT-135, design §11.3, §12.1).** Every hook invocation appends its wall time and deadline to `~/.fathom/hook-latency.jsonl`; `hook_budget` judges each channel's p99 against its deadline and counts breaker-open minutes, edits served unguarded while the breaker was open and harness kills (an invocation whose `seen` beat's `hook_inv` id never reaches the latency log — a silent exit is not a kill), from the beats of the whole 24 h across both beat-log generations (a shorter retained span is named, never presented as 24 h); `disk_retention` reports `fathom gc`'s reclaimable bytes by class and the home volume's free space; `fathom resources`' hooks line now carries the p99s. The custodian's non-git walk stops at depth 6, 20,000 entries and (in a hook) 2 s before the hook's timeout — a capped walk is unable-to-check, never a clean corpus — every hook's audit (SessionStart's attention line included) refuses a directory that is not inside a repository by name — a subdirectory of a repository is read through git, not refused — and FileChanged audits are debounced across sessions per (root, file, mtime).
- **Hooks fan out less (RT-130..RT-133, RT-169, design §11.3).** `gitToplevel` is memoized and cached per cwd; the Read|Grep|Glob observer and read-only post-Bash calls exit before any git fork or daemon request, and `fathom init`, `fleet reconcile` and `plugin render` register the observer `"async": true` on Claude Code (reconcile brings an installed entry up to it) (the harness registry gains `async_hooks` and `observe_only`); the Stop hook reads the transcript from a per-session offset; `GET /identity` answers the hooks' identity lookup from the daemon's registry through one cached resolver — SessionStart's stamp and primer share a single lookup, post-commit's registered-root guard reads it too, and `list_services` is read only when nothing matched or the daemon predates the route — and `SubagentStart` reuses the parent session's resolution with zero RPCs.
- **`fathom gc` and the daemon's own disk bounds (responsible tenant: RT-125..RT-128, design §12.1–§12.2).** `fathom gc` reports reclaimable bytes by class and changes nothing without `--apply`; the daemon's call-log writer rotates at 64 MiB by renames alone and a background pass (under a lock `gc --apply` shares; gc never renames a live log) rolls each previous generation up into a gzip archive that is never deleted, a failed rotation retrying after a minute rather than on every row; console tails read the rotated generation, and every usage reader (metrics, instrument, flywheel, doctor, fleet, research, discoveries) reads every generation, archives included, so a rotation hides no history (a generation caught both archived and pending is read once, and a windowed reader such as `discoveries --since` or `metrics traffic --since` never opens a generation wholly older than its window); the daemon's stdio logs rotate on their own 60 s ticker and stream the prior generation, truncating without a copy above 256 MiB; the managed daemon truncates the WAL after 5 idle minutes, at most hourly, and the store sets `journal_size_limit` to 64 MiB. The managed daemon applies gc's daily classes (session stamps, dead daemons' records, diag captures, the `$TMPDIR` caches) itself once a day, idle and at the ladder's normal level only (RT-181); `gc --vacuum` is deferred (RT-D10).
- **Diagnostics are captured at threshold crossings, pprof is guarded, and `/metrics` carries every tenancy family (responsible tenant: RT-122..RT-124, design §13.3–§13.5).** The daemon captures `~/.fathom/daemon/<id>/diag/<ts>-<trigger>/` (heap, goroutines labelled by tool and origin, the in-flight table, `metrics.txt` with the GC CPU fraction, and a flight-recorder `trace.out` from the shed rung on) on the first crossing of 1, 2, 4 and 8 GiB of its own footprint, on refuse entry and on pressure critical — each trigger kind at most once per 10 min, the exit rung only heap and in-flight; `/debug/pprof/*` and `/debug/inflight` answer a loopback peer only (a remote caller spoofing a loopback `Host` is refused), `/debug/pprof/*` caps `seconds` at 30 and runs one profile at a time (429 otherwise); `GET /debug/inflight` lists in-flight work; `/metrics` adds `fathom_volume_free_bytes{vol}`, `fathom_oldest_inflight_seconds{class}`, `fathom_abandoned_total{tool,cause}`, `fathom_bytes_read_total{tool}`, `fathom_cache_bytes{group}`, `fathom_lifecycle_starts_total{reason}`, `fathom_memory_released_bytes` and `fathom_sqlite_memory_used_bytes` (a `#` comment while this SQLite build keeps no memory status).
- **`[tenancy]` config (schema `#Tenancy`), `/metrics` ladder gauges (`fathom_tenancy_level`, `fathom_budget_bytes`, `fathom_footprint_bytes`, `fathom_children_footprint_bytes`, `fathom_host_pressure_level`, `fathom_swap_used_bytes`, `fathom_gc_cpu_fraction`, `fathom_quarantined_services`) and `/statusz` `quarantined` (RT-30, RT-31, RT-155, RT-161).** A service quarantined at the exit mark is never auto-refreshed until an explicit `ingest` clears it.

- `fathom assess` (DO-3, IR-3; ADR-046) asks a model typed decision-port questions about one session's committed work products, or with `--interventions` about the retro rows mechanics cannot decide, and never reads its transcript. A live run needs the default-off, fail-closed `assess` capability, a `[decide]` provider and the new `assess` site from the assessed repository's config, while `--dry-run` spends nothing; each run's cost lands on `~/.fathom/assess-ledger.jsonl`, which `fathom assess spend` totals.
- Judging spend is isolated under `IR-4@2026-09-25` (I46): the session ledger gains an additive `spend_label`, and a labelled row is never an arm, thread-bet or flywheel unit. The impact model, the brief, the thread bet and the flywheel each count and name the exclusion.
- `fathom research bundle --include-retros` (IR-5) adds an optional, counts-only `retros` group to the bundle's closed allowlist, from the retro corpus under the state directory's `artifacts/retros/`. Bundles built without the flag are unchanged, JSON and Markdown alike.
- `search` — new agent-surface tool: grep with a receipt (enclosing symbol/kind, test/generated/vendored flags, ident/comment/string classification); excludes vendored and `workspace/` trees by default (RC-24, 19-tool agent surface).
- `fathom discoveries`/`discoveries` MCP tool and `fathom routes` — recent call-log pointers (symbol ids, file paths), resolved fresh against the live index, handed to subagents and the next session (RC-25..33).
- Kotlin extraction (`.kt`/`.kts`): classes/objects/interfaces, functions, properties, imports, same-file call edges.
- **npm/pnpm/yarn `package.json` extraction lands (LB-Q-package-json, doc engine, pure-go:encoding/json): package/script/bin/export entities and workspace-member/dependency/devDependency/peerDependency/optionalDependency cross-file references via the `repo-path`/`module-source` strategies — the first doc-engine (`DocExtractor`, LB-F8) dialect to register.** Measured against `npm pkg get` (tier A) on babel/babel 7.29.9 and vercel/next.js 16.3.6 (396 real package.json files): `level_met` is `none` (sym_recall .1536, sym_precision .1705) despite entity counts closely matching (2152 fathom defs vs 2390 oracle defs) — root-caused directly to `internal/langbench`'s `BareName`, the same cross-cutting primitive `LB-Q-css-rest` found, here colliding with scoped package names (`BareName("@babel/core")`="core"), colon-namespaced scripts (`BareName("codesandbox:build")`="build"), and export subpaths (`BareName("./utils")`="utils"); a reverted-before-commit experiment (`BareName` forced to identity) raised matched from 367/2390 to 1991/2390 (L1 excellent on babel alone). `xref_precision`/`xref_recall` are additionally unmeasurable for any doc-engine dialect today: `BenchExtraction` never carries `DocResult.CrossFile`, and `internal/langbench`/`cmd/indexbench` have no `FamilyXref`/`CntXref*` scoring despite the metric names existing in `reading.go`. `LB-Q-package-json` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-package-json-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`); `cmd/indexbench/langoracle.go`'s `scoreToolOracle` also had a by-value `Subject` parameter silently dropping `KindIntersection` for every tool-oracle-only dialect (no ctags support) — fixed (pointer), a plumbing bug, not a policy call.
- **HTML extraction lands (LB-Q-html, `.html`/`.htm`, tree-sitter/tree-sitter-html): `id` attributes, custom elements, forms and named form fields, `<script>`/`<style>` content extracted as injected JavaScript/CSS (LB-F6), and `src`/`href` file edges via the `relative-path` strategy — a fleet gap with no incumbent extractor before this.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on html5-boilerplate 9.0.1 and bootstrap 5.3.8's own visual-test pages: sym_recall, sym_precision and kind_agreement are all 1.0 (154/154 matched), but `level_met` is `none` — `range_agreement` is unmeasurable because ctags' HTML parser reports no "end" field for any HTML kind (verified directly), and `internal/langbench` has no NotApplicable carve-out for that the way it does for `signature_coverage`; L2/L3 are separately out of reach with no SCIP producer named for HTML. `LB-Q-html` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-html-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **CSS extraction lands (LB-Q-css, `.css`, tree-sitter/tree-sitter-css): selectors (class/id/pseudo-class/universal/attribute, one per comma-separated child), `@keyframes`, custom properties, and `@import` file edges via the `relative-path` strategy — a fleet gap with no incumbent extractor before this.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on normalize.css 2.1.3 and picocss/pico 2.1.1: `level_met` is `none` (sym_recall .041, sym_precision .102, kind_agreement 1.0) — root-caused directly to `internal/langbench`'s `BareName` treating CSS's own `.`/`#`/`:` selector-marker punctuation as a qualification separator (a convention built for `Class.method`-style names), not to a tags.scm defect; tree-sitter-css v0.25.0 (latest release) separately cannot parse the CSS Selectors Level 4 `:not(A, B, C)` multi-argument form. `LB-Q-css` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-css-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **`pyproject.toml` extraction lands (LB-Q-python-packaging, doc engine, pure-go:github.com/pelletier/go-toml/v2): PEP 621 `[project]`/`[tool.poetry]` package entities, `scripts`/`gui-scripts`/`entry-points` as route entities, `[tool.*]` sections as a generic inventory, PEP 735 `dependency-groups` targets with an `include-group` intra-file reference, and dependency/optional-dependency/dependency-group/build-system cross-file edges via `module-source` — a fleet gap with no incumbent extractor before this.** Measured against a from-scratch independent tomllib reference oracle (tier C; the queue's own named oracle) on pypa/pip 26.2.1 and fastapi/fastapi 0.141.1: `level_met` is `none` (sym_recall/sym_precision .7778, 28/36) — 8 of 36 mismatches are all one cause, confirmed directly: `internal/extractor/docdecode.go`'s `tomlLocator.lineOf` reports line 1 for a `[tool.X]` section that is never given its own bare header and is only reached through a deeper dotted header (`[tool.ruff.lint]` with no `[tool.ruff]`, and similarly for `flit`/`pytest`/`coverage`/`ty`/`typos` across both subjects), a real, common TOML idiom, not a doc.json selector gap. The PEP 735 `include-group` reference — the one intra-file entity-to-entity edge this format has — measured perfectly (ref_recall/ref_precision 1.0, 6/6, fastapi alone contributing a real 5-edge chain). `LB-Q-python-packaging` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-python-packaging-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`). `requirements*.txt`/`setup.cfg` from the same queue entry stay unclassified: the doc engine decodes only YAML/JSON/TOML/XML (design §4).
- **Terraform / OpenTofu `.tf`/`.tfvars` extraction gains resource reference edges, module source recording and `.tfvars` keys (LB-Q-terraform, native engine, `hashicorp/hcl/v2`): `hclextract.go` now emits intra-file `Calls` for resource-to-resource, `var.*`, `local.*`, `data.*` and `module.*` references (including `depends_on`) on resource/data/module/output blocks and per-attribute on `locals`, records a module's `source` (local path or registry ref) in its `Signature`, and extracts each `.tfvars` key as a `TerraformVarValue` entity.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on terraform-aws-modules/terraform-aws-vpc v6.7.3 and terraform-aws-modules/terraform-aws-eks v21.26.0: `level_met` is `L1` (`excellent`; sym_recall 1.0, sym_precision .9864) once ctags' `local` kind is excluded from the kind intersection like `provider` — verified directly that its unmatched tags are false positives from tagging a nested map/list key inside a locals block's own value as a separate local definition, not a fathom miss (every real resource/data/module/variable/output tag matched). L2 (reference recall/precision) and L3 (import_resolution/file_edge) stay unmeasured: the queue's named oracle, `terraform graph` (tier A, offline init), needs `terraform init` to resolve every subject's providers, and the AWS provider alone ships a 127 MiB compressed darwin/arm64 zip — over this run's 300 MiB disk ceiling before a subject even runs. `LB-Q-terraform` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-terraform-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A tree-sitter `c` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-c's port of the CGO `cextract.go`/`cheaderextract.go` walkers (functions, structs, unions, enums, typedefs and `#define` macros — the incumbent's four kinds kept byte-identical plus unions and macros it never emitted — `#include` file edges via `include-path`), but fusion's `.c`/`.h` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to also meet its target level (L4), which it does not.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) and a real scip-clang 0.4.0 index (tier A — the `scip-clang-arm64-darwin` release binary needs no LLVM build) on libuv v1.53.0 (a hand-built `compile_commands.json` from `CMakeLists.txt`'s own APPLE file list, since this worktree has no `cmake`; 37 TUs, 0 errored): `level_met` is `none` (L1 good, sym_recall .8612) — root-caused directly to universal-ctags tagging libuv's `TEST_IMPL`/`BENCHMARK_IMPL` test macros (507 uses) as a function named by the macro itself, which fathom's real parse correctly does not do. L2/L4 (call/typed metrics) all read 0 despite the clean SCIP index because scip-clang 0.4.0 never sets `occurrence.enclosing_range` on any definition (verified: 5,716 definition occurrences, 0 with one) — `internal/langbench.ScoreSCIP`'s call-pair attribution needs it to find a reference's enclosing definition, a foundation-level gap outside this item's own queries. L3's `import_resolution`/`file_edge` scoring is separately unwired in `indexbench lang-oracle` for any dialect; the `include-path` strategy itself is proven end-to-end by the new `internal/fusion.TestResolveFileEdges_CIncludes`. `LB-Q-c` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-c-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A tree-sitter `cpp` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-cpp's port of the CGO `cppextract.go` walker: `C++` extraction at L1 excellent (sym_recall .9523, sym_precision .9674), measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 and a scip-clang 0.4.0 index on fmtlib/fmt and nlohmann/json, but not dispatched (§11 step 2: L4 is not met).** It keeps the incumbent's kinds and adds unions, `typedef`/`using` aliases and `#define` macros; a namespace is now a container (`ns.Name`), and a manifest's `qualify.scope_sep` rewrites `Widget::render` to `Widget.render`; L2 call_precision .6771 and L4 typed_recall .0441 record `LB-Q-cpp-rest`.
- **A tree-sitter `php` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-php's port of the CGO `phpextract.go` walker: `PHP` extraction at L1 excellent (sym_recall 1, sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1), measured against a nikic/php-parser 5.7.0 syntax dump (tier B; universal-ctags writes no end line and cannot see enums) and a scip-php index on guzzle/guzzle 7.9.3 and Seldaek/monolog 3.9.0, but not dispatched (§11 step 2: L4 is not met).** It keeps the incumbent's kinds and adds a `PhpNamespace` symbol whose name is the file's `Package`, an enum's methods, a signature on every callable, and `use` imports resolved through composer.json's PSR-4 prefixes; L2 call_precision .6924 (scip-php leaves vendor-typed method calls unresolved) and L4 typed_recall (no enclosing_range) record `LB-Q-php-rest`.
- **A tree-sitter `rust` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-rust's port of the CGO `rustextract.go` walker: `Rust` extraction at L2 excellent (sym_recall .9986, sym_precision .9784, call_recall .9593, call_precision .9846), measured against a rust-analyzer 1.98.1 SCIP index (tier A) on BurntSushi/ripgrep 14.1.1 and clap-rs/clap v4.6.1, but not dispatched (§11 step 2: L4 is not met).** It keeps the incumbent's kinds, names an `impl` by its head type, adds trait methods, unions, aliases, consts, statics and `macro_rules!` definitions, and resolves `mod` and `use` through crate-mod; L3 has no `import_resolution` yet and L4 def_mapping .692 and typed_recall .5222 record `LB-Q-rust-rest`.
- **A tree-sitter `sql` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-sql's port of the regex `sqlextract.go` (tables, views, indexes, functions, custom types — the incumbent's five kinds kept byte-identical, plus table columns it never emitted and FK references as L2 `reference.call` edges the queue note asked for), but fusion's `.sql` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to meet its target level (L2), which it does not.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on lerocha/chinook-database v1.4.5 and sqitchers/sqitch v1.6.1: `level_met` is `none` (merged sym_recall .2431, sym_precision .6649) from two independent tooling gaps outside this item's own queries — DerekStride/tree-sitter-sql v0.3.11 ERRORs on T-SQL bracket-quoted `CONSTRAINT ... PRIMARY KEY CLUSTERED` clauses (and, by the same shape, Oracle/Db2-specific DDL), and universal-ctags 6.2.1 separately mistags MySQL backtick-quoted DDL almost entirely (one bogus tag for a whole file, verified directly) rather than refusing to run. Isolated to chinook-database's Sqlite-flavoured file alone (the same 11-table schema, ANSI/SQLite DDL only), this dialect measures sym_recall 1.0, sym_precision 1.0, L1 good. `CREATE PROCEDURE` has no production in this grammar version at all (an incumbent kind this port cannot currently detect, disclosed in `internal/extractor/dialects/sql/PROVENANCE.md`); a named table-level `FOREIGN KEY` constraint desyncs the parser but still recovers a usable FK edge. `LB-Q-sql` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-sql-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger — including an Opus question of whether `calibration_subjects` should be re-scoped away from these subjects' non-Postgres/non-SQLite variants (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A tree-sitter `java` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-java's port of the CGO `javaextract.go` walker (class/interface/enum/record/method+constructor/field/import definitions — the incumbent's kinds kept byte-identical, plus annotation type declarations it never emitted — same-file calls and `import`/`package` clauses via the `namespace-dir` strategy and `@scope.package`), but fusion's `.java` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to also meet its target level (L4), which it does not.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on google/guava v33.7.1 (`guava/src`) and square/retrofit 3.0.0: `level_met` is `none` — merged sym_precision .8964 misses the L1 good floor .90 by a hair (recall .9741, kind/range agreement and signature coverage all 1.0), root-caused directly, not just measured, to universal-ctags substantially undercounting LOCAL Java classes (declared inside a method body) and their methods — verified on retrofit's `RetrofitTest.java`, where ctags tags 1 class/99 methods against this dialect's real parse finding 9 classes/142 methods, every extra one hand-confirmed as a genuine local declaration. L2–L4 stay `not-attempted`: a real scip-java v0.13.1 run was attempted on both subjects, not skipped — retrofit's Gradle build pins a JDK 8 toolchain with no arm64 build available in this sandbox, and guava's Maven build compiles cleanly under JDK 21 but scip-java's injected semanticdb `javac` wrapper produces no SCIP shards for that combination. `LB-Q-java` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-java-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- `summary` field (a deterministic ≤200-char answer sentence) on nine agent-surface tools: `find_callers`, `references`, `blast_radius`, `rename_impact`, `safe_delete_check`, `test_impact`, `localize`, `search`, `obligations`.
- `#Guard`/`#LayerRule` gain `enforce: bool | *false` (ADR-038): an enforced rule denies a violating Edit/Write/MultiEdit/Bash call outright instead of only advising after the fact; escape hatch `FATHOM_ENFORCE=off` or `.fathom/config.toml`'s `[guards] enforce = false`.
- Codex verified against codex-cli 0.153.1 (hooks and MCP surface confirmed live); `fathom harness hooks --harness ID` prints paste-ready hook JSON for a harness (like Codex) that only loads a user-global hook file.
- Seven advisory hook events (`SubagentStart`, `PostToolUseFailure`, `FileChanged`, `WorktreeCreate`, `CwdChanged`, `DirectoryAdded`, `Setup`), all fail-open; `doctor hooks_coverage` reports wiring against what the detected harness offers.
- `fathom intent render --playbook` writes a root `INTENT.md` inside fathom markers, held to existence by `config audit`'s `pointers_resolve`.
- `fathom compile --max-tokens-per-run`/`--max-usd-per-run` overrides a resolved layer's budget for one compile call (a positive number or `"all"` for unlimited).
- `review_diff`/`fathom review` and `fathom tests coverage` accept one Go coverage profile per test layer (`coverage_profiles: [{layer, path}]` / repeatable `--coverage-profile [layer=]<file>`), reporting per-changed-symbol coverage broken out by layer instead of one blended number.
- `read_source` gains `max_bytes` (default 8KB); `find_symbol` on a Go interface carries `implementations`.
- **The findings loop's governed import lands** (ADR-040, `docs/plans/findings-loop.md` FL-1..FL-14): `fathom findings import --store --service --subject --producer --file [--dry-run] [--json]` (and `pkg/client.ImportFindings`, the identical code path) accepts the review kit's projection under a provisioned `[findings] subject`, deriving one content-addressed claim per finding and mapping its lifecycle onto the existing status/event vocabulary instead of inventing a new one. A producer catalog (`schema/semantic/producers.cue`) caps served tier at `min(event tier, producer cap)` and refuses a `fathom`-origin producer outright, closing the circular-proof loophole. Ordering and idempotency are enforced per claim via a new `external_findings` cursor table (additive on both backends), and native review reconciliation (`StaleReviewClaims`) and the external importer stay provably separate reconciliation domains, discriminated by `PromptVersion`.
- The findings loop (`docs/vision/30-findings-loop.md`, ADR-040): `fathom init` scaffolds a commented `[findings]` section in `.fathom/config.toml`; a new `fathom-findings` skill (import/inspect/refute) and a `fathom-onboard` step document it end to end (`docs/adoption/findings-loop.md`, `docs/configuration.md`). The `fathom findings import`/`fathom findings candidates` CLI verbs it documents are other waves' work (`docs/plans/findings-loop.md` sections A-D) and are not yet present.
- Measurement corpus `testdata/scenariobench/findings-loop.json` (FL-24, `task bench-scenario-findings`): four tasks, each a real gap this repo's own tree carries, anchored to a review-kit finding served (full arm) or withheld (control arm) — committed only, not run.
- Findings served at the point of work (ADR-040, docs/vision/30-findings-loop.md §3.4): the pre/post-edit hook's "open findings on this file" advisory block (capped at 3 findings/1,200 bytes, drifted anchors flagged, superseded/refuted never shown, post-edit gated on the edit actually touching the finding's cited lines); the prompt hook's `open findings: N across the files this prompt names`; the session brief's `open findings: N (oldest …)` (a new plain `GET /findings/summary` daemon route, never an MCP tool); the session hook's outbox sync (`[findings] outbox` in `.fathom/config.toml`, a sha256 digest cursor, one attempt per session); `fathom findings candidates [--min-sites 3] [--json]` (rule candidates derived from `DerivePatterns`, native and external findings alike, with a drafted rule sentence and its ADR-038 check family — fathom never writes the rule); `findings status` gains `candidates` and `disagreements_by_rule`.
- **The edit hook asks a one-key verdict when a served governing rule is ignored (surface reckoning SR-48, F-31, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §5).** When the SAME rule (text + citation) tops a file's obligations two resolved turns running — the edit that followed serving it did not address it — the next turn's payload leads with a `[c]orroborate`/`[r]efute` prompt naming the exact `fathom claim corroborate|refute` command that records the verdict; a different rule, a resolved file, a same-edit `--pre`/`--post` pair, or no session id all earn silence as before.
- **`fathom doctor --stranger` times install to first proven answer against G16's 5-minute bar (surface reckoning SR-53, F-51, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §5).** Opt-in and slow (up to 10 minutes): it runs `scripts/stranger-check.sh` (skipped, not failed, outside fathom's own repository) and reports pass/fail with the elapsed time against the bar.
- **The console fleet page gains a "Check wiring drift" panel, `GET /fleet?reconcile=1` (surface reckoning SR-55, F-57, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §5).** Runs the same read-only wiring analysis `fathom fleet reconcile` runs before `--apply` across every registered project and lists which ones drifted; user-triggered only (never scheduled by fathom), and applying stays a deliberate, separate CLI step (`fathom fleet reconcile --apply`) — the console never writes to a repository's config.
- **`fathom trace export --standard <id> --out <dir>` writes an evidence pack on request, and `fathom trace verify <dir>` re-verifies it byte-identically at its commit (CX-14..CX-18, `docs/design/change-and-compliance.md` §3).** The pack holds the traceability matrix (plan rows, foreign spec tasks and `accept:` criteria to code, test, certificate, attested run and decision, each cell with its live state and reading) as JSON and Markdown grouped by a repository-authored `.fathom/trace/<id>.cue` mapping (`schema/trace/schema.cue`; fathom ships no clause text), artifact digests, readings, the commit and a sha256 manifest; a difference is named by file and cell.
- **Each ingest of a repository with `.fathom/rules/archconformance.cue` records the violation count per deny rule, with the commit, the previous reading's commit and the change (CX-20, `docs/design/change-and-compliance.md` §5).** The readings live in the store's `arch_violation_trend` table (the newest 500 per service); a repository with no rule pack records none, never zero.
- **`fathom intent audit` evaluates computable revisit and promote triggers when the operator runs it (CX-21, `docs/design/change-and-compliance.md` §5).** A `Revisit when`/`Promote when` carrying `{instrument, threshold}` (`arch_violations`, `arch_violations:<rule>`, `plans_failing`) reads fired, not fired, or unevaluated with the reason, with its reading (`--store`, `--kind triggers`, `triggers` in `--json`); prose triggers are listed unevaluated and the daemon never evaluates one on its own.
- **The Intent tab shows the drift radar (CX-22).** Decisions due for review (computable triggers that fired at this audit, cited) and the archconformance rules whose ingest-time violation count rose over the newest 20 readings, with the commit ranges that added violations; no trigger, no reading or an unreadable trend is an absence or an error with its reason, never 0.
- **`fathom intent adr-rules` proposes an archconformance deny rule for each dependency statement in the decision records, cited by ADR and `file:line` (CX-19, F24).** Proposals already in `.fathom/rules/archconformance.cue` are marked; fathom never writes the rule pack, a human accepts each.
- **`search` with `kind: "docs"` takes `pkg: pkg:<ecosystem>/<name>@<version>` and answers from that exact dependency version's README and doc comments, read from the local toolchain cache and never fetched (KR-11, KR-12).** A version the index does not hold, or a scope with no version, answers `status: "not_indexed"` or `"unpinned"` with the indexed versions instead of an empty result; nothing fills the index until the dependency plane's indexer (SG-37) calls `docindex.IngestDepDocs`.
- **`fathom plans context <plan> --row <id> --store DB --preflight` prints a row's task preflight, and `fathom plans outcome` records how it went (WI-16..WI-19, `docs/design/work-intelligence.md` U6).** The difficulty profile (blast radius, governing rules by level, certificates to re-prove, zone interest, the 5 nearest proven rows' effort) and an advisory `light`/`standard`/`heavy` tier from a published rule table; `--score` also asks the decision port over the new `preflight` site (in the default `[decide] sites`; no provider, no call), and the plan-row console page shows the same preflight without asking it.
- **`FATHOM_HOME` relocates the whole `~/.fathom` (ledgers, logs, stamps, daemon records, caches), and `--ledger <path>` on `fathom verify` and `fathom intent audit` names one ledger file (PE-26..PE-28, `docs/design/progress-and-economics.md` §11, O121).** The per-file variables (`FATHOM_VERIFY_LEDGER`, `FATHOM_INTENT_LEDGER`, …) keep working and win over it; see `docs/usage.md` (*Scratch and test runs*).
- **`fathom doctor` gains a `service_roots` row: a registered service whose recorded root no longer exists is named with the `ingest` call that re-points it (EW-20, O127).** FAIL for this repository's own service, WARN for another's; an unreadable root is named unreadable, never re-pointed.
- **`ingest` gains `remap_from` (EW-19, O127).** `remap:true` alone no longer re-points a service whose recorded root is a live checkout of the same repository; the call must also name that root.
- **PowerShell (`.ps1 .psm1 .psd1`) extraction at L2 good (LB-Q-powershell, tags engine, airbus-cert/tree-sitter-powershell v0.26.5, MIT), measured against PowerShell's own parser (a tier-B syntax dump; universal-ctags 6.2.1 writes no end line and cannot see class methods) on PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0 and Pester 6.2.0.** Functions, filters and workflows (`PowerShellFunction`, the signature running through the `param()` block), classes, class methods and constructors, enums and enum members, and call edges for command invocations and member calls: L1 excellent (sym_recall .9961, sym_precision 1.0, range_agreement .9971), L2 good (call_recall .9971, call_precision 1.0). Not extracted: class properties and script/global variables (a `$`-sigilled token the tags engine cannot reduce to its bare name). Grammar limits (two consecutive `= {}` parameter defaults lose the rest of a file; `param(...)` followed by a statement on one line) and UTF-16 files (skipped by ingest's binary gate) are disclosed in `docs/bench/readings/lang/powershell.json`. `ExtractorEpoch` 30 re-extracts every already-indexed `.ps1`/`.psm1`/`.psd1`.
- **Makefile (`Makefile`, `GNUmakefile`, `*.mk`, `*.make`) extraction at L2 excellent (LB-Q-makefile, tags engine, tree-sitter-make main@70613f3d, MIT), measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B, two disclosed corrections) and GNU Make 4.4.1's own database (tier A) on jart/cosmopolitan 4.0.2, prometheus/prometheus v3.9.1 and valkey-io/valkey 9.1.2.** Targets (`MakeTarget`, `MakePatternRule`), variables (`MakeVariable`), `define` blocks (`MakeDefine`) and each rule's literal prerequisites as references: sym_recall .9811, sym_precision .9985, ref_recall .9973, ref_precision .9845; a prerequisite spelled with a variable is not a name and is not emitted.
- **GitLab CI (`.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.gitlab/ci/**`, `*.gitlab-ci.yml`) extraction at L3 good (LB-Q-gitlab-ci, doc engine), measured against `gitlab-ci-local --list-json` 4.75.1 (tier A, jobs) and a yaml.v3 walk of `needs`/`dependencies`/`extends` (tier C, references and the cross-file ones) on gitaly v19.4.1, gitlab-development-kit e769382 and gitlab-pages v19.4.1.** Jobs and hidden templates, and their edges, are indexed; a job that extends a template or needs a job declared in another file gets a file-tier edge to it (99 jobs matched both ways, 131 reference pairs and 59 cross-file edges agree). `include:local` and `trigger:include` are recorded but do not resolve yet (LB-Q-gitlab-ci-rest: a pipeline file has no root entity for them to join), and gitlab-runner is substituted out because the tool rejects its pipeline.
- **Maven POMs (`pom.xml`) extraction at L3 good (LB-Q-maven, doc engine), measured against `mvn help:effective-pom` (Maven 3.9.11, tier A, artifacts) and an `encoding/xml` walk of each POM's own parent and dependencies checked against the effective POM (tier C, references and the cross-file ones) on apache/maven 3.9.16 and apache/dubbo 3.3.6.** Each POM's artifact (named by its artifactId) and its `build/plugins` entries are indexed, with the parent and direct dependencies as references; a `<module>` (in `<modules>` or a profile) resolves to the module's POM, and a parent or dependency that names another POM of the repository gets a file-tier edge to it (131 artifacts matched both ways, 1,235 reference pairs and 842 cross-file edges agree). apache/maven is measured on its 15 reactor POMs: its other 366 `pom.xml` are test and integration-test fixtures the tool does not build (with them in scope `sym_precision` reads .04, LB-Q-maven-rest). `ExtractorEpoch` 30 re-reads every indexed `pom.xml`.
- **`Dart` (`.dart`) extraction at L2 (L1 excellent, L2 excellent), measured against scip-dart 1.7.0 (tier A; universal-ctags has no Dart parser) on eight pure-Dart packages of felangel/bloc bloc-v9.2.1 and dart-lang/http http-v1.5.0 (225 files) (LB-Q-dart, tags engine, UserNobody14/tree-sitter-dart at commit be07cf71, `dart.wasm.gz` 205,433 bytes).** Classes (every modifier, `mixin class`, `class A = B with C`), mixins, extensions and extension types, enums and their constants, typedefs, functions (top-level, external and local), methods, operators, getters and setters, constructors (an unnamed one is `Foo.Foo`), fields and top-level variables, each callable with a `Signature` and an `EndLine` and each call named by its callee; `import` directives resolve through `pubspec.yaml`'s package name and relative paths (`namespace-dir`). sym_recall and sym_precision .9995, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement 1, call_recall .9971, call_precision .9648, def_mapping .9995. L3 (file_edge_recall .108: the index has an edge for every type reference and every import through a barrel file) and L4 (typed_recall .4331: 46% of the references target a parameter or a local variable) stay open as `LB-Q-dart-rest`. A tags query may now capture `@end`, the node that ends a definition whose body is a sibling of its signature; every already-indexed `.dart` file was skipped as unsupported, so `ExtractorEpoch` is 30.
- **A tree-sitter `swift` dialect is built and measured, and refused: `.swift` stays disclosed as unsupported (LB-Q-swift-rest, design §14.2).** Measured against the Swift compiler's own parser (`swiftc -frontend -dump-parse` 6.4, tier B) on Alamofire 5.9.1 and swift-nio 2.99.0 (642 files) it reads L1 excellent and L2 good (sym_recall .9974, sym_precision 1, call_recall .9976, call_precision .9994), but 36 of those files (5.6%) parse with an error under alex-pinkus/tree-sitter-swift 35245fb (Swift 6 syntax the grammar lacks), over §14.2's 5% refusal line, so ingest does not dispatch it, `list_services` and the ingest report still name Swift files as unsupported, and the tracker claims no level for Swift. A grammar that parses the subjects under the line re-opens it.
- **Cargo manifests (`Cargo.toml`) extraction at L3 excellent (LB-Q-cargo, doc engine), measured against `cargo metadata` 1.98.1 (tier A: each workspace's packages and dependencies, and the resolved graph with `[patch]` applied) on rust-lang/cargo 0.99.0 and tokio-rs/tokio 1.53.1.** Each manifest's package (named by its `[package]` name), its `[workspace]`, its `[features]` and its explicit `[lib]`, `[[bin]]`, `[[example]]`, `[[test]]` and `[[bench]]` targets are indexed, with every dependency (`[dependencies]`, dev and build, and their `[target.*]` forms) as a reference; a workspace `members` entry resolves to the member's manifest, and a dependency that names another package of the repository gets a file-tier edge to it (39 packages and 330 reference pairs matched both ways, 94 of 95 cross-file edges agree: one registry dependency shares a name with a workspace package, one implicit member, a root package, is not listed). rust-lang/cargo is measured on its 27 workspace manifests: its other 614 are test-suite fixtures (with them in scope `sym_precision` reads .05, LB-Q-cargo-rest). `ExtractorEpoch` 30 re-reads every indexed `Cargo.toml`, which no longer goes through the generic TOML extractor.
- **MSBuild projects (`.csproj`, `.fsproj`, `.vbproj`, `.props`, `.targets`) extraction at L3 excellent (LB-Q-msbuild, doc engine), measured against `dotnet msbuild` (MSBuild 18.9.11 in .NET SDK 10.0.401, tier A: `-getItem` for what each file declares, `-preprocess` for the files an evaluation imports) on App-vNext/Polly 8.8.0 and dotnet/aspnetcore v10.0.12 (`src/Http`).** Each file is one entity named by the file (a props or targets file included), signed by its target framework, with its `<Target>`s, its `PackageReference` names as references, and a file-tier edge from a `ProjectReference` (written with `\` or `/`) to the project it names: 75 files matched both ways, 62 reference pairs and 36 of 38 cross-file edges agree (the two misses are written from `$(RepoRoot)`). Not read: solutions (`.sln`, `.slnx`), `<Import>` edges and every other `$(property)`, conditions, and the `<Reference>` items aspnetcore's build turns into project references (LB-Q-msbuild-rest). The doc engine gains `$file` (a value: the file's base name) and reads `\` as a separator in a `repo-path`. `ExtractorEpoch` 30 re-reads every indexed `.csproj`, `.fsproj`, `.vbproj`, `.props` and `.targets`.

### Changed
- **A Stop that claims nothing costs one git process and no daemon request, however many plans the repository has (O140).** The Stop hook reads the HEAD text of the plans that carry a claim through one `git cat-file --batch` under its deadline, asks the daemon only for a plan that gained a claim, and skips its retro once the deadline is spent.
- **The console's What's next (`/support`) follows G2-6: a need is met only by a claimed row.** A deferred `[-]` need blocks its item like an open one, and an item whose need is refused is listed under "needs re-planning" (`replan` in the page's JSON) instead of being offered or hidden among the blocked, and so is an item refused itself (Swift: its row is open and its `-rest` sibling refuses it); `indexbench lang-next -all` prints the same as `needs re-planning: <rows>` (`docs/design/language-breadth.md` §13.0 step 2, amended 2026-09-29).
- **`go.mod` and `go.work` extraction at L3 excellent (LB-Q-go-mod, native engine, golang.org/x/mod), measured against the go command itself (tier A: `go mod edit -json`, `go work edit -json`, `go list -e -m -json all`) on kubernetes/kubernetes v1.37.1 and cli/cli v2.99.0.** A go.mod now indexes its module, its requirements (as before) and its replaces, and a go.work its workspace and uses (no extractor read a go.work before), and a require of a module another go.mod of the repository declares, a replace to a local directory and a `use` get a file-tier edge to that go.mod (2,865 definitions and 2,582 requirement pairs matched both ways, 392 of 394 edges agree: a registry requirement that shares a path with a staged module and a replace nothing requires are the two extras); both files are language `go-mod`, not `go`, in the language tables, and `ExtractorEpoch` 30 re-reads every indexed go.mod.
- **`Lua` extraction at L2 (L1 excellent, L2 good), measured against luaparse 0.3.1 (an independent Lua parser, a tier-B syntax dump; universal-ctags writes no end line for Lua and tags text as functions) and the grammar's own upstream `tags.scm` run by py-tree-sitter (tier C) on Kong/kong 3.9.2 and nvim-telescope/telescope.nvim v0.2.2, 662 files (sym_recall 1, sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1, call_recall .9845, call_precision .9679), retiring `luaextract.go`'s CGO walker: `.lua` files now extract through the tree-sitter dialect (`lua.wasm.gz`, 64,669 bytes gzip).** `LuaFunction` stays the one kind and a declaration is still named as written (`M.add`, `Class:new`); new are nested declarations (the walker never entered a body), functions assigned to a name (`local f = function`, `M.f = function`), to a table field (`{ handler = function() end }`) and to a string key (`{ ["on-close"] = function() end }`), a `Signature` and `EndLine` on every function, and a call named by its callee's last identifier (`foo`, not `M.foo`). `ExtractorEpoch` is 30: every already-indexed `.lua` file re-extracts. Not extracted: module tables and variables, functions in a computed slot (`t[KEY] = function`), the `require` graph (L3).
- **`Kotlin` extraction at L2 (L1 excellent, L2 good), measured against a Kotlin PSI dump (kotlin-compiler-embeddable 2.3.20's own parser, tier B) on square/okhttp parent-5.4.0 and JetBrains/Exposed 1.3.0 (sym_recall .9999, sym_precision .9998, range_agreement .9947, call_recall .9999, call_precision .9996).** A symbol's `Package` is now the file's `package` clause (was its directory); member and primary-constructor `val`/`var` (`KotlinProperty`), enum entries (`KotlinEnumEntry`), type aliases (`KotlinTypeAlias`) and secondary constructors (`KotlinConstructor`) are symbols; a call is named by its callee's last identifier (`find`, not `repository.find(id).await`); and `import` statements resolve through namespace-dir. The grammar moved to fwcd/tree-sitter-kotlin commit `1852ea17` (files with parse errors 16.6% to 1.1%; `fun interface` now parses) and `ExtractorEpoch` is 30, so every indexed `.kt` re-extracts. L3 and L4 were not reached: scip-java's Kotlin index carries no occurrence ranges (`docs/bench/readings/lang/kotlin.json`).
- **`Ruby` extraction at L2 (L1 excellent, L2 good), measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) and the grammar's own upstream `tags.scm` and `locals.scm` run by py-tree-sitter (tier C) on sinatra/sinatra v4.2.1 and jekyll/jekyll v4.4.1, 316 files (sym_recall .9669, sym_precision .9775, range_agreement .9935, call_recall .8688, call_precision .9938).** Constants (`RubyConstant`, named `Outer::CONST`), `attr_accessor`/`attr_reader`/`attr_writer` names (`RubyProperty`, `Owner.name`), aliases (`RubyAlias`), `define_method` with a literal name and `Name = Class.new` are symbols, a constant reference is a call target (`Registry.instance.register` calls `Registry`, `instance` and `register`), `def obj.x` on a local is no longer a symbol, `.rake`, `.ru` and `Rakefile` extract as Ruby, and `require`, `require_relative`, `load` and `autoload` with a literal path resolve to file-tier edges for a top-level name; L3 is not met (the harness computes no `import_resolution`; LB-Q-ruby-rest), and `ExtractorEpoch` is 30.
- The in-tree agentic-kit mirror moves from `beb53a4` to kit main `f006f08` and carries the kit's FSL-1.1-ALv2 licence (it was MIT at `beb53a4`). The kit's `llmclient` and `mcp-client` changes in between are opt-in, so fathom's provider and MCP calls are unchanged, and the kit's own instruction file is `AGENTS.md` alone.
- The in-tree app-kit mirror moves to app-kit main `d34067a`, whose `appkit adoption check` accepts the router in `AGENTS.md` alone (app-kit ADR-0088), so fathom's adoption check passes with no `CLAUDE.md` (`docs/app-kit.md`).
- **The obligations layer compiles `AGENTS.md` at the root, under `.claude/` and nested, beside `CLAUDE.md`; fathom's own stanza region is no longer compiled as a rule, and a `CLAUDE.md` section byte-identical to its `AGENTS.md` twin compiles once (ADR-059).** `.claude/AGENTS.md` and `.claude/CLAUDE.md` are root guides read by one bounded stat each; `obligations` with no path, the console's guide checks, `fleet report`'s stanza note and the bench arms name `AGENTS.md` first.
- **A compile keeps the claims of a section moved to another path, re-cited to its new path and lines, with no model call (AG-26).** The section's content digest and prompt identity must match claims of exactly one subject that left the corpus, exactly one current section may carry that digest, both paths must share one scope floor and declare no scope of their own, and every citation must re-anchor by one line shift; otherwise the section compiles as before. The old claims are superseded, each carried claim links to the claim it replaces, and `fathom compile` (and `compile_layer`) report `sections_carried` and `claims_carried`, dry runs included.
- **`fathom doctor` `stanza_present` checks the stanza in `AGENTS.md` and WARNs, with the remedy, when Claude Code will not load it (ADR-059).** It FAILs on an absent `AGENTS.md`, no region or a corrupted pair; it WARNs on a root CLAUDE file without an `@AGENTS.md` import or still carrying the old stanza, a CLAUDE file in an ancestor directory, `pluginConfigs["agents-md@builtin"].options.instructionFiles` set to `claude-md` or `managed-only` in managed or user settings (never written by fathom), or `claude --version` before 2.1.277; PASS names the load path. `stanza_loaded` SKIPs by name in a repository with no root CLAUDE file, where Claude Code loads `AGENTS.md` without an InstructionsLoaded event, and the obligations probe tries `AGENTS.md` first. `--json` keeps its shape.
- **`.claude/skills` is the only skills copy; `.agents/skills` is a relative link to it, written only for `--skills` (bare), `--skills=codex` and `--skills=agents` (ADR-059).** An earlier real `.agents/skills` holding only fathom's unedited files is replaced by the link with each file in the receipt (`fleet undo` restores them), a foreign entry there blocks it by name, `fleet reconcile` never creates the link and never writes through it, and doctor `skills_installed` resolves it.
- In `fathom harness capabilities --json` and `harness list`, Claude Code reads `AGENTS.md` natively (≥2.1.277) and `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md` in the new `import` mode; `schema/harness` retires `mirror` (ADR-059).
- **The stanza `fathom init` writes no longer tells agents to call `ingest`, and its routing/opening-moves guidance names only the eight-tool default floor (surface reckoning SR-34, design §1.1).** Auto-refresh (RT-99) already queues a re-ingest on a drifted read, so the old "refresh the index via the `ingest` MCP tool at session start" instruction taught a call auto-refresh makes unnecessary; the opening-moves table's four arrow-chains (`TestStanzaNamesOnlyDefaultSurfaceTools`) now use only `find_symbol`/`search`/`read_source`/`symbol_context`/`obligations`/`verify_plan`/`blast_radius`/`review_diff` — a client registered only at `/rpc/agent` (SR-33) could not have called anything else the old table named. The wider verb set is disclosed as reachable at `/rpc/extended` (or `/rpc/companion`) by DESCRIPTION only, never by citing an extended-only tool's exact identifier — the config custodian's `stanza_current`/`stanza_tools_served` concern (CC-14) flags any backticked or bare underscore-bearing name the `/rpc/agent` registry does not serve, and the first cut of this disclosure sentence still named nine of them, so a fresh `fathom config audit` kept failing. This repo's own CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md are regenerated to the fixed template (router_thin trims to 119/120 lines). Existing wired repositories pick this up on their next `fathom init`/`fleet reconcile --apply`.
- **`fathom gen-status-cue` and `fathom gen-plan-cue` leave `fathom help`'s rendered output (surface reckoning SR-30, F-44, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** Both are internal dev/round-trip tooling (ADR-018 decision 3), not agent-facing; they stay fully dispatched and callable — the same `verb.Hidden` flag SR-16/SR-18 use — so the round-trip tests and any script calling them directly are unaffected.
- **`fathom research` and `fathom assess` leave `fathom help`'s rendered output (surface reckoning SR-16/SR-18, F-53/F-70, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §3).** Both stay fully dispatched and callable — `research` on ADR-046 decision 6's closed allowlist for the retro corpus, `assess` behind its off-by-default capability (ADR-046/ADR-053) — a `verb.Hidden` flag freezes them out of discovery, not use; scripts, hooks and docs that already call them are unaffected.
- **`fathom metrics` gains `instrument`, `flywheel`, `retro`, `rate` and `arm` as five more subverbs alongside its existing `spend`/`traffic`/`channels`/`interventions`/`series`/`rollup`/`rotate`/`impact`/`catches`/`sessions`/`load`; the five top-level verbs leave `fathom help`'s rendered output (surface reckoning SR-31, F-52, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** `cmdMetrics` stays a pure dispatcher for the new five too — each keeps its own flags, output and exit code byte-for-byte. All five verbs stay fully dispatched and callable for one release (the same `verb.Hidden` flag SR-16/SR-18/SR-30/SR-32 use), so hooks and skills that already call them directly keep working.
- **`fathom doctor` gains a `providers` row folding `fathom providers`'s own zero/near-zero-token auth probe in; `providers` and `smoke` leave `fathom help`'s rendered output (surface reckoning SR-32, F-71, `docs/design/surface-reckoning.md` §4).** A repo with no `[profiles]` configured is `skipped`, naming providers/smoke as optional, never a false FAIL; a configured profile that cannot authenticate is a `fail` naming it. `fathom smoke`'s real-token spend (CF-6, deliberately opt-in) is never run from `doctor`. Both verbs stay fully dispatched and callable for one release (the SR-16/SR-18/SR-30/SR-31 `verb.Hidden` precedent).
- **TypeScript's language-breadth reading lands (LB-Q-typescript, docs/bench/readings/lang/typescript.json): measured against scip-typescript 0.4.0 (tier A) and universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on zod v4.4.3 and nest v11.1.26, `level_met` is `none` — `sym_precision` 0.7852 misses L1 good (.90) because ctags loses TypeScript class-body scope after a multi-line signature or a decorated/generic-heavy member (reproduces on nest's `Injector` class and on this dialect's own `describe` fixture), an oracle-side gap no query change fixes.** `LB-Q-typescript` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-typescript-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (docs/plans/language-breadth.md).
- **`unsupported_languages` (ingest report, `list_services`, doctor) now names every queued language without a dialect, generated from the language-breadth queue (LB-F25): HTML, CSS, SCSS, PowerShell, R, Fortran and some 40 others join Swift, Dart and the rest.** An extension two languages share (`.fs`, `.m`, `.pl`, `.v`) is no longer disclosed as either.
- **A JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Ruby or Kotlin file that is minified (average line over 500 characters in its first 64 KiB) or carries a `@generated` / `Code generated … DO NOT EDIT` comment in its first 2 KiB is no longer extracted (LB-F10).** It is fingerprinted and counted: `skipped_generated` in the ingest report (`fathom ingest`, `--json`) and in `list_services`' `classification`; natively extracted files (a `.pb.go` stub) are still extracted and tagged generated. `ExtractorEpoch` 9 re-extracts, so such files lose their old symbols on the next ingest.
- **Tree-sitter extraction holds at most six grammar modules process-wide (LRU, each idle module closed after two minutes and at the refuse and idle rungs), and compiled grammars are charged to the cache budget, trimmed at shed and dropped at refuse (LB-F15).** Grammar blobs ship gzip-compressed and are decompressed only on first use (LB-F13).
- **The managed daemon keeps compiled grammar code in `~/.fathom/cache/wazero/<wazero version>/`, and `fathom gc` ages it out (class `wazero-cache`: another version's directory, or an entry unwritten for 30 days) (LB-F26).** Every other process, an unmanaged serve-http included, compiles in memory and writes nothing.
- **JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Ruby and Kotlin symbols carry `EndLine` and, for callables, a `Signature` (LB-F3).** The signature is the definition up to its body, whitespace collapsed, at most 200 characters; `ExtractorEpoch` 8 re-extracts those files on the next ingest.
- **`task validate` takes a machine-wide gate lease and runs niced (`nice -n 10`) with `go test -p 4` (RT-129, design §12.3).** Concurrent gates queue on `~/.fathom/locks/gate.lock` and name the holder; `FATHOM_GATE_NICE=off` runs at normal priority and `=background` under macOS's `taskpolicy -b` (not the default: it ran the gate ~3x slower and timed `cmd/fathom` out); `task validate:unleased` is the old behavior. The pre-push hook's scratch ingest and `verify --strict` runs take the same lease at the same priority.
- **`task validate` takes a machine-wide gate lease and runs at background priority with `go test -p 4` (RT-129, design §12.3).** Concurrent gates queue on `~/.fathom/locks/gate.lock` and name the holder; `task validate:unleased` is the old behavior. The pre-push hook's scratch ingest and `verify --strict` runs take the same lease at the same priority.
- JavaScript's `tags.scm` gains two assignment-based definitions it previously dropped silently — a prototype method (`Foo.prototype.bar = function(){}`) and a single-level member assignment (`app.foo = function(){}`, `exports.foo = function(){}`) — surfaced by the dialect's first measured reading against express and axios (LB-Q-javascript). The reading records `level_met: none` (sym_recall .70 vs the L1 good floor .85); the residual gap is deferred (`LB-Q-javascript-rest`, `docs/plans/language-breadth.md`), not claimed.
- Python extraction at L1 excellent (sym_recall .9732, sym_precision .9999, fastapi 0.119.1 + flask 3.1.3) after `tags.scm` gains a class-level attribute with a type annotation and a default value (`count: int = 0`) and PEP 695 `type X = ...` aliases (`PythonTypeAlias`, deliberately absent from `oracle_kinds`: universal-ctags never tags the statement) (LB-Q-python). `level_met` stops at L1: call_recall/call_precision, def_mapping/typed_recall and file_edge_recall all miss their L2-L4 floors against real scip-python 0.6.6 truth, none of it query-fixable; the deferred disposition and trigger are in `LB-Q-python-rest` (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **Dockerfile extraction at L2 excellent (LB-Q-dockerfile, tags engine, camdencheek/tree-sitter-dockerfile v0.2.0), retiring `dockerextract.go`'s line-scanner.** Stages (`DockerStage`, the incumbent's own kind, kept), ARG/ENV keys (new: `DockerfileArg`/`DockerfileEnv`) and a stage's FROM-base same-file dependency edge (a prior stage or an external image, captured unconditionally like every other tags dialect's naive call collection — a behavior change from the incumbent, which only recorded a match against a known prior stage). Measured against `github.com/moby/buildkit/frontend/dockerfile`'s own official parser (the library `docker buildx build --print=outline` itself is built on; tier A) on docker/cli, docker/compose and docker-library/python (73 files): sym_recall .9949, sym_precision .9975, ref_recall/ref_precision 1.0 — L1 and L2 both excellent. `level_met` stops at L2: L3 is needs-strategy, not query-fixable — no tags-engine import strategy maps a local image tag to the Dockerfile that built it, and `internal/langbench.recompute()` does not score `xref_precision`/`xref_recall` for any tool-format dialect yet, a foundation gap this queue item surfaced. `ExtractorEpoch` 16 re-extracts every already-indexed `Dockerfile`/`*.dockerfile`; the deferred disposition and trigger are in `LB-Q-dockerfile-rest` (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A tree-sitter `bash` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-bash's port of the CGO `bashextract.go` walker (both function syntaxes, kept `BashFunction` byte-identical; new: `export NAME=value` as `BashVariable`, same-file call collection, and `.sh`/`.bash`/shebang (`bash`, `sh`) classification, none of which the incumbent had), but fusion's `.sh`/`.bash` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to also meet its target level (L2), which it does not.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on nvm-sh/nvm v0.40.8 and bats-core/bats-core v1.14.0 (56 files): `level_met` is `L1` (`good`; sym_recall .9805, sym_precision .9837, kind_agreement 1.0, signature_coverage 1.0) — capped below excellent only because ctags' `Sh` kind reports no end line for a function, so `range_agreement` is `not_measured` (design §1.2's rule, not a query gap). L2 (`call_recall`/`call_precision`) reads `null`: the queue names only `ctags` as an oracle, no SCIP producer for Bash is known to exist, and `internal/langbench`'s `FamilyCalls` — the only source of those two metrics for a `kind: language` reading — is fed exclusively by `ScoreSCIP`; a tool-oracle's references land in `FamilyRefs` instead, which `recompute()` only scores into metrics for `kind: "tooling"`, a foundation-level gap this queue item surfaced (not this item's `tags.scm` to route around). `LB-Q-bash` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-bash-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A tree-sitter `csharp` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-csharp's port of the CGO `csharpextract.go` walker (namespace/class/interface/struct/enum/method kinds kept byte-identical; new: `CSharpProperty`, `@scope.package` so `Package` carries the namespace, and conversion operators), but fusion's `.cs` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to also meet its target level (L4), which it does not.** Measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on JamesNK/Newtonsoft.Json 13.0.4 and App-vNext/Polly 8.8.0 (1,745 files): `level_met` is `none` — sym_recall is good on both subjects (.8968, .9167) but sym_precision falls short of good (.8539, .8343 vs .90), no single dominant cause isolated in three investigation rounds. Alongside this item, `cmd/indexbench lang-oracle` is fixed to measure a not-yet-merged port's own dialect (`ExtractForBenchWith`) instead of fusion's live incumbent dispatch — verified directly (a fresh run against the `c` dialect's own fixtures reproduced the identical wrong-extractor symptom before the fix), so every other pending port's own future reading is affected too, not only this one. `LB-Q-csharp` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-csharp-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **A doc-engine `github-actions` dialect is built and measured as LB-Q-github-actions's port of the pure-Go `ghaextract.go` walker (`GHAWorkflow`/`GHAJob`/`GHAAction` kept byte-identical; new: `action.yml`/`action.yaml` composite/reusable action definitions — `GHAActionDef`, inputs as `GHAActionInput`, outputs as `GHAActionOutput` — and steps with an explicit `id:` as `GHAStep`), but fusion's `.github/workflows/*.yml`/`action.yml` dispatch is unchanged: §11 step 2's merge gate requires the dialect to also meet its target level (L3), which it does not.** Measured against an independent `github.com/rhysd/actionlint` v1.7.12 AST-walk oracle (tier C) on actions/starter-workflows and cli/cli (24 workflow files, 112 definitions, 6 `needs:` references): `level_met` is `L2` (`good`; sym_recall/sym_precision/ref_recall/ref_precision all 1.0). L3 stays `not-attempted` for two independent reasons, neither query-fixable: cross-file (xref) scoring is open for every doc-engine dialect (LB-F30, the same cause `LB-Q-package-json-rest` found), and — new, GHA-specific — this format's `uses: ./path` convention is repository-root-relative while every existing `cross_file` strategy (`repo-path`, `module-source`) resolves a `./` value relative to the *referring file's own directory*, a `needs-strategy` case (design §13.2 step 13) verified directly on both subjects' own local `uses:` values. Alongside this item, `internal/langbench.Manifest.Owns` (the bench harness's subject file-listing) is fixed to also match `Match.Globs`, not only basenames and extensions — it had silently measured zero files for any glob-only dialect, a gap invisible until this item because no earlier dialect matched by glob alone. `LB-Q-github-actions` stays unchecked; `LB-Q-github-actions-rest` records the deferred disposition and its trigger (`docs/plans/language-breadth.md`).
- **The GC soft limit defaults to the budget's exit mark instead of a fixed 3 GiB, and a managed daemon runs at `GOMAXPROCS` max(2, NumCPU/2) unless the env sets it (RT-36).** `[daemon].memory_limit` / `FATHOM_MEMORY_LIMIT` still set it, named at startup as superseded by `[tenancy].gc_hint`; the startup line now reads `serve-http: memory gc hint … ; GOMAXPROCS …`.

- Agent-surface tool descriptions now carry only what a caller needs to call correctly — one opening sentence, at most two sentences on the result shape (budget/elision, tiers, checked:false), the "Reach for it when …" routing sentence, and "Required: …"; plan-row ids, ADR cross-references, and migration notes moved to `docs/mcp-tools.md` (nothing lost, address only), and the five tools whose result can be definitive (`find_symbol`, `find_callers`, `symbol_context`, `verify_plan`, `obligations`) keep a short "this result IS the answer" stop sentence (SB-4). Argument schemas are unchanged, pinned by a pre-diet golden. Measured `tools/list` schema bytes: 60,918 → 47,480 (still 25 tools); `agentSurfaceSchemaByteCeiling` re-pinned to 49,480.
- `find_callers`/`blast_radius` return only edges the Go typed pass resolved by default (heuristic edges behind `include_heuristic: true`); `references`' definition half gets the same treatment; every row carries `edge_confidence` and each response names `edge_filter`.
- `blast_radius` defaults to a depth cap of 2 and a 500-symbol listing cap (both overridable, reported alongside `total_symbols`/`truncated`/`depth_capped`); `diff_impact` stays the unbounded both-kinds walk for PR review.
- `references` defaults to `exact: true` where the service has typed edges (text mentions behind `include_heuristic`), renders one occurrence under 100 bytes (default cap 25), and refuses a query that is not an identifier, a dotted path, or a declared regex.
- `read_source`'s byte budget (`max_bytes`, default 8000) bounds the `lines` array as sent — each row's `{line, text}` envelope and JSON escaping count, as every other budgeted tool counts its rows — so a call returns fewer lines at the same budget, and an over-budget line is cut on a rune boundary (BP-42; an 8000-byte read had reached the model as 11,189 bytes). Its description again says when not to reach for it: a `find_symbol` hit already carries file and line.
- `find_callers` renders one caller under 100 bytes by default (`with_signatures: true` restores them) and now unions every candidate a bare name shared by two packages could mean, instead of silently falling back to the syntax answer.
- `find_callers`, `outline`, `symbol_context`, `staleness_report`, `repo_map` are now budgeted: a truncated answer keeps whole rows plus a one-line `elision`; `find_callers`/`references` add a `scope_required` escape hatch for a fan-in too large to page through at all.
- `obligations` orders served rules fresh-before-stale, then by layer role, then specificity; `#Layer` gains `role: "governs" | "claims"` (default `governs`) so external packs fill the serving cap first.
- A claims-layer rule is now scoped at serve time to its own row's `{package, symbol}` contract instead of binding by prose keyword; the tool inlines each rule's cited lines as `snippet` (`include_snippets`) and, called with no `file`, answers "what governs this repository" from the root scope.
- `references` on a query naming a method exactly one indexed interface declares now carries `implementations` first, one row per implementing type.
- `fathom init --hooks-scope project` (or `FATHOM_HOOKS_SCOPE=project`) forces every fathom hook to project scope regardless of Claude Code user-scope settings, so a clean-room or CI harness that loads only project settings still gets them wired.
- `fathom init --harness=codex` no longer writes `.codex/hooks.json` — codex-cli 0.153.1 never loads a project hook file — and prints the entries instead.
- `core.SymbolNode.EndLine` is now set by the Go extractor and most tree-sitter/AST-backed extractors, not only Markdown; `outline`/`query_symbols`/`symbol_context` render `end_line` for these symbols.
- Every Claude Code MCP entry fathom writes now sets `alwaysLoad: true`, avoiding a wasted `ToolSearch` turn per session on a headless run.
- The CGO Java extractor now also emits field and import declarations; `ExtractorEpoch` bumps 6→7, forcing re-extraction of every already-indexed `.java` file on next ingest.
- `fathom review --format github`'s "Changed symbols" caller listing now walks to depth 2 (direct callers plus callers-of-callers), labeled `[tier, depth N]`, instead of stopping at direct callers only; call-site completeness counts stay direct-callers-only. Moved the M15/VB-24 historical-PR recall bench from 5/42 (11.9%) to 6/42 (14.3%) with 0 regressions and 0 false findings — still well short of the row's 30% floor (`docs/bench/readings/review-recall-loop-2026-09-18.md`).
- C# file-resolved edges follow the ordinary `using X;` then `Y.Z()` idiom: an unaliased `using` is a wildcard import found through the files that declare the namespace, and a dotted call through a wildcard-imported type resolves to its member (for Java's `import a.*;` too) (owner decision D7, ADR-048 amendment).
- TypeScript extraction now emits abstract classes (qualifying their members), interface and abstract method signatures, and function overload / `declare function` signatures, all reported by scip-typescript and universal-ctags and missed before; `ExtractorEpoch` 17 re-extracts every indexed `.ts`/`.tsx` file (owner decision D2's re-measurement).
- SQL extraction no longer invents a column named `ATTACH` for PostgreSQL's `ALTER TABLE … ATTACH PARTITION` (only a CREATE TABLE column list or an explicit `ADD [COLUMN]` defines a column); SQL's calibration subjects are restricted to the families its grammar supports (sqitch's SQLite/plain files, pagila's PostgreSQL schema), with a tier-C sqlglot foreign-key call oracle (owner decisions D2, D5).
- Language readings re-measured with round-2 instruments (owner decisions D1–D7): TypeScript L1 good and JavaScript below L1 against scip-typescript (after the D3 audit tightened oracle-miss admissions to module-scope definitions), C# L1 excellent against a Roslyn dump, Java L2 good against a JavaParser dump, C L1 good (throughput judged on CPU time), SQL and Bash L2 good (tier-C call oracles), GitHub Actions and Kubernetes L3 good (cross-file references scored); `LB-Q-bash`, `LB-Q-sql`, `LB-Q-github-actions` and `LB-Q-kubernetes` are done.
- **`fathom harness capabilities` says what a Codex rollout is for (PE-14).** The Codex `session-ledger` note no longer calls the rollout format unstable: its token totals and tool calls are for cost per proven row, never a savings claim, and the note names the reader as a library no verb or hook calls yet; the row stays `absent`.

### Fixed
- **TypeScript, JavaScript and C# imports resolve where the language resolves them (O145).** A TypeScript ESM spec `./x.js` (`.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`) resolves to its source `x.ts`, `x.tsx` or `x.d.ts` (`.mts`, `.cts`) as the compiler's node16 and bundler resolution maps it; `require('..')`, `require('.')` and a spec ending in `/` name a directory (the repository root among them) resolved through its package.json `main` (for a TypeScript importer `typings`, `types`, then `main`) before its index file, as Node does; and a C# `using A = N.T;` or `using static N.T;` resolves to the files declaring type `N.T`, wherever they sit, so `A.M()` and, under `using static`, a bare `M()` that no type enclosing the caller declares draw file-resolved edges to T's member. `import_resolution` on the O145 subjects: zod 0 to 1 (504 imports), express .3419 to 1 (155), Polly 0 to 1 (15), Newtonsoft.Json 0 to .0415 (313; O148). `ExtractorEpoch` 31 re-reads every file.
- **`fathom map append` of an anchor the map already carries files no second entry (XM-33, O149).** The same why files nothing and says so; another why, or the same symbol under another spelling, is a note on the first entry with its own agent id, served by `map show` as another annotation.
- **`fathom map append` (and `thread note --map`) resolves a symbol anchor the way `find_symbol` does, and names an index it could not reach instead of blaming the anchor (XM-31, O147).** `ResolveImportSpecs`, `fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` and `internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` reach one symbol stored under one `dir.Name` key; an ambiguous name lists its candidates and files nothing; `--service` (also on `map show` and `map compose`) names the service, and a daemon that does not answer is waited for up to 8 s.
- **`fathom map name` makes a map durable whatever condition it was filed under (XM-32, O143).** A map named at the end of a session, or after its session ended, stays in `map list`, the session brief, the pre-compact resume and the location pointer instead of ending with the session that filed it; an unnamed map still ends.
- **`fathom doctor`'s `stanza_loaded` no longer says Claude Code loads `AGENTS.md` where `stanza_present` warns it will not.** In a repository with no root CLAUDE file, its SKIP now repeats that warning's reasons (an ancestor CLAUDE file, the `claude-md` setting, a release before 2.1.277) and remedies; with no warning it is the same named agents-md SKIP.
- The Claude Code harness descriptor no longer claims `.agents/skills` discovery: Claude Code reads skills from `.claude/skills` only (ADR-059).
- **The prompt hook drops a path or package token that names nothing in the working tree, and serves nothing for a message another agent relayed (EW-21, O126).** `bin/fathom` (a file) is no longer the package `bin/fathom/__prompt__.go`, a scratch, `~/` or `../` path reaches no daemon call and takes no candidate slot, and a prompt opening a line with `<cross-session-message`, `<teammate-message` or `[Workflow harness` is silent like a system notification.
- **The Bash-write hook no longer reads quoted text as a write, and serves one line, not the nearest guide's rules, when it cannot name what a command wrote (EW-1, O122).** A `>` or a writer's name inside a quoted string or a heredoc body is text, a write whose every target resolves outside the indexed roots (`~/.claude/…`, `/tmp/…`, `../x`) is silent, and an unnameable write gets one line once per session and directory; every post-bash emission is a `post-bash` payload-cost beat, and `fathom-bash-doubt` markers in `$TMPDIR` are pruned by `gc` after a day.
- The PostToolUse Bash hook no longer panics (exit 2, a Go trace shown to the agent) on a command that ends in an unmatched `(`, and an unmatched subshell keeps its last byte, so `(cat > a.go` names `a.go`, not `a.g`.
- The build-cache guard (`FATHOM_CACHE_MAX_MIB`) trims the Go build cache by entry age instead of running `go clean -cache`, and never removes an entry used within the last day. A build that starts during a trim no longer reads a cache being deleted (O124, EW-4).
- **The `wired` check stops counting four over-approximations the 2026-09-29 re-audit corpus exposed (EW-15).** A bare reference assigned to the blank identifier (`var _ = f`, `_ = f`) is no edge; a `main` in a file no default build compiles (`//go:build ignore`, a custom tag) is not an entry point; a receiver-variable call the syntax pass matched to the one first-party method of that name is not followed from a file the typed pass resolved; and an interface call reaches an implementation only once reached code instantiates its type. The typed pass now also resolves files that import "C" under their source name. The next ingest re-extracts every Go file (extractor epoch 18, typed-edge epoch 4).
- **The first `verify_plan` after an ingest or a daemon start answers within its deadline (EW-22, O130).** It waits for the call graph no longer than its deadline less a reserve, and when the graph is still building it answers with every row elided and a `partial` naming the build instead of "abandoned: the call's deadline passed"; the build keeps running and the next call (the CLI gate's own follow-up) is served from it. verify_plan now builds only the graph it reads, not the nav tools' syntax-only and resolved-only graphs.
- **The tree-sitter engine's parse and query budget is elapsed time, as documented.** The guest read wazero's default fake clock (+1ms per reading), so the 10s budget meant ~1,000,000 query operations and never fired on a pathological file (the whole `({[`x50,000 scan is ~300,000) — only the 20s hard backstop bounded it; a 300ms budget now cancels that scan at ~0.3s instead of ~8s of CPU.
- An ingest in which every file failed to read, over a service with no index to protect, is no longer recorded complete at HEAD with zero fingerprints (which auto-refresh then never retried): it writes nothing, records no watermark, stays `in_progress` for a retry and reports outcome `incomplete` (`--json`), naming the failure.
- An ingest onboarding a new service no longer fails for every file (`stat <service>/<path>: no such file or directory`) when a read names that service while it runs: a read's Discover of the not-yet-recorded root no longer replaces the ingest's root.
- `obligations` no longer reads the whole tree on every call: layer coverage and the raw fallback read only the layer's guides (paths from one names-only walk per ingest, each guide under a 2 MiB cap) and the edited file, the pack union compiles once per pack digest, and a plan row bound by symbol alone resolves through that symbol's rows instead of every observation in the service; per-call allocation on a synthetic corpus (one governing claim and two plan-row bindings) is about 155 KB at both 60 and 600 files, where the whole-tree reads cost 3.1 MB and 8.9 MB. Until the planned symbol index lands, SQLite still walks the service's rows to find a symbol-only row's names, so that lookup's CPU (not its memory) still grows with the service. A new guide is counted in `layer_coverage` after the next ingest, and `unread_corpus` names a guide too large or unable to be read, also when coverage is `unknown`, as does the raw-fallback rows' `why` (RT-81, RT-143, RT-83).
- `obligations` stats the edited file before its index-freshness check and never reads one over 2 MiB; the response names the skip as `index_unchecked` instead of reporting the file fresh or stale, and both edit hooks print `index freshness not checked for this file (…)` for it; `fathom doctor` warns when layer coverage is a lower bound because a guide could not be read (RT-26, RT-143).
- The daemon answers its own service registry in process: Observatory pages no longer round-trip `list_services` over loopback HTTP, which timed out under load and rendered project pages as "store unreadable", and no longer write a call-log row for a page render.
- `fathom plugin render` writes `"license": "FSL-1.1-ALv2"` into the Agent Plugins `plugin.json`, the licence `LICENSE.md` grants (ADR-052); it wrote `MIT`, which fathom is not released under.
